# SURVEY rev 49 — WHAT REMAINS BEFORE PHOTOREALISM
**A 19-agent coordinated survey, run at the owner's request at the close of rev 49:** twelve subsystem surveys, five adversarial refuters, a completeness critic and one ranked synthesis. Subagent concurrency on this box is 2, so it ran in ten rounds over roughly five hours.
**12 of 12 subsystem surveys returned. 78 findings before deduplication.**
Every agent was instructed to REFUTE ITS OWN FINDINGS before reporting, to state the ceiling of every number, and to record what it checked and found ALREADY CORRECT so the next revision does not re-litigate it.

---

## THE FINDINGS, ORDERED BY SEVERITY THEN CONFIDENCE

### 1. [BLOCKING] THE ROCKER IS NOT MODELLED AT ALL — the flank ends in a flat cut at ZB with white cyclorama immediately below, where both vehicles show a bright neutral trim strip and a rocker that turns under
*The flank, belt line and louvres — side elevation of the RED target bus (ref_side.jpg / ref_rear34.jpg), with geometry-only corroboration from the GREEN bus (IM*  
**confidence** high · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** In ref_side.jpg the red flank terminates in a bright, near-neutral metal strip with its own highlight, its own shadow line under it and a fitting mounted on it, and below that strip sits 15-25 px of dark rocker/underbody. The model has neither. t1_core.py:388 `ZB` is the shell's bottom edge and the loft closes there; the string "rocker" appears in t1_core.py and t1_shell.py ONLY inside comments — there is no rocker object anywhere in t1_detail.py, t1_shell.py or build.py. In the render the red paint runs down to a clean straight silhouette and then the white ground begins. Worse: the project has already LOCATED this feature and depends on it — t1_core.py:106-108 fits the rocker trim ridge sub-pixel at -0.025415 +- 0.000178 px/px (rms 0.299 px, n = 324) and t1_core.py:117-120 uses "rocker above hub" at both axles as METHOD 4 of the RAKE solve. RAKE_DZDX, on which every vertical number in the build depends, is measured off a feature the build does not contain.

**Evidence.** ref_side.jpg, detector = row of maximum luminance with G/R > 0.45 inside the band z 0.28..0.46 m above ground, 65 columns at 5 px spacing over X +0.82..-0.66 (clear of the leaning man and both arches), probe_scratch/rev50_rocker2.py: strip at z = 0.3633 +- 0.0281 m AG (bulk 0.3437-0.3620; the sd is inflated by a few outliers to 0.4617); strip luminance 121.7 against the red paint 6 px above at 46.7 = 2.61x; strip G/R 0.797 against the same columns' body red at 0.08-0.18. Raw pixels at col 470: rows 594-600 R125..93/G11..15/B3..10 (paint), row 602 R168/G143/B113 (strip), rows 604-613 lum 23-37 neutral (rocker/underbody). MODEL at the same stations, ZB minus rake_drop: 0.3242 (X+0.80), 0.3300 (+0.40), 0.3371 (0.00), 0.3442 (-0.40), 0.3491 (-0.66) — the silhouette bottom and nothing below it. Render side view, same Laplacian ridge detector, probe_scratch/rev50_rocker.py: the only peak in the band has peak luminance 213-236, i.e. it IS the white cyclorama on the far side of the silhouette, and it tracks ZB exactly (0.3216 at X+0.9 -> 0.3547 at X-0.7). Crops: probe_scratch/rev50_flank-belt_ref_rockerstrip_marked_x4.png (strip, its shadow and a fitting, unmistakable), probe_scratch/rev50_flank-belt_r49_rockerstrip_absent_x4.png (render, nothing), probe_scratch/rev50_flank-belt_workshop_sill_x4.png (GREEN bus, ref_workshop.jpg cols 560-900: the flank visibly turns under into a shadowed rocker between the wheels — geometry, so it transfers), probe_scratch/rev50_flank-belt_r49s_hero34f_full.png (the newly-landed 3/4 hero shows the same flat cut from a second viewpoint, where a rocker would self-shadow hardest).

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter-argument: the bright line is a specular highlight on the red paint's lower curvature, not a trim strip. It fails — a specular on red paint under a neutral sun retains a red bias, and this reads G/R 0.797 six pixels below paint reading G/R 0.08-0.18, i.e. it is essentially neutral; and it is a 1-2 px line at a constant height across 65 sampled columns, whereas a specular on a convex rocker would sit near the top of the curvature, not at the panel's foot. Second counter-argument: the dark band below it is the vehicle's ground shadow, not structure. That half I hold at lower confidence and do not quote a depth for — but it does not touch the strip itself, which is BRIGHTER than the paint above it (a shadow boundary is darker on the shadow side and equal on the lit side). Third counter-argument (rule 26): a trim strip could be hardware fitted to the RED bus only, hence not transferable. ref_workshop.jpg refutes the geometric half — the GREEN bodyshell's flank plainly turns under into a rocker between the wheels — so even if the polished strip is red-bus-only, the rocker return below ZB is geometry and is missing on both counts. Fourth: I first tried to make this a numeric height defect by comparing the photographed ridge (hub-0.4 mm / hub+42.2 mm, t1_core's own METHOD 4) with the model's ZB (hub-14.1 / hub+25.0) and calling the model 15 mm low. I DROPPED that — ZB is the sill's bottom edge and the ridge is a trim strip above it; they are different features and the subtraction is meaningless. The finding is absence, not height.

### 2. [BLOCKING] W4 HAS A PHOTOGRAPHED HANDLE AT LAST: the headlamp reads as a CIRCLE in every photograph and as the badge's own ELLIPSE in the render
*The nose and front: nose face and plan crown (W4), V swage, VW roundel and bars, headlamps and bowls, indicators, front blade/bumper, windscreen aperture and ru*  
**confidence** high · **effort** large · **blocked on** NOTHING to state the defect. To SEPARATE the three causes: one frame taken from beside the front corner at nose height, looking ACROSS the front face at grazing incidence, so the plan section appears directly as a silhouette against the background -- or a frame looking straight down onto the badge. A head-on frame does NOT do it: head-on, a 15 deg panel splay only compresses the bezel by 3.4%, which is 1 px on any nose we hold.

**What is wrong.** Two circular features live on the nose 0.545 m apart in Y: the VW roundel (y=0) and the headlamp aperture/bezel (y=+0.545). On a PLANAR nose their image ellipses must have the same aspect ratio, because a plane has one normal and the only thing that can differ between them is the change in ray direction across ~110 px of image. In three independent photographs the lamp reads 1.36-2.05x rounder than the roundel in the SAME frame. In the shipped render the two read the SAME. The model's nose is a plane (14.3 mm of recession over 0.70 m of half-width) and both lamps are placed with no rotation at all, so the render cannot produce the photographed signature at any pose. Whatever the cause -- panel plan-crown (W4 proper), outboard splay of the lamp axis, or the bezel standing proud enough to silhouette on its own girth -- ALL THREE live in this dimension and ALL THREE are absent from the build.

**Evidence.** Aspect = image width / image height of the outer outline, read off bounding boxes on magnified crops and confirmed by eye. ref_workshop.jpg (GREEN, GEOMETRY ONLY, lamp REMOVED so the outline is the bare aperture): roundel 60x91 px = 0.659 +- 0.03 (probe_scratch/rev50_nose-front_wk_roundel8x.png); near headlamp aperture 74x70 px = 1.06, second-moment fit 0.908, G/R-threshold sweep 1.04..1.27 -> take >=0.91 (probe_scratch/rev50_nose-front_wk_nearbore8x.png, probe_scratch/rev50_nose_ellipse.py). ref_nolita_front34.jpg (RED, NOLITA artwork -- these are GEOMETRY quantities): roundel 38x54 = 0.704, bezel 50x52 = 0.962 (probe_scratch/rev50_nose-front_nol_ro2_grid.png, _nol_hl2_grid.png). ref_nolita_front34b.jpg (RED, NOLITA): roundel 25.5x51 = 0.500, bezel 42x41 = 1.024 (probe_scratch/rev50_nose-front_b_ro2_grid.png, _b_hl2_grid.png). RENDER out/r49s_hero34f.png: roundel 61x69 = 0.884, near bezel 45x50 = 0.900, far bezel 40x49 = 0.816 (probe_scratch/rev50_nose-front_rend_rndl2_grid.png, _rend_hlnear_grid.png, _rend_hlfar2_grid.png). RENDER out/r49s_front.png head-on: roundel 115x118 = 0.975, bezel 88x84 = 1.048. THE WINDOW: for a plane, |psi_lamp - psi_roundel| is bounded by the angular separation of the two rays -- <=7 deg on ref_workshop for f>=1200 px, <=12 deg even at f=800; 3.8 deg in the render at f~1600 px. Render measures 2.0 deg (INSIDE its window). Photographs measure 24-48 deg (3.5-7x outside). Source: only forward-bulge constant is t1_shell.py:2245 `bulge = 0.019 * w * max(0.0, 1.0 - r)` with `r = (y/0.80)**2 + ((z-1.00)/0.46)**2`; at the lamp station that is bulge(0)-bulge(0.545) = 0.019*0.464 = 8.8 mm of crown, i.e. a panel normal 1.2 deg off +X.

**Its own refutation.** STRONGEST REFUTATION: the T1 bezel plus its domed lens is a convex proud assembly whose SILHOUETTE is circular from any direction, so the photographed ~1.0 measures proudness, not panel orientation. That refutation KILLS the two red frames and I accept it for them. It does NOT kill ref_workshop.jpg, where the lamp is removed and the outline is a bare hole -- and that frame still reads >=0.91 against a same-frame roundel of 0.659. SECOND REFUTATION, which I ran and which does bite: inverting the workshop pair into a plan crown gives a normal swing of 34-39 deg at the lamp station, a plan radius of ~1.1-1.2 m and a 160 mm sagitta over the half-width. That is a Beetle nose, not a Bus nose, so the inversion IS contaminated -- most likely by a raised rolled flange round the real aperture, whose outer edge silhouettes as a convex torus. I therefore report the OBSERVABLE and refuse to convert it into a value for `bulge`. THIRD: I checked the estimator is not the problem -- the same pixel reads reproduce build.py's own published chain on this frame (D_roundel/D_aperture 1.35 against its 1.384) and its roundel-above-lamp figure, so the instrument is calibrated against the project's own.

### 3. [BLOCKING] The engine-lid outline sits ~65 mm too HIGH on the tail — and the T-handle falls 48 mm OUTSIDE it, on the fixed body below the shut line
*THE TAIL AND REAR — tail panel, engine lid + shut line, T-handle, 1963 plate, tail lamps, rear window aperture and what shows through it, rear bumper, tail boar*  
**confidence** high · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING. Both readings come off ref_rear34.jpg, which the project already holds and which rev 49 established is 4:4:4, quality ~99 and only 3.77% clipped frame-wide. The T-handle half needs no photograph at all — it is two constants in the repository.

**What is wrong.** ENGLID_GAP's z centre (t1_shell.py:856, `v + 0.8700`) is an uncited literal. Its HEIGHT is right (0.5055 m built vs 0.512 m photographed, +1.3%) but the whole outline is ~65 mm too high relative to the number plate, which is the one piece of tail hardware whose size and placement carry a stated measurement. The consequence is visible without any measurement at all: englid_handle sits at z = PLATE_OUTER_CZ - 1.274*PLATE_OUTER_H = 0.5732, while the lid's lower cut edge is at 0.61725 — the T-handle is 46.8 mm BELOW the lid's own shut line, mounted on the fixed body panel, not on the lid its own docstring says it is on (t1_detail.py:2718 'engine lid T-handle, BELOW the number plate on the lid'). In ref_rear34.jpg the handle is plainly INSIDE the lid, ~40 mm above its lower seam. Moving the lid down ~100 mm satisfies the plate offsets AND puts the handle back inside the lid; nothing else in the tail has to move.

**Evidence.** FRAME: ref_rear34.jpg (RED, target vehicle, current artwork). QUANTITY: geometry, so the artwork state is not in play. RULER: the plate frame's own fitted outer edge lines from t1_detail.py:2543-2546, evaluated at the column band x 1000-1070 — top y=640.16, bottom y=698.4, PLATE_OUTER_H = 58.3 px. All terms are VERTICAL extents on one plane rotated about a vertical axis, in the same column band, so no px/m and no foreshortening correction enters (the method t1_detail.py:2702 itself endorses). MEASURED (70-column luma means, probe_scratch/rev50_tail-rear_REF_lid_grid.png, _lidzone5x.png, _lidbot6x.png): upper shut line y=575.0 (dip 65->48 DN), lower shut line y=752.5 (dip 47->42, lit lip at 757). SELF-VALIDATION OF THE RULER: lid height 177.5 px = 3.045 PLATE_OUTER_H against the built 0.5055/0.16823 = 3.005 — +1.3%, so the scale is good over the span I use it on. THE TWO DELTAS: plate_top->lid_top photo 65.16/58.3 = 1.118 vs built (1.12275-0.87166)/0.16823 = 1.4926 -> Δ 0.375 x 168.23 = 63 mm; plate_bot->lid_bot photo 54.1/58.3 = 0.928 vs built (0.70343-0.61725)/0.16823 = 0.5123 -> Δ 0.416 x 168.23 = 70 mm. Same sign, agree to 7 mm. CONTROL that the plate/handle pair is already right: plate_bot->handle photo 41.6/59.6 = 0.698 (at x=1125) vs built 0.7740 -> Δ 13 mm. RENDER CONFIRMATION (out/r49s_rear.png, probe_scratch/rev50_tail-rear_REN_lid_grid.png, 457.7 px/m from the plate's measured 77.0 px): lid top seam y=736.6, lid bottom seam y=962.9, plate outer 848.5-925.5, handle y=985 — the handle is 22.1 px = 48 mm below the lower shut line, matching the constants' 46.8 mm. Source: t1_shell.py:855-857 (ENGLID_CUT_DX / ENGLID_GAP), t1_detail.py:2714 (ENGLID_HANDLE_DROP), t1_detail.py:2728-2729.

**Its own refutation.** (1) 'y=575 is a shadow or a swage, not the shut line.' If it were, the lid height computed 575->752.5 would not land within 1.3% of the built 0.5055 m by coincidence. And the finding survives the other reading: if the LOWER line (595.5) is the shut line instead, plate_top->lid_top becomes 0.766 against 1.4926, i.e. 122 mm — LARGER, not smaller. Either assignment gives >=63 mm. (2) 'PLATE_OUTER_H is itself a rev-15 derivation (aspect 1.9616 +- 0.034), so the ruler could be wrong.' It could be up to ~20% wrong per its own Monte-Carlo band, which would scale 63/70 mm down to 50/56 mm — the defect does not vanish, and the T-handle-outside-the-lid consequence is scale-free. (3) 'Perspective could stretch the panel.' All terms sit within 0.19 m of the ruler and the ruler self-validates on the lid's own height over the full 0.51 m span; a perspective error large enough to fake this would have broken that check. (4) 'Maybe the PLATE is 65 mm too high, not the lid.' Under-determined by the plate offsets alone — but the T-handle test breaks the tie: the handle is correct relative to the plate (13 mm) and wrong relative to the lid (48 mm, sign-flipped), so the lid is the member to move. (5) 'The gap_englid boolean is the model's most delicate (t1_core.py:230-244, rejected at NHALF=56/SUB=2).' True, and it is why this is medium effort not small — the cutter's z must move and the boolean re-checked at both subdivision levels.

### 4. [BLOCKING] The rear serving aperture renders as a black cavity — 4.3x darker than the photograph and 4.5x darker than the model's own side serving bays in the same frame
*THE TAIL AND REAR — tail panel, engine lid + shut line, T-handle, 1963 plate, tail lamps, rear window aperture and what shows through it, rear bumper, tail boar*  
**confidence** high · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING for the diagnosis. What is behind the aperture 573 mm forward of the tail skin is only partly resolvable in ref_rear34.jpg (chrome, a bottle, pale slab shapes) — but the FIX does not require inventing contents: extending the galley's aft wall/backdrop to the tail station, as the side bays already have, is enough to stop it reading as a void.

**What is wrong.** The owner's rev-49 ruling made the rear window the vehicle's ONLY open service aperture. It does not read as one. The galley dressing's aft wall `gal_end_a` is built at x = -1.300 (t1_detail.py:3100), leaving 573 mm of undressed, unlit cavity between it and the tail skin at X_TAIL = -1.873, and nothing lights that cavity — the whole galley-light argument at t1_detail.py:3103-3121 is about daylight falling through the ROOF opening onto the three SIDE bays, which are all forward of x = -0.960. The rear aperture therefore looks into a dead box. The model already knows what a serving aperture must read as: t1_detail.py:2812 records the photographed side bays as 'NOTHING inside any aperture is darker than display luma 73, and the 2nd percentiles are 91/106/127. The galley is a bright room.' The rear aperture's 2nd percentile is 2.6.

**Evidence.** DIMENSIONLESS, using the vehicle's own flank cream near the tail as the in-frame reference — and that reference matches between frames to 1% (render 187.7, photo 189.4), so the comparison is admissible. RENDER out/r49shut2_hero34r.png (CURRENT shipped state): rear aperture interior (x 1030-1190, y 595-648) median L 26.2, min 0.0, 2nd pct 2.6 -> ratio to cream 0.140. PHOTO ref_rear34.jpg, same station (x 990-1195, y 300-430, the opening whose show-side jamb I locate below): median L 114.7, 2nd pct 31.0 -> ratio 0.606. That is 4.3x. INTERNAL CONTROL, needing no photograph at all: in the SAME render the model's own side serving bays read median 188.5 and 124.4 (x 640-700 and 720-780, y 510-575) -> ratios 1.00 and 0.66, i.e. on target. So the model lights three of its four serving apertures correctly and leaves the fourth a hole. Straight-rear frame out/r49s_rear.png gives the same story: rear aperture median 41.1, min 0.2. Crops: probe_scratch/rev50_tail-rear_REN_aperture4x.png, _REN_sidebays.png, _REF_uppertail4x.png. THAT THE APERTURE IS ON THE TAIL AND ITS WIDTH IS RIGHT: its show-side jamb (a gold trim strip, probe_scratch/rev50_tail-rear_REF_corner6x.png) is at x_img 978; the plate's raw horizontal scale at the tail is 97.9 px / 0.330 m = 296.7 px/m about the plate centreline x_img 1129.7, giving y_veh = +0.512 m against REAR_W/2 = 0.520 (t1_shell.py:230) — 8 mm. Corroborated independently: the built tail lamp at y = 0.620 (build.py:563) predicts x_img 946 and the lamp's measured centre is 944.5.

**Its own refutation.** (1) 'The photo window might not be the rear aperture — the source itself (t1_shell.py:1395-1396) calls the opening below the rear glazing one on the FLANK.' That reading is wrong and I checked it: bay 3, the rear-most side bay, ends at x_veh = -0.960 (t1_detail.py:1164), which along the flank projects to x_img ~753, and the three flank bays are plainly visible in ref_rear34.jpg at x_img 570-750. The opening at x_img 978-1200 is 0.51 m inboard of the tail's show-side edge, on the tail. (2) 'The comparison is between two lighting environments (white studio vs a shaded street).' That is why the primary statement is the INTERNAL one — the model's own side bays vs its own rear aperture in one render, one light, 4.5x apart. No photograph is needed for that half. (3) 'The interior red visible in the aperture is the owner's ruled bare-inside-of-red-paint.' Agreed and not disputed — that ruling (t1_detail.py:3104-3110) is about COLOUR, not about the space being unlit and undressed to a 2.6 second percentile. (4) 'My sample window includes the open pane and the dark frame edges.' I re-sampled a clear interior box inside the aperture only; median rose to 39 at best, still 3x short.

### 5. [BLOCKING] The mural lid is raked the wrong way: LID_OPEN_DEG = 104° tips it AWAY from the counter; all three in-service frames tip it OVER the counter
*The roof, lids and drip rail — roof skin/crown, roof aperture, raised mural lid (geometry, hinge, struts, junction), drip rail/gutter, bulb string, tail-board/r*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING for the sign and for the fix. The exact angle would tighten with a frame that fixes the camera distance for ref_side.jpg (any object of known size at a known depth), but no photograph is needed to establish that α < 90°.

**What is wrong.** t1_shell.py:1078 `LID_OPEN_DEG = 104.0  # past vertical, leaning over the counter`. With the hinge at LID_Y_HINGE = -0.545 (off side) and LID_W = 1.110, `_hinge` puts the free edge at y = -0.545 + 1.110·cos104° = -0.8135 — 87 mm OUTBOARD of the off-side roof edge (Yt = 0.7273) and 1.63 m away from the counter. The comment, and SPEC.md:135 ("swings up and over the counter"), describe the opposite of what is built. The photographs put the free edge on the SHOW side of the hinge (α < 90°), so the board leans over the serving counter like an awning. Consequence in the shipped frames: in out/r49s_counter.png — the view studio.py:1376 declares is "looking slightly up at the mural board" — the board has fallen off the top-left corner and is almost entirely out of frame; in out/r49shut2_hero34r.png it stands back over the off side. AUDIT_rev43.md:117 raised this and it has stood unverified and unfixed for six revisions.

**Evidence.** MEASURED THIS PASS, scale-free, on ref_side.jpg (RED, target vehicle, GEOMETRY): the yellow board rectangle's fore and aft edges fitted over four independent row windows. Fore edge x = 0.1511·y + 306.1 (rms 0.41–0.47 px, n = 88–146 rows), aft edge x = 0.0585·y + 739 (rms 0.60–1.69 px, n = 86–117). Span shrinks downward at 0.0216 ± 0.0026 %/row (windows y20–300: −5.73 %; y40–240: −3.95 %; y45–200: −2.75 %; y60–230: −4.05 %) → −5.3 ± 0.6 % over the board's own 244 px height. Nearer subtends more, and the camera is unambiguously on the show side (serving bays, counter, Señor Tacombi script and 100% Calidad decal all in frame), so THE TOP OF THE BOARD IS NEARER THE SHOW SIDE THAN ITS BOTTOM. Corroborated in two further frames by a cue needing no measurement at all: in ref_rear34.jpg (RED) and IMG_2073.jpeg (GREEN, geometry only) the support rod passes IN FRONT OF the board's painted face (probe_scratch/rev50_roof-lids_rear34_lid4x.png, rev50_roof-lids_2073_stayfoot3x.png) — only possible if the free edge overhangs toward the camera. Solving the taper jointly with the projected height for camera-to-board distances 6–13 m gives α = 61–78° from horizontal, i.e. the board leans 12–29° TOWARD the counter, against 104° built: a 26–43° error and a 0.6–0.8 m error in where the free edge sits in Y. Renders read at out/r49s_counter.png and out/r49board_side.png; crops rev50_roof-lids_REN_counter_board_missing2x.png, rev50_roof-lids_refside_lid4x.png.

**Its own refutation.** The strongest way I could be wrong is that the taper is an artefact rather than parallax. Three candidates, all checked and all pushing the OTHER way: (a) barrel distortion — the board's top edge lies nearer the frame's top edge, where barrel COMPRESSES, so it would shorten the top and hide the effect; (b) an upward camera pitch — keystone makes the upper part SMALLER, again hiding it; (c) threshold bleed — the bright wall behind the top raises B and trims the yellow gate inward at the top, shortening it. Every systematic I can name reduces the measured taper, so the true lean is at least what I measured. Second candidate: that the board's lower edge in the image is the free edge and the top the hinge — impossible for a fore-aft-hinged roof lid. Third: contamination by the vendor standing in the opening; he occupies x 355–430 and rows 258–320, so I refitted excluding rows above 240 and below 40 and the sign and rate held (−0.0208 %/row). I also DROPPED a related claim that did not survive: I read a pale band under the board's forward end at ref_side.jpg x 396–428, y 279–308 as daylight through a gap and derived a raised hinge at z ≈ 2.17 m from it; a 12× crop (rev50_roof-lids_REF_gap12x.png) shows it is the vendor's white shirt and cap. That derivation is withdrawn in full.

### 6. [BLOCKING] The tail board's foot is buried 63–75 mm inside the roof crown, and the rev-49 guard written to catch exactly this cannot fire
*The roof, lids and drip rail — roof skin/crown, roof aperture, raised mural lid (geometry, hinge, struts, junction), drip rail/gutter, bulb string, tail-board/r*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING. The 80 mm footing question the source declares (t1_shell.py:1600-1613) is a separate, photograph-blocked matter about whether the foot is bracketed, stepped or aft of the roof corner; this is simply the wrong z datum and a guard that compares a value with itself.

**What is wrong.** t1_shell.py:1621 sets `z0 = T.ZT_ALL(x0) - T.rake_drop(x0) + 0.0050`, and t1_shell.py:1633 sets `_crown = T.ZT_ALL(x0) - T.rake_drop(x0)`, then raises if `z0 < _crown`. z0 − _crown is IDENTICALLY +0.005 by construction, so the guard is a tautology: in the shipped path it can never fail, and it measures nothing about the roof. `ZT_ALL` is the top of the FLANK profile, not the roof surface — t1_core.build_kombi passes `CR_ALL(x)` into `ring()` as the crown, so the roof at y = 0 stands CR_ALL above ZT_ALL. At the board's own station the board therefore stands 74.6 mm below the metal it is supposed to stand on, across its whole 0.550 m width. This is LEDGER_rev49's own Rule 30 ("a fixture's foot must be clear of the body it stands on, and something must check it") failing one revision after it was written, on the object it was written for.

**Evidence.** Arithmetic reproduced by hand from the tables (no Blender): ZT_ALL at authored x −1.880 = 1.88513 (interp of (−1.892,1.884),(−1.820,1.8908)); CR_ALL = 0.07958 (interp of (−1.900,0.0700),(−1.800,0.1179)); RT_ALL = 0.07998; WX = 0.8730; Yt = WX·G(zt−rt) − rt = 0.7394. Roof surface at y = 0 is 1.96471; the board's foot is at 1.89013 → BURIED 74.6 mm. At the board's outer edge y = ±0.275 (TB_WIDTH 0.550 / TB_Y_CENTRE 0.000, t1_shell.py:1565,1567) the roof is 1.95370 → buried 63.6 mm. The rake cancels because both terms carry the same `−rake_drop(x0)`. SEEN, not only computed: probe_scratch/rev50_roof-lids_REN_tailboardfoot5x.png (crop of out/r49shut2_hero34r.png at 1020,300–1320,520) shows the roof surface cutting across the board's lower end so it grows out of the sheet metal; probe_scratch/rev50_roof-lids_REN_side_tailfoot5x.png (out/r49board_side.png, orthographic, 1230,330–1520,520) shows the same in a projection with no camera ambiguity. `grep -n tail_board verify.py verify_clone.sh` returns nothing — no other check covers it.

**Its own refutation.** Best refutations tried: (1) that CR_ALL is not actually in the shell, so ZT_ALL really is the roof top — refuted at t1_core.py:485-487, `ring(x, WX(x), ZB(x), ZT_ALL(x), RB_ALL(x), RT_ALL(x), CR_ALL(x), bcrown=0.012)`, and t1_shell.roof_z (the function the lid hinge and every strut foot use) is `zt + cr·(1 − (y/Yt)²)`, i.e. the same crown; (2) that the burial is an aft-of-X_TAIL roll-down where CR_ALL has died away — CR_ALL is 0.0796 there, not zero, and x0 = X_TAIL + 0.1745 is forward of the tail; (3) that subdivision or solidify closes the gap — Catmull-Clark shrink and a 2.8 mm skin are millimetres, not 65–75; (4) that the crop is the camera occluding the foot with roof in front of it — the ortho side view has no such freedom and shows the same thing; (5) that this is the declared 80 mm inconsistency being re-litigated — it is not: the declared item is the 80 mm between the near-edge read and a clear foot, and the source resolves it by CHOOSING the clear foot; the defect is that the chosen value is not clear, because it was computed against ZT_ALL.

The rim disc's dished front face (t1_detail.py:118-125, scaled by S = RIM_R/0.1905 = 1.15381) sits at y = 0.0430..0.0570 out to r = 0.1846. The hubcap dome (t1_detail.py:175-183) has its apex at y = 0.0745 but its outer lip at r = 0.1370 is at y = +0.0040 — 60 mm INBOARD of the rim flange lip at y = 0.0640. Solving the two profiles against each other, the disc face becomes the front-most surface at r = 0.1197 m, so everything of the cap outside r = 119.7 mm is occluded by the rim's own disc. That is a 12.6% loss of the cap's diameter. Worse: the disc's five vent holes (t1_detail.py:151-158, centres r = 0.118, radius 0.0235, reaching r = 0.1415) are cut straight through the dish exactly where the dome stops being visible, so each appears as a dark crescent bitten out of the red dome's edge. The cap reads as a cog or a flower, not a VW moon cap. t1_detail.py:151-158's own comment justifies not scaling the vents on the ground that scaling them would put '29 mm / 6 px of dark notch in a cream annulus that the photograph shows unbroken' — the render breaks it anyway, for a different reason the comment did not consider.

MEASURED on the render (out/r49board_side.png, side ORTHO, exact 271.186 px/m, hub at the computed (1098.30, 872.03)): red→cream luminance half-crossing over 720 rays gives a red edge of 119.7 mm (rear) and 121.7 mm (front) — the analytic crossover to 0.0 mm. The same probe on the cream ring returns 219.5 mm against RIM_R 219.8, so the instrument is sound and only the cap is short. DIMENSIONLESS, red-dome edge / cream-circle outer edge, identical method both sides: PHOTOGRAPH ref_side.jpg (RED, target, rear wheel; hub (749.36, 604.26) and rim R 46.494 px from a 356-ray circle fit, rms 0.53 px) = 0.6446; BUILT INTENT (CAP_R+0.0025)/RIM_R = 0.6233; RENDERED = 0.5453 rear / 0.5545 front. Apparent cap diameter 239.4 mm rendered vs 274.0 mm built vs 283 mm implied by the photograph. Crops: probe_scratch/rev50_wheels-stance_HUBCAP_COMPARE.png, rev50_wheels-stance_rend_hubcap_x7.png, rev50_wheels-stance_rend_h34r_rearwheel_x5.png (the notches are plainly visible in the shipped hero out/r49shut2_hero34r.png).

**Its own refutation.** Strongest argument against: the dark notches could be a modelled hubcap feature, and my 'red edge' could be the dome's shading terminator rather than an occlusion. Checked three ways and it survives. (1) The notch radius brackets the measured edge exactly — the vents span r 94.5–141.5 mm and the measured red edge is 119.7 mm, i.e. the visible part of each vent is precisely the outer crescent the dome no longer covers. (2) The analytic crossover computed from the two authored profiles, with no image involved, lands at 119.7 mm — the same number the image gives to 0.0 mm; two independent routes agreeing on a value that is neither CAP_R nor CAP_R+0.0025 rules out a shading artefact. (3) If my half-crossing were biased it would bias the cream ring too, and that returns 219.5 against a known 219.8. Second refutation: is 0.6446 measured on the right vehicle? Yes — ref_side.jpg is the RED target, and a ratio of two diameters is geometry, not paint. The dome's own shading darkens toward its rim and so biases the photographic half-crossing INWARD, meaning 0.6446 understates the truth and the gap to 0.5453 is if anything larger.

Every built X in galley_dressing() and in the counter-top prop block reproduces REF_MEASUREMENTS' map X = (495.8 - u)/211.5 to 1-2 mm, and t1_shell.py:129-136 says of exactly that map: "495.8 px IS the hub midpoint, and this model's axles are at +1.300 / -1.100, so its mid-wheelbase is x = +0.100. Every REF model-frame number is 100 mm aft of where it says." rev 13 moved the three apertures 105 mm FORWARD onto BAY_CX = (0.6720, 0.0470, -0.5980); the dressing behind them did not move. So the whole fit-out now sits ~106 mm aft of the aperture it was measured through, i.e. shifted by +0.19 of an aperture width. This is not an inference from the source: it is measured in the SHIPPED RENDER.

**Evidence.** SOURCE, 12 features, three bays, all agreeing to 1-2 mm with the retired map and disagreeing with the corrected stations by +0.096..+0.113 m: bay-3 S-hooks (t1_detail.py:3159-3161) built -0.503/-0.572/-0.677/-0.750/-0.829/-0.907 from u = .13/.26/.46/.60/.75/.90; corrected BAYS[2]=(-0.8558,-0.3402) gives -0.4072/-0.4742/-0.5773/-0.6495/-0.7268/-0.8042 (delta +0.096..+0.103), retired REF map (bay-3 image edges 588,699 -> -0.4359..-0.9607) gives -0.5041..-0.9082 (delta 0.001). Bay 2 gal_appliance (t1_detail.py:3200) built -0.0320..-0.2180 from u 0.44-0.80; corrected +0.0780..-0.1076 (delta +0.110), REF map -0.0339..-0.2194 (delta 0.002). gal_warmer (t1_detail.py:3311) built -0.6860..-0.9550 from image x 641-698; corrected -0.5864..-0.8513 (delta +0.100), REF map -0.6865..-0.9560 (delta 0.001). GAL_SEAM_X (t1_detail.py:2880) -0.6140 from u 0.34; corrected -0.5155 (delta +0.099). RENDER, out/r49board_side.png, aperture box computed from the model's own stations (image_x = 800.7 - 268.9*X, scale verified by the two bay pitches 168.0/173.5 px against 0.625/0.645 m -> 268.8/269.0 px/m): bay 3's hook row measures at u 0.32 / 0.46 / 0.67 / 0.81 / 0.98 and the sixth hook is off the aperture, against ref_side.jpg's 0.13 / 0.26 / 0.46 / 0.60 / 0.75 / 0.90 (crops probe_scratch/rev50_counter-galley_rend_bay3rail6x.png and rev50_counter-galley_photo_bay3rail8x.png, both read as images). The two hanging tools land at u 0.32/0.46 rendered against 0.13/0.26 photographed. Predicted shift from +106 mm / 0.5155 m = +0.206; observed +0.19 +- 0.02.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter-argument: the offset is deliberate because Y and Z are admittedly chosen, so X might be too. Refuted three ways. (a) The header explicitly names the aperture-fraction method and SPEC 10.11/RULES 3-4 as the reason, so a 106 mm rigid departure from it is not a choice, it is the retired map. (b) The offset is not a free parameter: it reproduces (495.8-u)/211.5 to 1-2 mm on twelve features in three bays measured in two separate revisions, which no deliberate choice would do. (c) It is visible in the render, not only in the source: the hook row is measurably 0.19 aperture-widths aft and the last hook has left the aperture. Second counter-argument: my image_x(X) map could be wrong. It is anchored on the model's own bay pitches (two independent pitches agreeing to 0.1%) and independently reproduces the counter slab thickness (29 px / 268.9 = 107.8 mm against the built 107.0). Third: could REF's map be the right one and rev 13 wrong? Then the APERTURES would be 105 mm out, and the bays' rendered widths (137-140 px = 0.510-0.520 m) match BAY_W 0.5155 exactly, so the apertures are where rev 13 put them.

This trace reproduces those breaks, because they are what was measured ... The finding therefore does not rest on refuting the mask. It rests on the file's own sentence: the mask is a 4-6 px stroke segmentation at a 2.07 px PSF, its breaks are declared tarnish artefacts, and the file itself says to bridge them when looking right matters more than measuring right. ### 10. [BLOCKING] The curvature edge-wear system degenerates into an all-over grey lichen speckle on every un-subdivided panel — 22.4 % coverage where the SAME material renders 0.01 % on the shell
*Materials and shading (t1_mats.py) — body paint, cream family, chrome/brightwork, rubber/tyre, glass, emissive bulbs, the WEATHER weathering/wear system, orange*  
**confidence** high · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** WEATHER's chip path is supposed to select EDGES via Geometry->Pointiness. On the subdivided shell it does. On every object built as a solid_prism / flat detail mesh, Pointiness saturates to 1.0 over the whole face, so BOTH gates in the chain saturate — `pw = smoothstep(PT, 0.520, 0.600)` -> 1 (t1_mats.py:962) and `deep = smoothstep(pw, 0.80, 1.00)` -> 1 (t1_mats.py:974) — and the chip mask collapses to `cm*clm > 0.35`, i.e. object-space noise with NO edge selectivity at all. The counter top and fascia, the raised-lid back skin, the sign skins and the VW roundel disc are therefore covered in hard-edged grey chips in the middle of flat painted boards. Because `apply_weather` links WEATHER's Metallic output whenever wear > 0 (t1_mats.py:1143-1144), roughly a quarter of those chips render at Metallic 0.7 on W_STEEL's 0.56 grey — bare steel in the middle of a painted plank, which is why 3.12 % of the fascia's chips come out BRIGHTER than the paint rather than darker. This is the loudest single CG tell in every shipped hero.

**Evidence.** INTERNAL CONTROL (needs no photograph), out/r49s_front.png, one material `paint`, one frame, one light: shell nose cream rows 640-700 cols 560-700 = 0.01 % of pixels more than 6 % below their local 25-px median, p2/p98 [-0.028,+0.023]; shell cab side cream rows 470-520 cols 430-520 = 0.00 %, [-0.029,+0.023]; raised-lid BACK skin rows 190-340 cols 670-930 = 22.44 % below -6 % and 9.61 % above +6 %, p2/p98 [-0.315,+0.181]. Both objects carry `paint` (build.py:290 body, build.py:313 lid_skins). Crop probe_scratch/rev50_materials_T1paint_lidskin_vs_shell.png. PHOTOGRAPHIC CONTROL at matched physical scale: out/r49base_side.png counter fascia (`countercream`, build.py:330) rows 639-655 cols 1000-1270, side ortho 1600 px / studio.py:1360 ortho_scale 5.90 m = 271.2 px/m -> 17.75 % below -6 %, 3.12 % above +6 %, p2/p98 [-0.138,+0.069], darkest specks -15.8 %, median blob 109 mm^2. ref_side.jpg rows 423-433 cols 660-900 at the project's own 211.4 px/m -> 0.58 % below -6 %, p2/p98 [-0.019,+0.017], row sd 2.5-3.4 DN. Resampled to 211.4 px/m and blurred by a generous sigma 0.8 px PSF the render's 100-mm-box residual is 3.53 % against the reference's 1.71 %; unblurred 4.91 % against 1.71 %. NOISE FLOOR: r49base_side minus r49board_side over the same fascia window = sd 0.465 DN, i.e. <=0.33 DN per image (0.25 %), three orders below the effect (denoising on, studio.py:1144-1146). ANALYTIC PREDICTION from the source constants: Monte-Carlo of cm=clamp((n-0.42)/0.16), clm=clamp((n-0.44)/0.16), hard=(cm*clm)>0.35, core=(n>0.573) with Blender Fac ~ N(0.5, 0.10-0.13) gives 25.4-26.1 % chip coverage and 5.9-7.5 % of the panel at bare steel; measured 17.8 % dark + 3.1 % bright = 20.9 %. Second closer view: out/r49s_counter.png fascia rows 640-790 cols 60-900 = 22.65 % below -6 %, p2 = -0.363. Crops: probe_scratch/rev50_materials_render_fascia_x8.png vs rev50_materials_ref_fascia_x8.png; rev50_materials_render_countertop.png vs rev50_materials_ref_countertop.png; rev50_materials_render_roundel.png; rev50_materials_counter.png. Mechanism is stated by the file itself at t1_mats.py:247-255.

**Its own refutation.** (a) 'ref_side.jpg is too soft to resolve 40 mm blobs.' Refuted: the same band resolves the ~5 mm gold counter-nosing pinstripe immediately above it as a distinct coloured 1-2 px line, and the render's median blob is 109 mm^2 = 4.9 px^2 at the reference's scale carrying 11-30 DN of contrast against a 2.9 DN row sd. (b) 'It is the W_ALBEDO 0.260 breakup, not chips, so the lever is a different constant.' Refuted: a multiplicative noise field is symmetric, and the measured residual is strongly one-sided — 17.75 % below -6 % against 3.12 % above, p2 -0.138 against p98 +0.069 — with hard boundaries, which is the GREATER_THAN(craw, W_CHIP_CUT) signature at t1_mats.py:972, not a MapRange. (c) 'It is dust.' Refuted from the graph: the fascia sits at z ~0.95-1.05 m, above W_DUST_Z_HI 0.480, so `hgt` = 0; its normal is horizontal so `upn` = 0; and W_DUST_FAC_UP is retired to 0.0. Both dust branches are identically zero there. (d) 'SPEC rev6 sec.3 rules the finish WEATHERED with chipped edges, so this is a ruling not a defect.' It says EDGES, and the file's own note at t1_mats.py:247-255 diagnoses this as an artefact of per-vertex Pointiness on unsubdivided meshes; the rev-12 cluster gate it added reduced coverage without restoring edge selectivity. (e) 'rev 14 / SPEC 10.29 already measured and REFUTED weathering-too-heavy.' That RMS was taken on the FLANK cream — a subdivided surface where pw = 0.500 and the chip path is exactly zero — so it could not see this; the surface it vindicated is the one I measure here at 0.01 %. (f) 'It is Monte-Carlo noise.' Refuted by the pair-difference above.

### 11. [BLOCKING] The stitched delivery frame has NO contact shadow — hero.py's T1_FX=0 gates out the whole of SPEC 10.116, and post.py has no shadow stage to replace it. The brief's claim is CONFIRMED.
*Lighting, shadow, camera and optics*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** hero.py:112 sets "T1_FX": "0" in the strip environment. studio.py:762 computes `on = optics and _envi("T1_FX", 1)`, and studio.py:825 gates the ENTIRE contact-shadow subgraph on that same `on` (`if on and _sh > 1.0:`). So on the stitched path the alpha floor+gain never runs and the vehicle returns to the rev-45 floating arm exactly. post.py cannot put it back: its whole option set is _FLOATS = {bloom, ca, vig, grain, ...} (post.py:384) and main() (post.py:441-465) applies backdrop_headroom -> bloom -> chromatic -> vignette -> grain. There is no shadow stage anywhere in the file. Nor can a matte rescue it: studio.matte_tap.__doc__ states plainly that "hero.py does not do that today and this file cannot make it; until it does, the matte for a stripped hero has to come from a single-pass render", and hero.py:167 invokes `post.py raw final` with no --matte at all. Every other optic survives the round trip (post.py re-applies CA, vignette, grain); the contact shadow is the one element of the chain that lives ONLY in the compositor and is therefore the one element the delivery path silently drops. SPEC 10.116 is titled "optics-6 CLOSED" and 10.116.5 publishes G1 0.9756 -> 0.8729 as WHAT SHIPS. On the prescribed delivery path (SPEC 10.14: "four abutting T1_BORDER strips, T1_FX=0, post.py once on the stitch") it does not ship.

**Evidence.** SOURCE: hero.py:110-116 (env.update with T1_FX:"0"); studio.py:762 (`on = optics and _envi("T1_FX", 1)`); studio.py:825 (`if on and _sh > 1.0:`); studio.py:846-870 (the floor/power/SetAlpha subgraph, all inside that block); post.py:384-388 (_FLOATS/_STRS - no shadow key); post.py:441-465 (main - bloom/chromatic/vignette/grain only); hero.py:167 (post invoked with a.post_args default ""). POSITIVE CONTROL on the direct build.py path which keeps T1_FX=1: out/r49s_hero34f.png, ground window rows 950-972 x cols 700-780 (just outboard of the near front tyre contact) mean 226.09 DN sd 6.06 against open ground rows 930-990 x cols 1150-1400 mean 255.00 -> ratio 0.8866; tightened to rows 947-955 the same window gives 215.80 -> 0.8463. Those bracket SPEC 10.116.5's claimed 0.8729, so the gain demonstrably IS live on the direct path and demonstrably IS gated off on the strip path. Window caveat (rule 8): the ratio moves 0.846->0.887 over an 8-row vs 22-row window, so quote the window with the number. What would make this reading wrong: if the owner has stopped using hero.py and now judges only direct build.py renders - every frame currently in out/ is a direct render (they carry the shadow, so they cannot have come from hero.py).

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter-argument: post.py might re-create the shadow under another name. I read every function in post.py - srgb_to_lin, lin_to_srgb, _blur, _erode, _border_connected, backdrop_mask, backdrop_headroom, bloom, chromatic, vignette, grain, _parse, _mask, main - and none darkens the ground; backdrop_headroom only ever SCALES the masked backdrop with k clamped <= 1 applied uniformly, which cannot make a localised pool. Second counter: maybe T1_FX=0 was chosen deliberately and the shadow was meant to be re-applied in post; hero.py's own docstring says only "Optics (bloom/CA/vignette/grain) are forced OFF during the strip renders ... and applied once by post.py" - it enumerates four effects and the shadow is not among them, which is why the rev-45 addition slipped through the gate untracked. Third counter: is the shadow node really inside the gate? It sits between `on = ...` at line 762 and the bloom block, and its own `if` tests `on` first - verified by reading, not inferred. Survives.

It manufactures m1, and m1 is indeed 0.05-0.06 in the render — while m5 sits independently at 0.05 with m2/m3/m4 dead, which a centring error cannot produce. Fifth: I checked the source is not doing this deliberately — t1_detail.py:145-149 argues explicitly that the holes 'must stay under the hubcap' and believes they do; the defect is that nothing compares the two surfaces' AXIAL positions.

### 16. [MAJOR] t1_detail.py:2124-2126 still publishes "length 0.385" as the MEASURED louvre-block length while the build ships 0.2952 — 90 mm apart, 2.2 sigma outside the header's own stated error, and nothing in the file says so
*The flank, belt line and louvres — side elevation of the RED target bus (ref_side.jpg / ref_rear34.jpg), with geometry-only corroboration from the GREEN bus (IM*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING to correct the comment and re-parameterise the block as (station, length). A raking-light frame (PHOTOS_WANTED_rev48 item 3) is needed before the LENGTH itself can be re-valued.

**What is wrong.** The block header presents "MEASURED on ref_side.jpg, FRAME UN-DROPPED: block X -1.285 ... -1.670 (+-0.03/0.04), length 0.385" as the measurement of record. Twenty lines later, t1_detail.py:2144 passes BOTH endpoints through T._aft(), which re-spaces an aft station by its fraction of the rear overhang (t1_core.py:60-70, O_NEW/O_OLD = 0.773/1.008 = 0.7669). That does not just move the block — it SCALES ITS LENGTH by 0.7669, to 0.2952. Nothing between the two lines acknowledges that the published length no longer describes the build; the true value is printed 200 lines away in probe_rev48_louv.py:22's watched print. This is the RIM_R / X_TAIL / SCR-z1 pattern that has cost this project whole revisions, and it is a live trap: a future revision that "restores the measurement" lengthens the block by 90 mm. Separately, the parameterisation itself is wrong in kind. A pressed louvre block is a fixed stamping. Only its STATION should re-space with the overhang; its LENGTH should not. Expressed as two aft stations, the block's length will move again for no physical reason the next time the overhang is re-measured — and the same two constants drive louvre_cutters() (:2210-2211) and louvre_backing() (:2243-2244), so the aperture and the bay follow it.

**Evidence.** t1_detail.py:2123-2130 (the header's claim), :2144 `LOUV_X0, LOUV_X1 = T._aft(-1.2850), T._aft(-1.6700)`, t1_core.py:56-70 (`_aft`, O_OLD 1.008, O_NEW 0.773), probe_rev48_louv.py:22 (watched print: x -1.5371..-1.2419, len 0.2952). Confirmed independently in the shipped render: probe_scratch/rev50_render_louv.py finds the block's dark rows repeatedly bounded at cols 1137..1217 = X -1.2427..-1.5377 through the exact ortho — the built values to 1 mm. Which number is right? My own read of ref_side.jpg (probe_scratch/rev50_louv_ends.py, horizontal high-pass profiles along the three strongest slot rows 471/472, 481, 490, cols 740-880) puts the dark lines at cols 772..837 -> X -1.2086..-1.4946, length 0.286 — consistent with the BUILD, not the header. Treat that as an indication only, see the next-but-one finding.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter-argument: the header is not stale, it is simply in a different frame — it says "FRAME UN-DROPPED", so perhaps -1.285/-1.670 are pre-_aft authored inputs and the reader is expected to apply _aft mentally. That fails on the word LENGTH: 0.385 is quoted as a measured metric quantity with its own +-0.03/0.04, and a length measured off a photograph lives in the real vehicle's frame, which _aft does not change. The build's 0.2952 is 2.2 sigma outside it. Second counter-argument: maybe the build is the thing that is wrong and 0.385 is right. My ref_side read (0.286) says no — but that estimator failed its own positive control, so I am NOT asserting the build's length is correct, only that the header and the build cannot both be, and that the header is the one with no live derivation behind it. Third: is re-spacing a length ever defensible? Yes, for a lofted shell's own knots, which is what the rev-16 note at t1_core.py:47-56 was written about. It is not defensible for a discrete stamping, and the note never distinguishes the two cases.

### 17. [MAJOR] The louvre BLADES are centred on the ten measured SLOT centres, so every rendered aperture sits half a pitch from the station it was measured at and the block's dark band is 16.5 mm taller than the measurement
*The flank, belt line and louvres — side elevation of the RED target bus (ref_side.jpg / ref_rear34.jpg), with geometry-only corroboration from the GREEN bus (IM*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING to fix the construction (offset the blade stack by half a pitch and pull the cutter's z margin back to the outermost slot). The raking-light frame of PHOTOS_WANTED_rev48 item 3 is what confirms the sign and settles LOUV_APERTURE with it.

**What is wrong.** The header records the measurement as "10 slots, pitch 21.1 mm, top slot centre 1.085, bottom slot centre 0.895, slot aperture ~7 mm". A slot is an OPENING — the ten dark lines in the photograph are the apertures. The build then places a solid blade at each of those ten stations: t1_detail.py:2271 `z = LOUV_Z_BOT + LOUV_PITCH * k` and :2276 `pr = [(a * -s, b + LOUV_SECT * 0.5) ...]` centre the 14.11 mm blade section on z, and the docstring at :2262-2263 states it outright — "The 11 mm section is centred on the measured slot centre". So the model puts METAL where the photograph shows a HOLE, and the holes fall between. On top of that, louvre_cutters() opens the aperture half a blade PLUS 2 mm past the outermost blade at each end (t1_detail.py:2208-2209), which adds a full-width dark band above the top blade and below the bottom one. Net: the built aperture band is one pitch taller than the measured slot band, and every aperture is 10.6 mm from its measured station.

**Evidence.** Shipped render out/r49board_side.png, measured through the exact ortho (ppm 271.1864, x_px = 800 - ppm*X, y_px = 962.203 - ppm*Z) parsed from studio.views()["side"], probe_scratch/rev50_render_louv.py: at three independent column windows (1150-1156, 1170-1176, 1195-1201) the block gives 11 dark minima at rows 672,677,683,689,694,700,706,712,717,723,728, pitch 5.600 px = 20.65 mm, top band z 1.0701, bottom band z 0.8636, dark span 206.5 mm. Measured slot span from the header, carried into the above-ground frame (authored minus rake_drop(-1.39) = 0.02325): 1.0617 down to 0.8717 = 190.0 mm. 206.5 - 190.0 = 16.5 mm = 8.7 %, and the interior bands sit at the half-pitch midpoints (e.g. render 1.0517 against the midpoint of the measured 1.0617 and 1.0406, which is 1.05115). Crop: probe_scratch/rev50_flank-belt_r49_louv_x10nn.png.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter-argument, and it killed the loud half of this finding: I first wanted to say "the render shows 11 dark bands where the photograph shows 10". IMG_2073.jpeg (GREEN bus, geometry transfers) refutes that — probe_scratch/rev50_green_louv.py at cols 1166-1172 and 1172-1178 gives ELEVEN dark minima (rows 476, 483, 491.5, 500, 508, 516, 524, 532, 540.5, 548.5, 557) at a uniform 8.15 px pitch. So the count is right and I withdraw it. What survives is narrower and does not depend on the count: the build's own header says the ten measured stations are SLOT centres and the build's own code puts blade metal on them, so the aperture phase is 10.6 mm off by construction and the aperture band is 16.5 mm taller than the measurement. Second counter-argument: 16.5 mm is 3.6 px at ref_side.jpg's 217 px/m, at the edge of that frame's resolution, so the visual cost is small — true, and stated. This is a per-measurement error with a small visible consequence, not a large visible one. Third: is the block's centre still right? Yes — built centre 0.96675 AG against the measured centre 0.96670, so the error is purely phase and extent, which is why nothing about it shows in a modulation statistic.

### 18. [MAJOR] The louvre block's length and station CANNOT be measured from ref_side.jpg — I built three instruments and all three failed their kill or positive controls, one of them on the render where the answer is exact
*The flank, belt line and louvres — side elevation of the RED target bus (ref_side.jpg / ref_rear34.jpg), with geometry-only corroboration from the GREEN bus (IM*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** A raking-light frame of the rear quarter at the block's own scale, or any frame of the RED bus's rear quarter at IMG_2073's resolution. PHOTOS_WANTED_rev48 item 3 should be widened from "pressing depth" to "pressing depth, block length and block station".

**What is wrong.** PHOTOS_WANTED_rev48 item 3 is currently scoped to the pressing DEPTH. That scope is too narrow. The block's LENGTH is unverified (see the previous finding), its STATION is unverified, and neither can be recovered from the frame the project holds. Three independent estimators, two of which looked authoritative, all fail. This matters because the next revision will look at a 7x crop, see the slot lines plainly, and read the ends by eye — as I did twice, getting 0.310 m and then 0.377 m before the numerics gave 0.286 m. Any block geometry "re-measured" off ref_side.jpg must be rejected unless the estimator is first shown to recover the render's known ends.

**Evidence.** probe_scratch/rev50_flank_louv5.py: normalised modulation (std of the 9-tap-detrended column profile / local mean luminance), cols 700-900 — median 0.14 over the block band rows 443-492 against 0.15-0.24 over the blank painted band rows 500-549; blank paint is MORE modulated than the block. probe_scratch/rev50_flank_louv6.py: a row-coherence detector (each column's high-passed profile correlated against the rake-sheared mean of its 14 neighbours) reads median 0.86 on the block against 0.62 and 0.45 on two blank-paint bands — not separable. probe_scratch/rev50_blockends.py: a fractional-threshold longest-run end-finder repeated over 6 slot rows x 4 thresholds, run as a POSITIVE CONTROL on out/r49board_side.png where the block is exactly -1.2419..-1.5371, returns fwd -1.2591 +- 0.0345 and aft -1.4152 +- 0.0785, i.e. it under-reads the aft end by 122 mm and the length by 140 mm; on ref_side.jpg the same estimator returns -1.298 +- 0.085 / -1.431 +- 0.054, length 0.134 +- 0.049. probe_scratch/rev50_flank_louv4.py: periodic-power pitch search returns 9.44 px on the block against 6.80 px at amplitude 3.39 on a control window of plain painted flank and 12.12 px at 2.15 on a control window below the block — the block is not separable from paint by periodicity either, exactly as probe_rev48_louv.py's deleted bounder found.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter-argument: I am declaring the frame unusable when a better estimator exists — for instance a matched comb filter at the built pitch and rake. I tried the narrowband version of exactly that (probe_scratch/rev50_flank_louv3.py / louv4.py) and it is the estimator whose control failed hardest, returning a plausible 9.44 px pitch on the block and a comparable 6.80 px on blank paint. Second counter-argument: my eye clearly separates the block, so a good instrument must exist. That is true and is precisely the danger — the eye's discriminator is 'horizontal AND periodic', and both of my attempts to encode it (coherence, periodic power) are defeated by JPEG blockiness and shading gradients at this frame's 2.32 bits/px. Reporting 'I cannot measure it' is the honest output; reporting a number I could not validate on the render is what rev 47's LINE_GAP did. Third: the positive control on the render might itself be unfair because the render's paint-wear speckle fragments the runs — that is the point; ref_side.jpg's paint has scrollwork and flowers over the same band, which fragments them worse.

### 19. [MAJOR] BOTH HEADLAMPS AND BOTH BULLET INDICATORS ARE PLACED WITH ZERO ROTATION -- their axes are exactly +X
*The nose and front: nose face and plan crown (W4), V swage, VW roundel and bars, headlamps and bowls, indicators, front blade/bumper, windscreen aperture and ru*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING for the fact. The magnitude of the splay is the same quantity finding 1 is blocked on -- but any non-zero splay tied to the nose's own local normal is strictly better than the present zero.

**What is wrong.** Every lamp part is a body of revolution about X built at the origin and placed with a TRANSLATION ONLY. So on the finished vehicle the two headlamp axes are exactly parallel to each other and to the vehicle centreline, the two bullet indicators likewise, and the bored recesses likewise. There is no outboard splay and no vertical cant anywhere in the front lighting. That is wrong even against the model's OWN nose, whose panel normal at y=0.545 is 1.2 deg off +X, and it is the mechanism that guarantees the render can never reproduce finding 1's signature. It is also the cheapest thing in this dimension to fix and it is independent of how W4 finally resolves: whatever crown is chosen, the lamps and pods must be set normal to it.

**Evidence.** build.py:506 `D.place(o, loc=(HL_X, s * HL_Y, HL_Z))` for ring, lens and bowl -- no rot= argument. build.py:520-521 `D.place(ibase, loc=(2.0960, s * IND_Y, IND_Z))` and the same for ilens. t1_shell.py:412-473 `headlamp_recess_cutters` extrudes the bore along `(-1, 0, 0)` with the profile plane spanned by (0,s,0) and (0,0,1), i.e. bore axis = X for both sides. Corroborating photographs that the real parts are NOT axis-parallel: ref_nolita_front34.jpg's bullet pod is canted forward-and-down relative to the image horizontal (probe_scratch/rev50_nose-front_nol_ind_grid2.png, pod x 264-277, y 235-253, with a cast shadow on the red at x 272-282 y 250-256); ref_side.jpg's near indicator (probe_scratch/rev50_nose-front_refside_nose4x.png) points forward and downward, not along the image x-axis.

**Its own refutation.** Could the real lamps be AIMED forward rather than set normal to the panel, making zero splay correct? On a road car the beam is aimed, but the BEZEL and the pressed bowl follow the sheet metal, and the bezel is what the camera sees. And it cannot rescue the indicators: a bullet pod is bolted through the panel and its axis is the panel normal by construction. I also checked this is not already handled downstream -- `nose_shape` runs on the body only (build.py:235), after the detail objects have not yet been created, and nothing else rotates them.

### 20. [MAJOR] THE TWO WINDSCREEN PANES ARE COPLANAR -- the model has no split screen, it has one flat screen with a slot in it
*The nose and front: nose face and plan crown (W4), V swage, VW roundel and bars, headlamps and bowls, indicators, front blade/bumper, windscreen aperture and ru*  
**confidence** high · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING for the fact that the panes are coplanar -- that is read straight off the source. The VEE ANGLE needs a measurement: ref_workshop.jpg is the frame (glass out, sharpest silhouette in the set), and my first attempt at tracing the aperture's top rail (probe_scratch/rev50_ws_header.py) recovered only 16 usable columns on the far pane because the pale wall behind kills the contrast there. A bottom-rail trace or a lower CMIN would likely close it without any new photograph.

**What is wrong.** `_ws` builds both panes from ONE origin plane and ONE basis: same `WS_MID + WS_N*off`, same `(0,1,0)` / `-WS_DIR` / `WS_N` frame, differing only by a y offset of +-(WS_DIV + WS_PANE_W/2). WS_N is constructed with zero y-component. So the aperture, the glass and the seals are all a single flat plane 1.222 m wide, and the centre divider is a 52 mm slot in it rather than the pillar between two panes set at an included angle. A T1's screen is two FLAT panes vee'd about the centre pillar precisely because the nose is domed and a single flat pane cannot follow it -- the divider exists to carry that kink. This is the same statement as W4 made at a second station, and it will keep the cab reading flat even after the nose panel is crowned.

**Evidence.** t1_shell.py:12-18 `WS_N = Vector((-WS_DIR.z, 0.0, WS_DIR.x))` -- y component hard-zero; t1_shell.py:22-30 `_ws()` loops `for s in (1,-1)` changing only `yc`, then `T.solid_prism(o, (0,1,0), tuple(-WS_DIR), tuple(WS_N), pts, depth)` -- one plane, one extrusion direction, both panes. t1_shell.py:33-56 cutters, glass and seals all route through `_ws`/the same basis, so the aperture inherits it. Visible in out/r49s_front.png (probe_scratch/rev50_nose-front_r49s_front_small.png): head-on, both panes read as one plane with a thin bar; the divider carries no width and no shading break. ref_workshop.jpg with the glass out shows a substantial divider seen with two faces and a slope change in the aperture's top rail across it (probe_scratch/rev50_nose-front_wk_ws4x.png).

**Its own refutation.** Is the T1 screen really vee'd, or two coplanar flats with a decorative bar? If it were coplanar there would be no reason to divide it at all -- a single pane would be cheaper. And my own header trace on ref_workshop.jpg fits one straight line at 3.31 px rms with a 21.3 px worst residual, but splits into two segments with slopes -0.253 and +0.014 at joint rms 1.08 -- consistent with a kink, though on only 16 far-side columns, which is why I have marked the ANGLE blocked rather than quoting it.

### 21. [MAJOR] The one aperture the owner designated for service has no rubber seal, no hinge and no stay — every other aperture on the vehicle has a seal
*THE TAIL AND REAR — tail panel, engine lid + shut line, T-handle, 1963 plate, tail lamps, rear window aperture and what shows through it, rear bumper, tail boar*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING for the seal (the side bays' own conform_ring recipe applies unchanged, and the aperture outline already exists). The HINGE TYPE and whether the pane is stay-held or counterbalanced ARE blocked — t1_shell.py:1392 already declares it and the owner said at rev 49 that no new photograph is possible. A seal is not blocked by that: it is present whether the pane is open or shut.

**What is wrong.** bay_seals() (t1_shell.py:214-226) builds conform_ring surrounds for the two cab doors and the three side bays, per side. It builds NOTHING for the rear aperture. rear_cutter() (t1_shell.py:233) leaves a bare 2.8 mm sheet edge, and open_rear_hatch() (t1_shell.py:1809) swings `glass_rear` — a 6 mm glazed prism with no frame, no gasket, no hinge barrel, no bracket and no prop — to 64 degrees on nothing. In the shipped hero the pane reads as a flat translucent grey slab lying over a black hole. This is precisely the fidelity bar bus_model_ref.JPG was supplied to set: small features built as real geometry with depth that self-shadows rather than implied. And the black glazing gasket is one of the most prominent single features of the tail in ref_rear34.jpg — an ~18 px band at ~350 px/m against cream at L 176.

**Evidence.** SOURCE, exhaustively: `grep -n 'seal' t1_shell.py` returns seals only for DOOR_MAIN_S, DOOR_VENT_S and BAYS[i]; t1_shell.py:244-251 `rear_glass()` returns a bare solid_prism; t1_shell.py:1391-1394 states outright what is not built — 'the ANGLE... stay-held vs counterbalanced. NOTHING is built for it'. build.py:304 assigns `glass` and build.py:940 calls open_rear_hatch with nothing carried. RENDER: probe_scratch/rev50_tail-rear_REN_aperture4x.png (out/r49shut2_hero34r.png, x 1000-1290, y 510-700) — the pane's whole perimeter is a hard cut edge, no gasket, no hardware, and it hangs at 64 deg unsupported. PHOTO: probe_scratch/rev50_tail-rear_REF_uppertail4x.png — the tail's glazing carries a thick continuous black gasket, top edge y~203, gasket band y~270-290 at x 1035-1200.

**Its own refutation.** (1) 'Maybe the black band in the photo is shadow inside the aperture head, not a gasket.' Possible — and it is why I do not rest the finding on the photograph. The source fact is decisive on its own: four apertures on this vehicle, three get seals, one does not, and the one that does not is the one the owner just designated for service. (2) 'The hinge/stay is a declared NOT MEASURED, so proposing it violates rule 7(c).' Agreed, and I have split it: the seal and the fact that the pane has no mounting hardware of any kind are reportable; the stay's TYPE stays blocked. (3) 'A 7.5 mm ring is what the side bays get, so the rear one would be equally thin and would not read at hero scale.' Then the finding sharpens rather than dies — the side-bay ring section may itself be under-scale against a ~20 mm T1 glazing gasket, and that is measurable in the same frame.

### 22. [MAJOR] Both `lid_rail` objects are zero-area: `_rag_grid` is called with x0 == x1, so the lid's proud perimeter rail has never rendered
*The roof, lids and drip rail — roof skin/crown, roof aperture, raised mural lid (geometry, hinge, struts, junction), drip rail/gutter, bulb string, tail-board/r*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING to fix the degeneracy. Whether the built rail should be a full perimeter or two end ribs is a separate question ref_workshop.jpg can answer, since that is the frame the 26 ± 7 mm came from.

**What is wrong.** t1_shell.py:2021-2024 builds the rails as `for (xa, xb) in ((LID_X0, LID_X0), (LID_X1, LID_X1)): r = _rag_grid(RAG_HW, xa, xb, RAIL_PROUD, bows=False, nx=1, ny=18, name="lid_rail")`. Inside `_rag_grid` (t1_shell.py:1011-1021) the x of every vertex is `x0 + (x1 - x0) * ix / nx`; with x0 == x1 that is x0 for both ix = 0 and ix = 1, so vertices a and a+1 (and b, b+1) are COINCIDENT and every one of the 18 quads per rail is degenerate with zero area. `me.validate()` only strips faces with repeated INDICES, not repeated positions, so the objects survive, are counted in the 171, are assigned "paint" at build.py:314 — and contribute no pixels. The feature the comment promises ("the shallow frame the skin sits on, standing PROUD of the roof by the measured 26 +/- 7 mm", cited to ref_workshop.jpg) is absent from every shipped frame, and SPEC.md:4388 publishes "Proud height 0.0228 skin / 0.0213 rail" as a measured pair of which one half has never been in a render.

**Evidence.** Source-level and deterministic; no measurement window is involved. `grep -n _rag_grid t1_shell.py` returns exactly two lines — the definition at 1011 and this single call at 2022 — so the retired ragtop's grid builder now exists only to make degenerate rails. Confirmed absent in the renders: probe_scratch/rev50_roof-lids_REN_roofjunction4x.png (out/r49board_side.png, 500,400–1150,540) and probe_scratch/rev50_roof-lids_REN_counter.png show the aperture's edge as a bare cut with no raised rim anywhere along it. `grep -n "zero.area\|degenerate\|calc_area" verify.py verify_clone.sh` returns nothing, so neither VERIFY (0 fail, 0 warn) nor verify_clone (113 PASS) can see it. Separately, RAG_HW = 0.545 would give the rails a span of ±0.545 against an aperture of −0.545…+0.565, so even non-degenerate they would be 20 mm short on the show side and would be two transverse ribs rather than a perimeter.

**Its own refutation.** I tried three ways to be wrong. (a) A solidify modifier giving the ribbon volume: `_rag_grid` adds none (unlike `_lid_panel`, which does at t1_shell.py:1192). (b) `validate()` merging or repairing it into something with area: Blender's validate removes faces with duplicate vertex indices; here the indices differ and only the coordinates coincide, so the faces persist with zero area. (c) That something else renders the rail: the only other object at the aperture edge is the roof skin itself, and the crops show no proud rim. The one thing I cannot settle without Blender is whether Cycles silently drops the objects or renders them as nothing — either way the visible result is identical and the finding stands.

### 23. [MAJOR] The tread band has no transverse pattern — four revolved circumferential grooves only — while the target vehicle's tyre shows clear transverse lugs
*Wheels, tyres, hubcaps and stance*  
**confidence** high · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING to establish that lugs exist. A precise block pitch and pattern would need a closer frame; an approximate pitch is readable off ref_rear34's exposed arc if that is acceptable.

**What is wrong.** t1_detail.py:52-61 builds the tread as a crowned band with four circumferential grooves, revolved 112 ways about Y. There is nothing transverse: no lugs, no sipes, no shoulder blocks, and no bump or displacement on M['tyre'] (t1_mats.py:1820 is a dust film over a flat base). The rendered tread band is four smooth rings, which is what a toy tyre looks like. Rule 3's fidelity bar is specifically 'small features built as REAL GEOMETRY WITH DEPTH that self-shadows, rather than painted into a texture' — the tread is the largest such feature on the vehicle and it is absent entirely.

**Evidence.** PHOTOGRAPH ref_rear34.jpg (RED, target), crop (705,608)-(790,660) at x10 autocontrast: probe_scratch/rev50_wheels-stance_rear34_tread_x10.png shows an evenly pitched fan of transverse grooves running across the tread band and over the shoulder, ~10-12 across the exposed arc. Corroborated independently on IMG_2073 (green bus, geometry) front wheel — probe_scratch/rev50_wheels-stance_2073_frontwheel_x4.png shows the same lugged pattern with visible shoulder blocks at much higher resolution. RENDER out/r49shut2_hero34r.png, crop (880,880)-(960,950) at x10: probe_scratch/rev50_wheels-stance_rend_tread_x10.png shows only the four longitudinal grooves, unbroken.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest argument against: JPEG ringing along a high-contrast tyre/arch edge can manufacture stripes. Rejected — ringing runs PARALLEL to the edge that causes it, whereas these lines run radially ACROSS the tread band and continue around the shoulder curvature at a steady angular pitch. Second: ref_rear34 is a different frame of the same RED vehicle and tread is geometry, so it transfers; and the pattern reappears on the second vehicle at much higher resolution, where it is unambiguous. Third: could the render simply be too small to resolve tread at 96 spp? No — the same crop resolves the four circumferential grooves crisply, so transverse cuts of comparable depth would resolve too.

*Glazing, seals, mirrors and wipers*

This is the same evidence class as the over-rider bar the owner withdrew at rev 37 and the rear bumper SPEC 2.4 removed: stock hardware that the in-service photographs do not show. The photographs show the SPINDLES survive and the arms and blades are gone.
