Checked and it fails -- in nolita_front34b the pane's whole height is legible and the wiper would have to be parked at 90 deg to the built 20 deg park to hide there, and even then the BLADE at 13 mm x 300 mm would run along the sill as a black bar; the sill in that frame is a clean 7 px gasket with nothing on it. Second counter: I might be reading the wrong vehicle. Rejected -- all three frames are the red bus (two artwork states), and wipers are hardware, so geometry transfers per rule 2. Third counter: the vertical dark line at x 112-118 in the f34b crop could be an arm. Rejected: it terminates at y=200, 33 px above the sill at y=233, and a bottom-pivot arm must reach its spindle at the sill. I did NOT verify the spindle should stay -- the two dark cowl stubs are 3 px objects and could equally be washer jets; keep wiper_pivot/wiper_boss and delete only wblade/wiper_arm.

### 25. [MAJOR] The glazing has nothing to reflect: the white backdrop is a compositor fill with zero scene presence and the world is at 0.05
*Glazing, seals, mirrors and wipers*  
**confidence** high · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** studio.py:51-57 `cyclorama()` builds a FLAT ground plane only and its own docstring says 'Background comes from the compositor (pure white) instead'. studio.py:422-430 sets the world to white at strength `T1_WORLD` = 0.05. So every pane whose mirror ray leaves the ground plane -- which is every pane at every hero camera except the narrow band that happens to catch a softbox -- reflects a 0.05-strength world, i.e. nothing. The panes are therefore pure transmission over a dark interior. This is precisely the brief's 'a photoreal pane needs a surface reflection, not just transparency', and it is a SET defect, not a material defect: the white the viewer sees behind the bus does not exist to the glass.

**Evidence.** Source: studio.py:51-57, studio.py:422-430, studio.py:1196 `film_transparent`. Rendered consequence, off-side flank in out/r49s_front34.png (probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_REN_f34_offglass.png, 4x of box (490,460)-(820,620)): the cab door main, the door vent and the three off-side bay panes all render as near-uniform dark rectangles. Measured in sRGB codes over the boxes named: bay pane (645,480)-(700,540) mean 38.4, max 101; bay pane (575,470)-(625,535) mean 32.2, max 93; adjacent cream pillar (628,480)-(642,540) mean 66.3, max 96. NO pane pixel is brighter than the sheet metal beside it. Contrast the target frames: ref_playa_34.png cab door main glass box (205,140)-(245,172) mean 128.3 with max 255 against the cream cowl box (75,192)-(110,200) max 239 -- the glass carries pixels BRIGHTER than the paint; ref_nolita_doorshut.jpg door main (80,145)-(110,170) max 189, and probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_ref_ds_doorgrid.png shows the reflected wall and palms as legible image content on both panes. The clearest single instance is the hinged-open rear pane in out/r49shut2_hero34r.png (probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_REN_h34r_tail4x.png): a pane tilted up ~40 deg to the sky renders as a dark speckled slab.

**Its own refutation.** I first tried to prove this with a texture statistic and IT REFUTED ME, so I dropped it: blurred coefficient of variation on the panes gives render 0.30-0.46 against photograph 0.15-0.59 -- no separation, because the render's panes are full of structured INTERIOR geometry. The claim that survives is narrower and is about the high end, not the variance: in the render no pane pixel exceeds the adjacent bodywork (ratio of maxima 101/96 = 1.05, 93/96 = 0.97), while in ref_playa_34.png the same comparison is 255/239 = 1.07 with the bright content spatially organised as a reflection. Second counter: `spec=0.35` on t1_mats.py:1816 (Specular IOR Level 0.35 against the physical 0.5) would also cut the surface reflection by ~30 %. I did NOT confirm this -- Blender 4.x may ignore Specular IOR Level once Transmission Weight is 1.0, and I could not run Blender to A/B it. Treat 0.35 as a suspect with unknown provenance, not as the cause. Third counter: raising T1_WORLD is the obvious fix and it is the wrong one -- the comment at studio.py:426 records it was cut 0.30 -> 0.17 -> 0.05 because achromatic fill desaturates the paint, which is W6's problem. The fix that serves both is W6's own conclusion: a diffuse dome / a real white card the glass can see, not a brighter uniform world.

### 26. [MAJOR] The mirror arm is a horizontal 185 mm stub; the photographed arm is a long stalk raked up and aft from a bracket at the base of the A-pillar
*Glazing, seals, mirrors and wipers*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** t1_detail.py:1741-1753 `mirrors()` builds `mir_arm` as a cylinder from (1.815, +/-0.905, 1.545) along (0.16, 0.98, 0.10), length 0.185, and places `mir_head` at (1.845, +/-1.052, 1.567). The head therefore sits 22 mm ABOVE and 30 mm FORWARD of where the arm meets the body -- the arm is essentially horizontal, and its root is at z 1.545 un-dropped, level with the middle of the windscreen. The photographed assembly is the opposite: a long slender stalk running from a bracket at the BASE of the A-pillar, at the windscreen's lower corner, up and outboard to a head that stands well above it.

**Evidence.** ref_playa_34.png -- TARGET vehicle, TARGET artwork (REFERENCE_FRAMES_rev45.md:43 confirms it is the only frame showing the livery being reproduced). probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_ref_playa_wsgrid.png (12x of box (55,110)-(200,215)) and probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_ref_playa_mirror.png (12x of box (140,150)-(320,220)). Read off the grids: head outline x 167.2..182.2, y 164.4..185.6, so head centre (174.7, 175.0) and major axis 21 px; the arm is a continuous thin dark line from the head's lower inboard corner down-forward to a white bracket at (146, 199). DIMENSIONLESS RESULT, which needs no scale: the head sits 24 px above its body mount against a head major axis of 21 px = 1.14 head-diameters. The model gives 0.022 m / 0.131 m = 0.17 head-diameters. A 6.7x difference in a scale-free ratio. In metres at the frame's 140 px/m (from the cab door glazing span, see ceiling): the head is ~170 mm above the mount against 22 mm built, and the mount is at the windscreen's lower edge rather than 138 mm above it.

**Its own refutation.** Counter 1: projection. The head is the outboard end, so it is closer to the camera than the mount, and in a front-3/4 with the mirror above the image centre that pushes the head UP -- inflating my 24 px. Bounded: the outboard run is ~150 mm at a camera distance of several metres, so the lift is a few pixels at most; halving the whole 24 px still leaves 0.57 head-diameters against 0.17. Counter 2: the line I call the arm might be a shadow or a cable. Rejected -- it is continuous, uniform, terminates in a visible white bracket, and the same assembly reads identically in the second crop at a different magnification. Counter 3: ref_workshop.jpg shows a completely different mirror (a big rounded-rectangle elephant-ear on a down-raked arm, probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_ref_ws_mirror_grid2.png). I nearly reported a shape-and-size finding off it and DROPPED IT: the workshop shell is the CONVERSION stage, the same class SPEC 10.75/10.93 already ruled does not transfer to service, and the in-service frames show a round head. Do not chase the rectangular mirror.

### 27. [MAJOR] The mirror is not a mirror: the whole head, reflecting face included, is one revolved solid in tarnished chrome with a 0.205-0.520 roughness map
*Glazing, seals, mirrors and wipers*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** build.py:404 `A(D.mirrors(), "chrome")` assigns one key to both the arm and the head. `chrome` is t1_mats.py:1812 `tarnished("chrome", (0.860,0.868,0.880), 0.14, 0.30)`, whose roughness is driven off an image texture through a Map Range with outputs 0.520 (tarnish) to 0.205 (clean leaf) -- t1_mats.py:1748-1753. There is no mirror-glass material in the file and no separate reflecting disc: `mir_head` (t1_detail.py:1745-1751) is a single revolve, so the rearward-facing plate a viewer sees from the rear 3/4 hero is a 20-52 %-rough metal dish. A door mirror's whole visual job is to carry a sharp reflected image; at roughness 0.2-0.5 it carries none. Secondly, the photographed head's shell is not chrome: it reads dark.

**Evidence.** Source: build.py:404, t1_mats.py:1740-1757 (tarnished), t1_mats.py:1812, t1_detail.py:1741-1753. Rendered: probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_REN_mirrorfar.png (8x of out/r49s_hero34f.png box (280,430)-(430,600)) shows the head as a dappled chrome dish with no reflected image; probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_REN_h34f_door2x.png shows the near head as a mottled chrome blob over the door glass; probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_REN_f34_mirror.png the same on the off side. Colour of the photographed shell, ref_playa_34.png, mean over box (168,168)-(180,182) = RGB (108.1, 103.0, 81.0) against cream body over (200,196)-(230,202) = (193.5, 128.5, 116.2). A tarnished-chrome shell in that light would not sit at 108 codes while the cream sits at 193.

**Its own refutation.** Counter to the material half: none survives -- it is a grep, not a reading. `chrome` is the only key on `mirrors()` and there is no second material. Counter to the dark-shell half: the head in ref_playa_34.png faces aft, so its back may simply be in its own shade; the sun in that frame is diffuse but the reading could still be a shadow rather than a dark paint. That is why the shell-colour claim is carried at medium and the roughness claim at high. Note also that this finding is partly masked today by finding 2 -- with a 0.05 world there is nothing for a correct mirror to reflect either, so fixing the material alone will not show. Fix the set first, then the mirror face.

### 28. [MAJOR] Three of the six S-hooks in bay 3 hang in mid-air: gal_rail is 165 mm too short AND 218 mm too far forward, and it crosses the pillar into bay 2
*The counter, galley and props (counter slab / nosing / top, galley interior seen through the three serving bays, the equipment and counter-top props, the galley*

The hooks on the next line reach X -0.907. The three aft hooks (-0.750, -0.829, -0.907) therefore hang 40, 119 and 197 mm beyond the end of the rail they are supposed to hang from, with nothing above them. At the other end the rail runs forward to X -0.050, which is inside BAYS[1] = (-0.2108, 0.3048): a rail whose own measurement is 'bay 3, u 0.02-0.98' crosses the pillar and shows in bay 2. The measured rail (u 0.02-0.98 of BAYS[2]) is centre -0.598, length 0.495; the built one is centre -0.380, length 0.660.

**Evidence.** t1_detail.py:3157-3161. Rendered and READ AS AN IMAGE, out/r49board_side.png cropped to bay 3 (probe_scratch/rev50_counter-galley_rend_bay3rail6x.png, 6x): the dark rail terminates at u 0.725 of the aperture and three isolated hook ticks stand at u 0.67, 0.81, 0.98 with no rail line above the last two. Predicted rail end from the source: u = (-0.3402 + 0.710)/0.5155 = 0.717 -- observed 0.725, 0.8% apart. ref_side.jpg bay 3 (probe_scratch/rev50_counter-galley_photo_bay3rail8x.png, 8x) shows every hook attached, the hook row running to u 0.90, and no hook past the aperture.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter-argument: T.cylinder's first argument might be an end, not a centre, in which case the rail spans -0.380..+0.280 and my complaint is mis-stated. Checked: t1_core.cylinder:679 calls solid_prism(center,...) and t1_core._frame:595 extrudes for s in (-depth/2, +depth/2) about origin -- it is the centre, and the other span would be worse, not better. Second counter-argument: the rail could be hidden behind the head rail so the floating hooks are invisible. Refuted by looking: the rail IS drawn in the shipped side view and its end is plainly visible, with bare hooks beyond it. Third: this could be an instance of finding 1 rather than a separate defect. It is not -- the rail's centre matches neither the corrected (-0.598) nor the retired-map (-0.698) station, so it is independently wrong; but note that fixing finding 1 alone would NOT reattach the hooks.

### 29. [MAJOR] gal_caddy_fill's X inset has the wrong sign: the pale fill is 24 mm WIDER than the caddy that contains it, protrudes 12 mm past both ends, and fill1 interpenetrates caddy0
*The counter, galley and props (counter slab / nosing / top, galley interior seen through the three serving bays, the equipment and counter-top props, the galley*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING. Two signs on one line.

**What is wrong.** t1_detail.py:3325-3326 builds the fill as _gbox(..., bx0 + 0.012, bx1 - 0.012, cy0 + 0.024, cy1 - 0.024, ...) inside a loop where bx0 is the FORWARD face and bx1 the AFT face, so bx0 > bx1 (both negative). bx0 + 0.012 moves the forward end 12 mm further forward and bx1 - 0.012 moves the aft end 12 mm further aft: the fill is 137 mm wide inside a 113 mm caddy. The Y pair on the same call (cy0 + 0.024, cy1 - 0.024, with cy0 < cy1) insets correctly, which is what the X pair was meant to do. Consequence: fill0 spans -1.0300..-1.1670 and pokes out of caddy0 (-1.0420..-1.1550) at both ends; fill1 spans -1.1480..-1.2850 and its forward 7 mm is INSIDE caddy0's solid.

**Evidence.** t1_detail.py:3322-3326. Rendered and READ AS AN IMAGE: out/r49s_counter.png cropped 5x at probe_scratch/rev50_counter-galley_rend_plate5x.png shows a white slab protruding forward from the front face of the near caddy, floating clear of it against the flank -- exactly a 12 mm proud fill seen at ~24 px. No such object exists in ref_side.jpg's or ref_rear34.jpg's box rank; both show plain closed foil boxes.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter-argument: the protrusion might be intended -- a napkin stack standing proud of an open-topped caddy. Refuted twice: (a) the fill's TOP at z 1.4060 is 3 mm BELOW the caddy top at 1.4090, so it is meant to sit down inside, not stand proud, and a fill that is simultaneously below the rim and outside the walls is not a modelled napkin stack; (b) the Y pair on the same call insets correctly, so the two X terms are the odd ones out. Second counter-argument: maybe it is invisible at hero scale. It is not -- it is legible in the shipped r49s_counter.png at 1:1 and I cropped it before I read the code.

### 30. [MAJOR] The counter's cream fascia carries a chip/wear field that NEITHER reference photograph has: 19.1% of its area is >=15 codes below the clean tone against the photograph's 0.66%
*The counter, galley and props (counter slab / nosing / top, galley interior seen through the three serving bays, the equipment and counter-top props, the galley*  
**confidence** high · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING. The mechanism is already diagnosed in t1_mats.py:249-257; what is missing is scoping the chip mask to the lip (or dropping WEAR for these two materials), and rendering one arm to confirm.

**What is wrong.** `countercream` and `countertan` are handed apply_weather(dust=1.4, wear=0.7) (t1_mats.py:2006-2008, 1982) and WEAR 0.7 is the joint-highest weight on the vehicle (t1_mats.py:681). The chip path is gated on Pointiness, and t1_mats.py:249-257 already records that pointiness saturates to 1.0 across the WHOLE counter slab because the slab is unsubdivided detail geometry, so 'Wear 0.7 turned into 29% chip coverage on a flat slab instead of a chipped lip'. The fix applied at the time was a low-frequency cluster gate (W_CLUST_SCALE 7.0 = 143 mm features), which reduced the AREA but left the chips on the flat face instead of on the lip. The result is a blotched, camouflage-patterned fascia and top on the largest, most-looked-at fitting in every 3/4 hero, where both RED frames show a clean uniform painted slab.

**Evidence.** Matched-scale, matched-length band comparison, both bands saved and READ AS IMAGES (probe_scratch/rev50_counter-galley_rendfascia_band.png vs rev50_counter-galley_photofascia_band.png). Window: the counter fascia between the brass nosing and the cream/red break, followed as a fitted line, 2.31 m of run in out/r49board_side.png at 268.9 px/m and 2.36 m in ref_side.jpg at 211.5 px/m. Fraction of area more than N display codes below the band's own p90 clean tone -- render 27.4% / 19.1% / 9.2% at N = 8 / 15 / 25, photograph 12.4% / 0.66% / 0.37%; sd(L) 9.43 against 4.22; means agree (191.0 vs 193.8), so this is texture, not exposure. RESOLUTION CONTROL RUN: downsampling the render to the photograph's 211.5 px/m leaves the figures unchanged (27.4/19.1/9.2 -> 28.0/19.6/9.1, sd 9.43), so it is not a sharpness artefact. Second view, out/r49s_counter.png, counter top band (probe_scratch/rev50_counter-galley_topsample_marked.png): 46% of the top's area >=15 codes below its p90, blotches 100-200 px across.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter-argument: ref_side.jpg is a 1024-px JPEG and chroma subsampling could iron out real chipping. Refuted by magnitude and scale -- the render's blotches are 17-140 mm patches 25-40 codes deep; JPEG at 211 px/m cannot erase a 25-code step 30 px wide, and the photograph's p2-to-p98 spread over 2.36 m of fascia is only 188.6 to 202.5. Second: the two frames could be a freshly repainted counter and the model an older one. Both RED frames -- ref_side.jpg and ref_rear34.jpg, different cameras, different light -- show the same clean fascia, and there is no photograph of this vehicle anywhere in the repo showing counter chipping. Third: is this just the vehicle-wide cream mottle, i.e. someone else's dimension? The body cream is separately governed (WEAR['cream'] = 0.3, less than half); the counter carries 0.7 and is the surface the pointiness note names by name, so this is the counter's own defect. Fourth: SPEC rev 12 warns 'the flank cream must not be cleaned up any further' (1.24% RMS against 7.37% measured) -- that is the FLANK's breakup node, a different node from the chip path, and it does not license 19% chip coverage on the counter.

Second: could boxes 3-5 be something else -- a reflection, or the Calidad decal behind? No: the luminance profile has five clean bright plateaus at 203-218 separated by four dark gaps down to 57-95, all at the same rows and the same height, and the 6x crop reads as five identical foil boxes. I first read the boxes as 26% too tall; that does not survive -- the model's top (z 1.409) matches the bright foil's top (z 1.4085 by the counter's own datum) to 0.5 mm, and what I was measuring was the separate dark cap band. Ratio 2.1-3.1x, centre 2.4x. POSITION: photo star centre (718.5,311.5) vs burst centre (785.5,344.5) -> dy = -0.423 burst-heights; build (149,1636) vs (1238,952) -> dy = +0.418. ### 33. [MAJOR] The menu-board header discards its own measured cap height; the '&' ships 58 % too tall
*Decals, signwriting and typography*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING. Measured on the shipped texture against a figure the same file declares.

**What is wrong.** lid_gen.py:734 sets cap_t = th * 0.46 from the measurement it states at line 184 ('cap height 64 px = 0.46 of the strip height'). Line 742 then rescales each word independently so the string exactly fills its measured x-run: f = _font(cap_t/0.72 * (x1-x0)/wpx), WITH NO UPPER CLAMP. Two measured quantities (run length and cap height) cannot both be met by one scale in a substitute face, and the code silently sacrifices the cap height without saying so. The result is five different type sizes on one strip and an ampersand set 1.58x the measured cap, which breaks below the baseline the caps sit on and is the loudest thing on the board in every side and hero frame. This is the file's ONLY unclamped text fit: _arched() at line 571 uses k = min(1.0, wpx/tot) and the side-strip branch at line 793 uses k = min(1.0, wid*0.86/wpx). The photograph's own numbers, in this same file at lines 177-184, bound it: the '&' run is 1658-1772 = 114 px against a 64 px cap, w/cap 1.78; built it is 91x83, w/cap 1.10.

**Evidence.** Measured on the generator's own output, tex/lidmural.png (2048x1238), th = round(1238*STRIP_T_F 0.0923) = 114, ink gate L < 0.62 x the strip's 95th-percentile L, each word inside its own declared u-run: FRESH 244x48 -> cap/th 0.421; JUICES, 283x63 -> 0.553 (comma descender included); GOURMET TACOS 588x44 -> 0.386; & 91x83 -> 0.728; TORTAS 286x48 -> 0.421. Declared 0.460. So the & is 1.58x the measurement and the four cap-only words are 0.84-0.92x it, with an 8 % step between the read words (0.421) and the reconstructed span (0.386). Source: lid_gen.py:184, 734-743 vs :571 and :793. Crops: rev50_decals-type_tex_topstrip.png (the texture strip, no lighting), rev50_decals-type_render_header.png (r49board_side.png x530-1090 y130-190), rev50_decals-type_ref_header.png (ref_side.jpg x320-760 y40-110).

**Its own refutation.** 'The owner ruled the yellow menu strips CORRECT as built, so this is retired.' His ruling was about the strips' presence and design against a proposal to change them; it cannot sensibly extend to a generator setting one glyph at 1.58x its own recorded measurement while the file's two sibling call sites clamp for exactly that. I also checked the two things that would have made this an artefact: (a) the strip height — STRIP_T_F 0.0923 gives th=114 and my row scan of the texture found the yellow ending at y 114-116, so th is right; (b) the missing word space in 'JUICES,GOURMET' — that is MEASURED (the photograph's gap is 935-926 = 9 px of 2400; the build's is 0.3896-0.3858 = 9.1 px), so I dropped it. And I dropped a separate 'wrong typeface class' claim: an ink-coverage test (half-max gate, self-normalised to the word's own ink bbox) gave 0.48 photo against 0.42 build — a 12 % difference, inside my instrument's error, so the face-weight claim does not survive on its own.

### 34. [MAJOR] The nose VW roundel's V-arms stop 15-19 mm short of the ring, against the source's own stated photographed requirement
*Decals, signwriting and typography*  
**confidence** high · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING for the built side. The photographed zero-gap is the source's own reading of ref_workshop.jpg, an oblique frame (axis ratio 0.687) — the only frame in the project that shows the nose emblem. A square-on nose frame would tighten it but is not needed to see that the V arms and the W arms disagree with each other in the build.

**What is wrong.** t1_detail.vw_logo_fit's docstring states the photographed condition explicitly: 'in the photograph every stroke end -- both V arms, both W outer arms, both W legs -- disappears into the ring band, and the ring's outer boundary is unbroken.' The rev-44 fix scales the glyph so its single extreme CORNER sits 20 % into the band. That corner is the outer corner of a mitred end face; the rest of every end lies inboard of it. In the shipped square-on frame the two W outer arms and both W legs do reach the band, but BOTH V arms stop clear of it with bare cream visible between the stroke end and the ring's inner edge. Both V-arm ends also carry a step/notch in the end face, visible at 7x in two independent frames, so it is mesh geometry rather than render noise.

**Evidence.** r49s_front.png, orthographic, 1600/3.55 = 450.7 px/m — verified against geometry: measured ring outer 63.0 px = 0.1398 m against ROUNDEL_D/2 = 0.1400 (build.py:579), measured ring inner 51.0 px = 0.1132 m against roundel()'s R-0.028 = 0.112 (t1_detail.py:929-932). V-arm end radii read off the marked overlay (green = ring inner at 50.5 px): left end r = 305.7/353.5 of the inner radius -> 6.8 render px short = 15.2 mm; right end -> 8.7 render px = 19.3 mm. That is 5.4-6.9 % of the 280 mm badge diameter. Source: t1_detail.py:1916-1980 (the requirement at :1938-1941, the 20 % band fit at :1962-1979). Crops: rev50_decals-type_badge_overlay.png (7x with the ring's inner and outer circles drawn), rev50_decals-type_front_badge.png, rev50_decals-type_render_badge.png (detail_f, same notches), rev50_decals-type_workshop_badge.png (ref_workshop.jpg (255,490)-(356,608) at 10x — all six ends fused into the band, zero gap), rev50_decals-type_nolita_badge.png (ref_nolita_front34.jpg, same).

**Its own refutation.** First refutation: 'this is bodywork, not decals.' It is a logotype and it is the largest piece of lettering on the vehicle, so I kept it. Second, and the one that nearly killed it: my first radial scan appeared to show gaps at all six ends, and it was WRONG — the scan radius was capped at ring_inner-1, so four ends read as 'reaching the cap' spuriously. I redid it as a drawn overlay and looked, and only the two V arms are short; the W arms and legs do reach. Third: 'the ends are hidden behind the proud ring, so they only look short.' The ring stands 2.5 mm proud and its inner silhouette is at 51.0 px; a hidden end would show ink right up to that silhouette, and the V arms show cream for 7-9 px inside it. Fourth: 'ref_workshop is the GREEN bus.' The badge is a VW part, not livery — geometry, admissible under rule 26, and it is the frame the source itself measured this from. I dropped a stroke-width claim (build bars looked ~1.7x fat) because ref_workshop is too oblique to measure a perpendicular width from.

### 35. [MAJOR] `countertan` still carries WEAR 0.7 — chipping a surface the owner already ruled CLEAN, on a ruling this file used to retire the dust, and rev 20 scheduled the ablation three revisions ago
*Materials and shading (t1_mats.py) — body paint, cream family, chrome/brightwork, rubber/tyre, glass, emissive bulbs, the WEATHER weathering/wear system, orange*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING — `T1_CTAN_WEAR=0` already exists at t1_mats.py:1980 and is one environment variable

**What is wrong.** t1_mats.py:1982 applies `apply_weather(M['countertan'], dust=1.4, wear=WEAR['countertan']=0.7, ...)`. 0.7 is the joint-highest wear weight on the vehicle (t1_mats.py:681). The owner ruling quoted at t1_mats.py:384 — '[stated, rev 28, SPEC 10.81] the COUNTER TOP is CLEAN VARNISHED PLYWOOD' — was strong enough to retire W_DUST_FAC_UP to 0.0 across eleven materials, but the wear path on the same surface was left untouched, and it is the path that actually produces the visible speckle. t1_mats.py:1973-1981 records the intent verbatim ('rev 20: `wear` is overridable for the same reason and it is the next candidate after dust was excluded ... Defaults unchanged') and exposes T1_CTAN_WEAR; it has never been exercised. Separately, `rough=0.42, coat=0.05, spec=0.32` (t1_mats.py:1806-1809) is not a varnish — every other coated material in the file gets Coat Roughness 0.030 from simple() (t1_mats.py:714-716), and a coat weight of 0.05 makes that moot.

**Evidence.** out/r49s_counter.png fascia/top band rows 640-790 cols 60-900: 22.65 % of pixels more than 6 % below their local 41-px median, 17.54 % below -10 %, 2nd percentile -36.3 %. out/r49s_hero34f.png counter top rows 560-640 cols 900-1260 shows the same crust (probe_scratch/rev50_materials_render_countertop.png). ref_rear34.jpg rows 380-480 cols 520-960 — the frame the counter-top solve was anchored on — shows a smooth uniform tan top with a clean brass nosing and a clean cream fascia below it, no speckle at any scale (probe_scratch/rev50_materials_ref_countertop.png). ref_side.jpg rows 423-433 cols 660-900, the same fascia: 0.58 % below -6 %.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter: SPEC 10.81's ruling was about DUST, and extending it to chips is my extrapolation, not the owner's word. That is exactly why the finding is 'run the ablation rev 20 already scheduled', not 'set it to zero'. Second counter: maybe the wear is what stops the plywood reading as plastic — the file's own standing objection to constant-roughness surfaces. Refuted by the photograph and by colour: ref_rear34's tan top is uniform, and the render's speckle is neutral grey (W_PRIMER / W_STEEL), not wood-coloured, so it is not standing in for grain. Third counter: this is just finding 1 restated. It is not — finding 1 needs a shader change to restore edge selectivity, this one is settled by an owner ruling already in the repository and costs nothing.

### 36. [MAJOR] The contact shadow terminates in a hard, ragged rim — the alpha floor plus the 1/9 power is a discontinuity, and SPEC 10.116's C3 control reads only the frame's upper margins so it cannot see it
*Lighting, shadow, camera and optics*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** studio.py:855-870 computes a' = clamp((a - 0.030)/0.970) then a'' = a'^(1/9). Both halves have unbounded derivative at the floor: for a = 0.030+eps, a'' = (eps/0.97)^(1/9), which jumps from 0 to ~0.43 for eps as small as 5e-4. So the 0.030 alpha contour of a Monte-Carlo-noisy catcher becomes a visible edge in the beauty frame - the shadow does not fade out, it STOPS. SPEC 10.116.5 states the cost as "it also erodes the faintest real shadow", which describes a shadow getting shorter; the actual cost is a shadow that acquires an outline. The control that was supposed to police this, C3, reads the frame's top two corners (10.116.2 item 3 says so explicitly) - it is structurally blind to a defect that lives on the ground. That is 10.116.6's own rule turned on itself. Note also that rev 45 REFUSED T1_CATCH=0 partly because it introduced "a margin whose row-to-row step goes 0.100 -> 22.123 DN"; the accepted alternative introduces a step of its own, on the ground rather than at the horizon, and it was never measured with the same instrument.

**Evidence.** Dimensionless statistic on five shipped frames: gradient in the outermost 7 px of the shadow divided by the gradient 30 px inside the same column/row (both DN/px, so exposure and shadow depth cancel). out/r49s_low34.png, 160 independent columns x=60..700 step 4: terminator 1.204+-0.328 DN/px vs interior 0.265+-0.075 DN/px -> RATIO mean 5.09, median 5.36, IQR 4.12-6.12. Re-run per view on horizontal scans: r49s_hero34f n=50 median 5.85 (IQR 4.33-6.96); r49s_front34 n=92 median 7.05; r49s_topdown n=126 median 5.36; r49s_counter n=17 median 4.29. Raw profile, out/r49s_low34.png col 140: rows 990->1018 go 238.0->246.0 (0.286 DN/px) and rows 1018->1022 go 246.0->255.0 (2.25 DN/px), then exactly 255.000 outward forever. Col 540: 239->245 over 38 rows then 245->255 over 4. Interior row-to-row null inside the pool is 2.86 DN (pure MC noise). VISUAL: probe_scratch/rev50_lighting-camera_shadowedge_raw.png (crop 60,960-760,1060 at 2x, unstretched) shows a torn-paper terminator with the naked eye; probe_scratch/rev50_lighting-camera_shadowedge_stretch.png is the same crop with 235-255 DN stretched to 0-255 and shows the noise-thresholded contour plus a warm CA fringe riding on it. What would make this reading wrong: if the true penumbra really does have its steepest gradient at its outer end - it cannot, a convolution of an extended source with an occluder asymptotes to zero gradient outward.

**Its own refutation.** Refutation 1, the one I expected to kill it: the apparent termination is 8-bit quantisation - a smooth decay simply rounds to 255. Checked and it fails: at the interior gradient of 0.265 DN/px the last 8.4 DN would take 32 px; it takes 7, and nine distinct code values are crossed in those 7 px, so it is not a rounding edge. Refutation 2: the ratio is contaminated by the vehicle or a tyre entering the window. Guarded - columns whose window minimum falls below 150/180 DN were rejected, and the interior null is reported beside the signal. Refutation 3: it is one view or one seed. Reproduced across five views with n = 17 to 160 and the median in a 4.3-7.1 band. Refutation 4: maybe the floor is innocent and only the 1/9 power is responsible. Both are - x^(1/9) also has infinite slope at 0 - which does not weaken the finding, it widens the fix: neither the floor nor an exponent that steep is admissible on a shadow that has to fade into a white sweep. The one render that separates mechanism from artefact is T1_SHADOW=1.0 T1_SHADOW_FLOOR=0 on low34: if the rim survives with the gain off, the compositor is innocent and the denoiser is the suspect.

BEZEL, ref_rear34.jpg (probe_scratch/rev50_fidelity-bar_bezel.py): detector = sat < 0.66 AND lum > 70, scanned at y = 655..705 step 5 over x 910..985. A 4-8 px run at x 923-929 appears on EVERY row from 660 to 700, plus a 2 px run at x 960-963; the lens itself reads sat 0.77-0.90 and the adjacent paint sat 0.78-0.92. Second counter: the amber material might be transmissive, so we are seeing the body through the lens. Killed by the negative control — the same detector finds nothing on 75 columns of paint at the same rows, and the runs are 4-8 px wide, not one. FOURTH, AND I DROPPED IT: I first wanted to report that the lens is a rounded-rectangle OBLONG, not a circle. My saturation/luminance mask cannot separate dark amber from dark red paint (it returned row widths of 40-54 px well outside the lens), so the shape claim failed its own control and I withdraw it — t1_detail.py:2588-2591's existing 'the tail lamp is round' control stands. Fifth: the lamp's PROUDNESS is explicitly declared unmeasured at build.py:540-545 and I have not touched it; this finding is a penetration and a missing part, not a depth.

NEXT_CONTEXT_PROMPT_rev50.md:384 and LEDGER_rev49.md:349 both print "171 objects, 0 bare materials". The row's own comment at verify_clone.sh:517-519 already records this failure mode happening once before: "the committed STATE.md on the rev-44b tip ITSELF still said 190: it was never regenerated after the cab was added, so that record was stale on its own branch." CEILING: I cannot run Blender, so 221→≥227 is an inference from counted mesh-creating calls, not a measured object count; the direction is certain, the magnitude is not.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter: verify_clone.sh says in its own comment that these rows check the RECORD and not the build, so they are doing exactly what they claim and no row is lying. I accept that as far as it goes — the rows are honest about their scope, which is why I am reporting the STALENESS as the defect and not the rows' design. But it leaves the project with eight green rows about a four-revision-old vehicle and no row anywhere that would notice, and STATE.md's own header claims a primacy those rows then propagate. Second counter: maybe audit.py IS run and STATE.md just happens to be byte-identical. Impossible — its provenance table stamps the generation timestamp and the commit hash, both of which would change. Third counter: maybe `171` and `221` count different things (all objects vs mesh objects, SUB=1 vs SUB=2) and there is no contradiction. Quite possibly — which is the point: nothing in the repo says which, so a reader cannot tell a legitimate difference of definition from a fifty-object drift.

The only thing checking it is one build-time assert inside `t1_shell.tail_board()` — the foot-clearance guard, whose first version rev 49 wrote was a tautology. Meanwhile the project HAS a guard family for exactly this: four rows of the form "X declares itself NOT MEASURED" (verify_clone.sh:395 STAR_N, :425 LOUV_APERTURE, :456 TRUNK_OPEN_DEG, :469 REAR_OPEN_DEG). It was not extended to the board. Nor is the parallax bound enforced: `W ≤ 0.590 m` appears only in a comment (t1_shell.py:1558) and a log string (:1747) — set `TB_WIDTH = 0.90`, past the only admissible bound the project has, and nothing fires. AND A WITHDRAWN NUMBER IS STILL LOAD-BEARING: `t1_shell.py:1826-1828` reads "this board stands **80 mm** clear on the roof (see `tail_board()`)" and uses it to justify not driving the stay to its measured landing. `tail_board()` computes `z0 = _seat + _hang + 0.0040` — **4.0 mm** of daylight under the lowest corner, by construction — and SPEC §10.123.2a withdraws the 80 mm outright. The figure is 20× wrong, in the very file the rev-49d commit says it swept ("withdraw the declared 80 mm across the record, not just the source").

**Evidence.** `grep -ni 'board\|NOTAILBOARD\|TB_\|bulb\|stay' verify_clone.sh` → nine hits, every one about `signboard` (the retired ground sign) or unrelated; `grep -cE '^\s*ck ' verify_clone.sh` → 116. `grep -ni 'tail_board\|tb_bulbs\|NOTAILBOARD\|tail board' verify.py` → one hit, line 484, inside `_bounds.__doc__`. `grep -rln 'tail_board\|TB_WIDTH'` across all .py/.sh → build.py and t1_shell.py only. `grep -n '0\.59' t1_shell.py verify.py verify_clone.sh` → :1558 (a comment), :1747 (a log string), and nothing in either verifier. The 80 mm: t1_shell.py:1826-1828 verbatim; against t1_shell.py:1717 `z0 = _seat + _hang + 0.0040` with `_hang = TB_T*0.5*cos(tilt)` and `_seat` read from `T1_body`'s own vertices; against SPEC.md:10288 "**§10.123.2a — THE 80 mm FOOT INCONSISTENCY IS WITHDRAWN, rev 49d**" and the LEDGER's "photographed 1.747 ± 0.027 against a skin at 1.7497 — 2.7 mm". `grep -rn '80 mm' t1_shell.py` → :1602, :1603, :1610 (inside the superseded paragraph, followed at :1620 by the *** withdrawal ***), :1742 (correctly describing it as dissolved) and :1828 (the live one). CEILING: I cannot run the build, so the 4.0 mm is read off the expression, not off a log line; if `_seat` were being computed against something other than the skin the number would change, but the guard at :1730 asserts against that same `_seat`.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter on the guards: the tail board is NOT_BODYWORK — a fixture, not sheet metal — so it is out of scope for a verifier that tests the vehicle. It fails on the project's own precedent: `LOUV_APERTURE`, `STAR_N` and the two lid angles are all guarded, and STAR_N is not even geometry, it is a decal count. The guard family is about PROVENANCE, and the board carries more unbacked provenance than any of the four. Second counter on the 80 mm: it is dead prose, so it changes nothing that renders. Partly true — I read the stay code and it does land at the measured `zb = 1.5780, xb = X_TAIL` and reports its angle residual honestly, so the operative reason for the departure is the depth-plane argument in the paragraph below, not the 80 mm. That is why I graded this half of the finding lower and did not report it separately. It still matters, because it is a withdrawn figure standing as the FIRST stated reason in a comment block a future context will read to decide whether to re-seat the stay — the exact half-retraction class the rev-49 ledger headlined. Third counter: maybe a guard names the board indirectly (by object name, by NOT_BODYWORK membership). I checked `NOT_BODYWORK` and `SWUNG` — both are consumed by `_bounds()` for EXCLUSION, which is the opposite of a check.

So the correct claim is weaker than my first one and I have downgraded it: §8.4 IS approximately recoverable once you guess both the statistic and a five-row band, and still misses one of its three published figures by 9.8 DN. Second counter: §8.2/8.3/8.5's substance may be perfectly sound even without coordinates — and it is; I confirmed the blackwall/painted-rim lock by re-deriving §8.1, and the red-on-cream roundel and cream bumper are plainly visible in probe_scratch/rev50_the-record_playa_nose.png. True, and it is why I graded this major rather than blocking. ### 46. [MAJOR] W4's FIX IS CAPPED BY THE SAME FRAME -- the crown correction cannot be 'several times 14.3 mm'
*The nose and front: nose face and plan crown (W4), V swage, VW roundel and bars, headlamps and bowls, indicators, front blade/bumper, windscreen aperture and ru*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING for the bound. Tightening it needs the far lamp's centre, which the panel's own silhouette clips in this frame -- the same grazing frame finding 1 asks for would give it directly.

**What is wrong.** LEDGER_rev47 sec.267 records that the real crown 'is unknown and may be several times the built 14.3 mm', and that has stood unbounded for three revisions. It is not unbounded. On ref_workshop.jpg the far lamp, the roundel and the near lamp form a three-point collinearity test whose residual is a direct measure of the forward crown, and it caps the crown at a few tens of mm. A correction that pushes the badge 100 mm or more forward of the headlamp chord is EXCLUDED by the photograph. This matters because the natural over-reaction to finding 1 is to multiply `bulge` by 5-10, and that would break this test.

**Evidence.** For three points equally spaced along a line, perspective gives 2*u_mid = (Z_n/Z_r)*u_near + (Z_f/Z_r)*u_far, and the relation is INDEPENDENT of the principal point cx (the cx terms cancel identically). Depth ratios from the lamps' unforeshortened vertical extents (Z_far/Z_near = d_near/d_far = 75.6/60.6 = 1.247, hence Z_n/Z_r = 0.8897, Z_f/Z_r = 1.1103). Image columns: near lamp 417 +- 2 (blob centroid 416.8), roundel 305 +- 4 (bbox centre; second-moment 296-303; 8x visual 306.9), far lamp 229 +- 5 (clipped by the panel's own silhouette, biased inboard). Residual 2*305 - (0.8897*417 + 1.1103*229) = -15.3 px; with the alternative centres (312, 419, 229) it is +1.3 px. Sensitivity d(residual)/d(crown) = 2 * (f/Z_ro) * sin(psi) = 2 * 325 * 0.751 = 488 px per metre, where f/Z_ro = 325 px/m is anchored on ROUNDEL_D = 0.280 m over the roundel's measured 91 px vertical. So crown = -31 +- 25 mm, i.e. |crown| < ~80 mm at 2 sigma. BUILT crown at the same span = bulge(0) - bulge(0.545) = 0.019*(1 - (0.545/0.80)^2) = 8.8 mm at z=1.00, 11.3 mm at z=1.25 (interpolating LEDGER_rev46 sec.198's raycast table). The built value is INSIDE the bound.

**Its own refutation.** The residual sign says the roundel sits BEHIND the lamp chord (a concave nose), which is physically impossible -- that is the tell that a systematic dominates, and I have traced it: making the far lamp's true centre 14 px further outboard than the 229 I can see zeroes the residual, and the far bore IS clipped by the silhouette in exactly that direction. So the point estimate is worthless and only the BOUND survives, which is what I report. Second check: the scale anchor. My first pass calibrated f/Z from build.py's '0.149 m' and got 381.7 px/m; re-reading build.py:572 that 0.149 is the roundel BELOW THE BELT, not above the lamp, and the correct anchor (ROUNDEL_D over 91 px) gives 325 px/m. Every metric number here uses the corrected 325.

### 47. [MAJOR] The tail lamps sit ~46 mm too high — the photograph puts the lens centre BELOW the plate's centre, the model puts it above
*THE TAIL AND REAR — tail panel, engine lid + shut line, T-handle, 1963 plate, tail lamps, rear window aperture and what shows through it, rear bumper, tail boar*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING.

**What is wrong.** build.py:563 places both lamps at z = 0.8250, an uncited literal, while PLATE_OUTER_CZ = 0.787545. That makes the lens centre 37.5 mm ABOVE the plate's outer centre. In ref_rear34.jpg the lens centre is 15 mm BELOW it. It is a sign flip, not a magnitude quibble, and it is in the same sense and of the same order as the engine-lid error — consistent with a single cause: the hardware BELOW the two-tone break sits too high on the tail. Everything else about the lamp checks out (size, lateral station, lens colour), which is why this is worth separating from the noise.

**Evidence.** FRAME: ref_rear34.jpg (RED, target). QUANTITY: geometry. Lamp outer boundary extracted as an R-B ring (the dark bezel against red paint), probe_scratch/rev50_tail-rear_REF_lamp10x.png: x 924..965, y 648..712, centre (944.5, 680), outer AR 64/41 = 1.56 — inside the 1.189..1.847 band build.py:582 requires for a round lamp between the two planes. Plate fitted lines at x=944: top 648.6, bottom 705.5, PLATE_OUTER_H = 56.9 px, outer centre 677.05. Photo: lamp centre 2.95 px BELOW the plate centre = 0.052 PLATE_OUTER_H below. Built: (0.787545-0.8250)/0.16823 = 0.2226 PLATE_OUTER_H ABOVE. Δ = 0.275 x 168.23 = 46 mm. WINDOW/CEILING: the plate lines are extrapolated 135 px beyond their fitted range (1079-1181) and their slopes are ~1.2 sigma detections (t1_detail.py:2553), so I allow +-2 px on each line -> +-3 px on the centre -> 46 +- 12 mm. Lever arm is 3 px, so no scale error can manufacture this. CROSS-CHECK against a second datum: measured from the cream/red two-tone break at the same column (break y = 532.56 at x 920-970, 50%-crossing of a 187->77 DN step), the photo puts the lens 147.4 px = 0.436 m below the break; the model puts it 0.318 m below (break z 1.1431 from out/r49s_rear.png, lamp z 0.8250) — 118 mm, same direction, and matching the lid's own 110 mm against the same datum.

**Its own refutation.** (1) 'The plate line fits are extrapolated 135 px to reach the lamp.' Yes — that is the whole ceiling, and I stated it as +-12 mm. The finding is 4 sigma against it. (2) 'The lamp sits on the corner rounding, so its projected centre could shift relative to a flat-panel feature.' A lateral shift of the corner does move the lens's apparent centre in x, but the quantity I measure is its VERTICAL centre against a horizontal ruler at the same x — surface curvature about a vertical axis does not move a feature's image height. (3) 'Maybe the plate is too low rather than the lamps too high.' Under-determined by this one measurement, which is why I carried the break cross-check: against the break, the lamp needs 118 mm and the plate only 45 mm, so the lamp is the larger offender either way. (4) I checked and DROPPED an adjacent candidate: the lens's apparent shape. The photo's lamp has a width profile fuller than an ellipse (30 px wide at 25 px above centre where an ellipse predicts 26), hinting at an obround rather than a round lamp — but at 41x64 px with JPEG blur that is a ~2 px effect and does not survive. 'ROUND (locked)' stands. (5) I also checked and DROPPED the lens colour: lens/adjacent-paint luminance ratio is 0.977 photographed against 1.153 / 1.089 rendered, and lens G/R 0.446 photographed sits between the two renders' 0.515 and 0.382. Rev 13's amber call is confirmed.

### 48. [MAJOR] The lid props stand on the OFF-side roof edge; the photographed stays run from the SHOW-side roof up across the opening, and the rev-44b lean guard forbids the photographed geometry
*The roof, lids and drip rail — roof skin/crown, roof aperture, raised mural lid (geometry, hinge, struts, junction), drip rail/gutter, bulb string, tail-board/r*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING for the side and the topology. The stay's exact foot station and diameter would want a raking frame of the roof, which PHOTOS_WANTED already asks for on other grounds.

**What is wrong.** t1_shell.py:2102-2118 puts each prop's foot at `_roof_edge_y(xs, LID_Y_HINGE)` — a walk in the −y direction from y = −0.545, i.e. the OFF-side roof edge at y ≈ −0.727 — and its tip at the free edge, y ≈ −0.806, giving a rod that stands almost vertical (3° from vertical) on the far side of the vehicle. Both RED in-service frames and the GREEN frame show a single long thin rod near each end running from the SHOW-side roof up and across the opening to the lid's free edge. The rev-44b guard at t1_shell.py:2133-2135 (`assert lean < 20.0`) would fire on that geometry, so the guard as written blocks the correct answer. This is not a reopening of the owner's rev-44b ruling: he asked for the rod to meet the board AT the edge that bears rather than "from the sides", and a raked stay landing on the free edge satisfies him; what is wrong is which side the foot is on.

**Evidence.** Two independent frames, and the cue needs no scale. ref_rear34.jpg (RED, target) — probe_scratch/rev50_roof-lids_rear34_lid4x.png (crop 780,0–1200,300 at 4×): a thin straight bright rod runs from about (870,220) up to (1132,5) in original pixels and is drawn OVER the mural, i.e. it lies between camera and board. IMG_2073.jpeg (GREEN, geometry only) — probe_scratch/rev50_roof-lids_2073_stayfoot3x.png (crop 430,0–1000,340 at 3×): the same arrangement, a long rod crossing in front of the quilted face with its foot down on the roof. A rod that crosses in front of the board cannot be standing beyond the board on the far side. Consistency with finding 1: correcting α to 61–78° puts the free edge at y ≈ −0.02…+0.02, i.e. directly over the OPEN aperture, so no prop can stand under it at all — the rev-45 guard at t1_shell.py:2141-2145 (foot must be outside the aperture's y band) would then be unsatisfiable by a vertical prop, which is itself evidence that the real member is a raked stay.

**Its own refutation.** The obvious refutation is that the rod I see is not a lid stay at all — it could be a guy line to the palapa, a wire, or the tail board's own stay. Against that: it appears in the same relation to the lid in two frames of two DIFFERENT vehicles, it terminates at the lid's corner in both, and in IMG_2073 it is plainly one of a symmetric pair, one near each end, which is the owner's own rev-37 count of "two bars propping up the art sign on either side". The second refutation is that a 3/4 view cannot recover a 3D angle — true, and I therefore claim only the SIDE (which the in-front-of-the-face occlusion settles without any angle) and not a degree figure. The third is that the owner already ruled here — checked: his words are about how the prop MEETS the board, not about where it stands, and rev 44b moved the foot on its own initiative, not on his instruction (t1_shell.py:2088-2092).

### 49. [MAJOR] LID_W = 1.110 m is too narrow: a pure ratio in ref_side.jpg needs W·sinα ≥ 1.187 m against the built 1.077 m, and the joint solve gives W = 1.40–1.49 m
*The roof, lids and drip rail — roof skin/crown, roof aperture, raised mural lid (geometry, hinge, struts, junction), drip rail/gutter, bulb string, tail-board/r*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING for the floor of W ≥ 1.19 m, which needs only the aspect ratio. Tightening the 1.40–1.49 m bracket wants a frame that pins ref_side.jpg's camera distance.

**What is wrong.** t1_shell.py:1077 `LID_W = 1.1100  # across, hinge -> free edge`, sourced at SPEC.md:4386 as "measured at 211.5 px/m". That derivation divides the board's projected image height by the FLANK's px/m, but the board stands roughly 1.2 m further from the camera than the flank, so the divisor is too large and the width comes out short. The error propagates into the roof aperture (roof_cutters expresses the hole as LID_Y_HINGE…LID_Y_HINGE+LID_W), into the strut tips and into the board's whole silhouette in every hero.

**Evidence.** ref_side.jpg (RED, target, geometry). Projected height of the yellow board rectangle: top edge fitted y = −0.0621·x + 76.25 (rms 0.35 px, n = 178 columns, window x 300–772); bottom edge taken from six clean column profiles at x = 470/520/560/600/640/690 reading y = 288/288/286/284/280/279 (the fitted bottom is unusable, rms 16.3 px, because the vendor and the roof occlude it). Height at those six stations: 240.9/244.0/244.5/245.0/243.5/245.6 → h = 244 ± 3 px. Span at mid-height ≈ 418 px. SCALE-FREE STATEMENT, no px/m and no camera model in the chain: the board's aspect is 418/244 = 1.713 photographed against 1.889 built (2.034 / (1.110·sin104°)) — so W·sinα must be L/1.713 = 1.187 m at the built L = 2.034 m, against a built W·sinα of 1.077 m, 10.2 % short, and since sinα ≤ 1 this is a hard floor of W ≥ 1.19 m. Adding the taper of finding 1 and solving jointly over camera-to-board distances of 6, 8, 10 and 13 m gives W = 1.488 / 1.419 / 1.404 / 1.430 m — remarkably insensitive to the distance — i.e. W = 1.40–1.49 m, 0.29–0.38 m wider than built.

**Its own refutation.** The fragile input is the bottom edge, which I could not fit and had to read column by column. If those six readings are systematically 5 px low the height becomes 239 and the aspect 1.749, still 7.4 % from the built figure — the sign does not turn over until the height is wrong by 28 px, which the six agreeing readings and the 10× crop (rev50_roof-lids_REF_boardbot10x.png, which shows a real thin yellow bottom border at that row) rule out. Second: the outer yellow rectangle may not be the lid's own edge — there is a cream margin outside it, exactly as `_lid_face(inset=0.030)` builds, so I compared the yellow rectangle against the DECAL's numbers where the ratio is 1.938 and against the lid's where it is 1.889, and quoted the smaller discrepancy. Third: the joint solve inherits the taper, so if finding 1 falls this bracket falls with it — which is why the scale-free floor is quoted separately and survives on its own. Fourth: applying the flank's 211.5 px/m to the board is the very error I am accusing the original of, so I did not do it — the floor uses only a ratio of two lengths in the same plane.

### 50. [MAJOR] The tyres have no contact patch: the vehicle stands ~30 mm too high on four rigid circles that touch the ground at a single point
*Wheels, tyres, hubcaps and stance*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING for the fact. A frame shot level with the wheel centre, or a raking-light contact shot, would tighten 30 ± 11 mm to a few mm.

**What is wrong.** build.py:357-360 sets every wheel centre to exactly z = TIRE_R with the comment 'a circle resting on flat ground: contact patch on z = 0, no tilt', and step 8b (build.py:870-873) deliberately exempts the wheels from the ride shear. The model's hub height above ground is therefore TIRE_R = 332.5 mm by construction, with zero tyre deflection. The photographed vehicle's hub sits at 301 ± 10 mm above the pavement on the same rim — about 30 mm of squat the model does not have. Two visible consequences: the whole vehicle floats 30 mm too high on its wheels, and each tyre meets the ground at a mathematical tangent point instead of on a flat of roughly 2·sqrt(2·R·d) ≈ 280 mm. This is precisely the 'reads as SITTING ON the ground or floating' question.

**Evidence.** DIMENSIONLESS: hub-centre height above ground / rim-flange radius. PHOTOGRAPH ref_side.jpg (RED, target, rear wheel): the rim flange fits a circle over 356 rays at centre (749.36, 604.26), R = 46.494 px, rms 0.527 px, per-octant mean residual within ±0.46 px. The sunlit paving under that wheel begins at row 668.3 ± 1.5 — raw columns x = 730/742/748/754/766 all step from L≈21 at y=667 to L≈95 at y=669. Hub height 64.0 px → ratio 1.377 ± 0.05. BUILT = TIRE_R/RIM_R = 0.3325/0.2198 = 1.5127, confirmed on the render (out/r49board_side.png hub row 872.0, ground row 962.2: 90.17/59.61 = 1.513). Difference 0.138 × 46.494 px = 6.4 px = 30.4 mm at the project's own 211.5 px/m. RENDER contact geometry, column by column: last non-background row 963 at dx=0, 961 at dx=±20, 948 at dx=±50, 917 at dx=±80 — a pure circle of R = 90.17 px, zero flat. Crops: probe_scratch/rev50_wheels-stance_ref_groundline_x2.png, rev50_wheels-stance_ref_rear_contact_darkstretch_x8.png, rev50_wheels-stance_SIDEBYSIDE_wheel.png.

**Its own refutation.** I first read this measurement as TIRE_R being 62 mm too large (tyre/rim 1.371 photographed against 1.5127 built) and would have published that. REFUTED before reporting, on ref_rear34.jpg (also the RED target), where the rear tyre's TOP is silhouetted against the arch void so the unsquashed radius is directly readable on the ellipse's major, unforeshortened axis: column x = 756-758 gives cream ring outer edges at rows 663 and 803 (centre 733, semi-major 70.0 px) and the tyre crown at row 627 (106.0 px) → 1.514 against the built 1.5127, a 0.1% match. TIRE_R/RIM_R is CORRECT and the whole deficit sits at the contact. Second refutation, aimed at the ground row itself: the bright/dark boundary at row 668 is straight to rms 0.47 px over x = 690..815 (free circle fit R = 251 px), which is a shadow-edge signature and not a tyre silhouette — so it may be the near edge of the bus's ground shadow rather than the contact line. But a shadow's near edge is CLOSER to the camera than the contact and therefore LOWER in frame, so the true contact is at or above row 668 and the squat is ≥ 30 mm, not ≤. Third: this does not disturb the arch datum — the render's arch gap measures 40.1 mm off the ortho and the photograph implies 44.6 mm, both inside SPEC §2's 41 ± 8, so the body-on-wheel relationship is right and only the wheel-on-ground relationship is wrong. Confidence is medium, not high, because the 30 mm rests on one luminance step in a region where tyre, arch shadow and ground shadow share a tone.

PHOTOGRAPHS: (1) ref_rear34.jpg, a TARGET frame -- probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_ref_rear34_hatch4x.png, 4x of box (950,80)-(1200,400): the rear window sits in an unmistakable heavy black gasket. (2) ref_nolita_front34b.jpg -- probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_ref_f34b_wsgrid.png: the windscreen pane's vertical extent is 72 px for the built pane's 0.335 m vertical projection -> 215 px/m, and the gasket along the top edge reads 7 px = 32 mm. Second counter: a T1 windscreen gasket and a rear-window gasket are different parts, so agreeing at 32 mm could be coincidence -- but they agree, and both are consistent with the stock T1 part. The MISSING-rear-seal half is not a measurement at all, it is a grep result and is not exposed to any of this: there is no seal object for the rear aperture, full stop. Report that half at high confidence and the width half at medium.

### 53. [MAJOR] Every flank rosette is drawn at the diameter of its GOLD CORE, so the flowers are half size
*Decals, signwriting and typography*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING for the diagnosis. Re-deriving d needs folk_door.md's definition of the measured blob (whether it is the gold gate or the flower outline); the flower outline can be re-measured off ref_side.jpg directly, as above.

**What is wrong.** folk_gen.py:1467 derives each rosette's diameter from the measured GOLD-BLOB area: d = 2*sqrt(area/pi). folk_gen.rosette() (lines 1179-1188) then treats that d as the flower's FULL OUTER diameter — the pearls sit at 0.845R + 0.152R, i.e. the flower's outline — and puts the gold in a disc of radius 0.400R. The photograph's flower is not a solid gold disc: it is a scalloped gold petal ring outlined in dark brown, a cream pearl ring, an orange-red disc and a pale centre, and the gold gate resolves only its core. So the equivalent-circle diameter of the gold blob is the CORE's diameter, not the flower's, and the generator draws the whole flower at that. Measured, the flower is drawn at exactly half its photographed width; the drawn gold is 0.126*d^2 against a measured 0.785*d^2, i.e. 16 % of the gold that was measured. The area is redistributed to the two big masses because REAR_GOLD is bisected to 27.86 % afterwards (folk_gen.py:1795-1826), so coverage passes and composition does not.

**Evidence.** PHOTO, ref_side.jpg, flower at (857,508) on the rear quarter. Full outer extent against a red ground of G/R 0.10-0.15: 26 +- 2 px in both axes at G/R>0.30 (printed G/R map, rows 490-527, cols 842-879), confirmed at 11x in rev50_decals-type_ref_motif_hi.png and 24x in rev50_decals-type_ref_rosette.png. Gold core at the source's own gate (G/R>0.42): two components 12x12 and 11x10, 136 px^2 combined, d_eq 13.2 px. Scale 197 +- 8 px/m (cal_gen's 194.8 rear panel / 211.5 mid-body, cross-checked against the rear tyre at 147 px for TIRE_R*2 = 0.665 m -> 221 px/m at the wheel). Flower 132 +- 12 mm, core 67 +- 6 mm. BUILD: FLANK_ROSETTES's largest is 3632 mm^2 -> d = 68 mm; the entry nearest this station (X = -1.80 from photo x 858 via cal_gen's tail datum) is -1.848 at 740 mm^2 (d = 31 mm) or -1.744 at 1838 mm^2 (d = 48 mm). RENDER corroboration: r49board_side.png at 271.19 px/m (ortho_scale 5.90 / 1600, studio.py:1359) — the rosette at render (985,748) measures 15.4 px = 57 mm (rev50_decals-type_render_rosette.png, 14x).

**Its own refutation.** The obvious refutation is 'the source's measurement is simply wrong', and I tested that and it is NOT: my own gold-gated area for the flower at X = -1.688 comes to ~3500 mm^2 against FLANK_ROSETTES's 3564 mm^2 — the AREA is right to 2 %. What is wrong is only the area -> diameter step, which is why this survives. Second refutation: 'the 26 px full extent is blur halo.' At G/R>0.30 the ground is 0.10-0.15, so the gate sits 2-3x above ground, and the 11x crop resolves the scalloped petals and the dark outline ring individually inside that 26 px — it is structure, not bloom. Third: 'the px/m is wrong.' The claim is a factor of 2; the scale would have to be wrong by 2x, and two independent anchors (cal_gen's panel measurement and TIRE_R) agree within 12 %. I DID drop a companion claim: that the photographed scrollwork is two-tone gold. At 11x the fronds are one flat lemon-gold; the 20-50 deg hue spread I measured was a lighting gradient across the flank.

### 54. [MAJOR] W6's headline G/R compares two display-referred ratios at different display levels, and that ratio is measurably level-dependent in this pipeline — 15-34 % of the published 3.5 sigma is a units artefact
*Materials and shading (t1_mats.py) — body paint, cream family, chrome/brightwork, rubber/tyre, glass, emissive bulbs, the WEATHER weathering/wear system, orange*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** The lever table at LEDGER_rev49.md:181-188 and NEXT_CONTEXT_PROMPT_rev50.md:192-199 ranks five arms by body-red G/R measured on the AgX+Punchy beauty, and compares them to a photographed 0.223 +- 0.066. No row quotes the display LEVEL it was measured at, and G/R on this asset is a strong function of that level: the same paint, same shader, same window gives 0.4605 at red R = 185 and 0.3300 at red R = 118. The photographed target sits at R = 144, 22 % below the base render's level, so the comparison is not like-for-like and every future lever will 'improve' G/R simply by darkening the red. LEDGER_rev49.md:46 already flags these figures as 'quoted with NO WINDOW ... the decimals are not reproducible'; this puts a number on how much that costs.

**Evidence.** Window: red flank rows 690-790 cols 640-940 of each 1600x1100 side render, gate saturation > 0.45 and R > G, n = 21 906-24 715 px. out/r49base_side.png RGB (185.1, 85.3, 74.6) G/R 0.4605; out/r49board_side.png (185.1, 85.2, 74.5) G/R 0.4603 (repeat, +-0.0002 — the estimator is stable); out/r49cyc30_side.png (172.0, 77.0, 66.9) G/R 0.4475; out/r49soft35_side.png (118.3, 39.0, 29.3) G/R 0.3300. ref_side.jpg rows 470-545 cols 600-700, same gate, n = 7313: (144.4, 27.0, 8.5) G/R 0.1871. Slopes d(G/R)/dR: 0.00219 per code value (base->soften), 0.00099 (base->cyc30). Extrapolating the base render to the reference's R = 144.4 gives G/R 0.381-0.420 against 0.187, so 0.040-0.080 of the published 0.455 - 0.223 = 0.232 excess (17-34 %) is level rather than chroma. Backgrounds are 255 in all four render arms, so this is not a background-composite effect.

**Its own refutation.** The strongest counter is decisive against the causal half and I am dropping that half: removing a genuine achromatic veil k lowers BOTH the level and the ratio, since (G-k)/(R-k) < G/R whenever R > G. So level-and-ratio co-movement is equally predicted by the veil hypothesis and my three arms cannot discriminate. I therefore make NO claim that the veil diagnosis is wrong, and I do not reopen the DEAD verdicts on cyclorama albedo or the short-axis softbox. What survives is narrower and still real: a display-referred ratio compared between two images at different levels is not a like-for-like comparison; the level-matched residual is 0.381-0.420 against 0.187, not 0.455 against 0.223; and a table that ranks levers by an exposure-sensitive statistic without recording the exposure will keep rewarding arms for darkening. Second counter: my slopes come from three points and the two arms disagree by 2.2x, so I quote a range, not a correction. Third counter: T1_SOFTEN changes the light's shape as well as its level, so the 0.00219 slope is an upper bound; that is why the range's lower end uses the cyclorama arm.

### 55. [MAJOR] W6's P1 is measured in display code values through AgX-Punchy, where the cream normalisation does NOT cancel exposure — and both levers already scored move P1 by moving the level. The same-frame control that separates them is unused: the tail face reads 1.46 sigma while the flank reads 3.49 sigma.
*Lighting, shadow, camera and optics*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** probe_rev45_paint.py:91 is `img = np.array(Image.open(PNG).convert("RGB")).astype(float)` - a display-referred 8-bit PNG that has been through AgX + Punchy. Its header calls the cream normalisation "a von-Kries normalisation [that] cancels BOTH the exposure and the illuminant's colour". A von-Kries normalisation does that in a LINEAR space. Through a per-channel S-curve with a chroma inset it does not, and the render and the photographs are read at very different points on their respective curves: the render's plain red sits at red/cream luminance 0.61, ref_side.jpg's at 0.30. studio.py's W6 comment asserts the same invariance ("scaling every light together (T1_KEY) scales a_R.E and A alike and moves it by exactly nothing") and cites as proof that T1_W_FADESAT and T1_MOT_AMP are bit-identical - which are not exposure knobs, so the claim is proved for scene-linear and only asserted for the quantity the probe actually reads. The consequence is structural: BOTH arms rev 49 scored also darken the frame, so the ablation cannot separate "the veil went away" from "the red got darker". That matters because the surviving recommendation - replace the directional rig with a diffuse dome - is the largest look change on the table.

**Evidence.** Independent instrument using probe_rev45_paint's OWN classifiers (is_red_paint: (R-G) > 0.25*max(R,1) and R>40; is_cream: (max-min) < 0.10*max and 90<mean<249) on fixed pixel bands of the three side arms in out/ (red band rows 600-860, cream band rows 470-620, both cols 400-1400), medians: r49base_side red L 116.00 cream L 190.00 red/cream 0.6105 P1 0.4474 (published 0.455); r49cyc30_side red L 105.33 cream L 189.00 red/cream 0.5573 P1 0.4110 (published "2-5%, dead lever"; I read -8%); r49soft35_side red L 63.67 cream L 142.67 red/cream 0.4463 P1 0.3275 (published 0.351). P1 is monotone in the red's display level across all three arms. studio.py's own _softbox comment declares the level move for the winning arm: "at k=3.5 the cream falls to 0.706 of base and the red flank to 0.545". PHOTOGRAPH (ref_side.jpg, RED target, current artwork - the right frame for paint): plain red window (636,470)-(690,505) R 124.89 G 19.87 B 6.18 L 50.31 against the cream band (470,420)-(560,438) L 167.19 -> red/cream 0.301, half the render's operating point. THE UNUSED CONTROL, out/r49shut2_hero34r.png, same material, same frame, same exposure: three flank windows (520,655,575,695)/(706,684,760,740)/(600,750,660,785) and two tail windows (1020,700,1110,745)/(1030,790,1180,830), all visually verified clear of gold, script and lamps (probe_scratch/rev50_lighting-camera_grwindows2.png) - flank median G/R 0.4536 (z = +3.49 sigma against 0.223+-0.066), tail median G/R 0.3193 (z = +1.46 sigma), a 30% chromaticity swing between two faces of one paint. In ref_side.jpg the same paint holds G/R 0.153/0.144/0.140/0.154 across L bins 17-39, 39-47, 47-56, 56-65 - flat to +-7% over a 4x luminance range. SETTLE IT WITH ONE RENDER: `side` at T1_EXP ~= -1.0, nothing else changed, re-measured with probe_rev45_paint. Nothing physical moves, so any P1 movement is the display transform, and it prices the level component of the 3.5 sigma exactly.

**Its own refutation.** Refutation 1, the serious one: the flank-tail swing could be AgX's own highlight desaturation rather than a veil, since the flank is at L 110-128 and the tail at L 58 and AgX desaturates upward - the confound points the same way as the accusation. I could not settle it (PyOpenColorIO is not installed, so I could not apply AgX_Base_sRGB.cube directly), which is exactly why the finding is framed as "the instrument is confounded" and not as "the veil is 0.134". Refutation 2: maybe the tail is a different material or fade zone. The windows sit on the plain engine-lid red between the two tail lamps and on the lower lid, both plain body paint; if they were a different material the R channel would differ in kind, and it is 114.06 / 113.05 against the flank's 181-188 - a pure level difference, not a hue one. Refutation 3: maybe I mis-implemented P1. My flank reading of 0.4474-0.4536 reproduces the published 0.455 on two different views with two different window schemes, and my soften/cyc arms reproduce 0.351 and "2-5%", so the instrument agrees with theirs where theirs exists. Refutation 4: three points is not a correlation. Agreed, and the finding does not rest on it: the claim is that the EXPERIMENT DESIGN is collinear, which is true whatever the correlation turns out to be, because no arm in the five-arm ablation holds the level fixed.
