**confidence** medium · **effort** large · **blocked on** NOTHING for category (c). Categories (a) and (b) are recorded, not requested — he answered at rev 49.

**What is wrong.** This is the deliverable, not a defect. (a) BLOCKED ON A PHOTOGRAPH — the owner said at rev 49 "Neither is possible right now", so these are recorded, not requested: the tail board's WIDTH, lateral centring, thickness and fore-aft depth plane (TB_WIDTH 0.5500 / TB_Y_CENTRE 0.0000 / TB_T 0.0220, t1_shell.py:1546-1567; parallax gives W ≤ 0.590 m with NO lower bound); the decal's word gap, star count and burst colour (LINE_GAP 0.43 "TRANSFERRED, not measured on the target" cal_gen.py:168; STAR_N 7 plus STAR_BAND_X/Y and STAR_R, cal_gen.py:430-440); the louvre PRESSING DEPTH (LOUV_APERTURE 0.0070 INFERRED, plus LOUV_SECT / LOUV_PROFILE / LOUV_OFF 0.0020 / LOUV_BAY_D 0.070 NOT MEASURED, t1_detail.py:2189-2199); the headlamp bowl's depth and section (52 mm straight bore, "AUTHORED, NOT MEASURED", t1_shell.py:438); B_PILLAR 0.0120 ("TRUE WIDTH IS OPEN AND UNMEASURED", t1_shell.py:535-546); the cab-door vent's true top edge (VENT_TOP_DROP, t1_shell.py:83); the absolute roof height (verify.py:83, 762); X_NOSE, "never been measured", occluded by a lamppost at ref_side.jpg cols 62-79 (verify.py:43, 768); BAY_DEPTH 0.42 and the trunk bay's contents (t1_shell.py:1430) — now permanently unseeable, since he shut the lid; the whole cab interior (COL_MID/COL_AX/COL_LEN/COL_R rev-8 authored and UNMEASURED, t1_detail.py:1353-1357; FLOOR_W 1.200 and WH_R/T/Y_IN/INSET/SWEEP AUTHORED and absent from SPEC and REF_MEASUREMENTS, :1209-1214); FLAP_X ("on the side neither frame covers", :2322), the bulb ball diameter and pitch (:2340), the off-side lamps (:2421); the wiper set (COWL_PIVOT/COWL_N/WIPE_LEN/WIPE_STANDOFF/WIPE_PARK_DEG 20.0, all INFERRED, :1770-1774); HINGE_BARREL_R/HINGE_H/HINGE_PROUD (authored, :1554-1556); and the ENTIRE OFF SIDE — 804.9 mm graded E, plus a possible second stay that "would be invisible in every frame we hold" (t1_shell.py:1821). (b) BLOCKED ON AN OWNER RULING — W6's re-light, which must be PUT AGAIN with k=1.0 and k=3.5 frames side by side and the 29 % cream cost stated, because he chose "re-light to match your photographs" before it was known the only working lever replaces the rig with a dome; REAR_OPEN_DEG 64.0 (t1_shell.py:1401) — he ruled WHICH bay, never HOW FAR; whether the rear PANE is the hinged part at all (t1_shell.py:1393-1399, "HIS INSTRUCTION applied to the part that occupies that station, not a reading of a photograph"); the die-cut sticker and the Playa hero's emotional bar (finding 3); and whether PLATE_W 0.3300 is a catalogue figure, which SPEC §10.48 says would collide with his standing instruction never to correct this vehicle toward the factory catalogue. (c) MEASURABLE FROM FRAMES WE ALREADY HOLD, NEVER DONE — re-derive SPEC §8.1-§8.5's five ⚠ locks on ref_playa_34.png (§8.1 done in this pass) and so close ledger finding 21, open seven revisions and mis-recorded as owner-blocked; give §8.2/8.3/8.4/8.5 their windows; run W4's Method 2, the silhouette corner-wrap on ref_workshop.jpg, which PHOTOS_WANTED_rev49 §3 calls "still the live one" and "IT HAS NEVER BEEN RUN", together with the rev-49 survey's headlamp-aspect handle on that same frame; run the ten subsystem surveys that did not return; regenerate STATE.md; add the four missing declaration rows and enforce the ≤ 0.590 m bound; re-publish and burn down the 21 abandoned register rows; and land the three source-level retractions in findings 5, 7 and 9.

**Evidence.** Every constant above was located by a scan of module-level assignments in the ten build sources against the marker phrases NOT MEASURED / POSE CHOICE / INFERRED / UNMEASURED / AUTHORED / not measured / chosen, not measured, with the matching comment line printed for each hit and each entry then read by hand (probe_scratch/rev50_the-record_decl.py; 111 raw hits, hand-filtered to the ~40 that are genuine self-declarations rather than neighbours of somebody else's paragraph — the scan's false-positive rate is high, e.g. it flags TB_CHORD, which is MEASURED ±0.028, because TB_T's comment sits above it). Guard coverage was measured by testing each surviving name against the text of verify_clone.sh: four constants have a "declares itself" row (STAR_N, LOUV_APERTURE, TRUNK_OPEN_DEG, REAR_OPEN_DEG); none of the rest does. Category (a)'s photograph list is cross-checked against PHOTOS_WANTED_rev49.md's five items so this register is a superset of it and not a competing one. CEILING: this register is built from what the sources DECLARE. A constant that is unmeasured and says nothing is invisible to it — PLATE_W was found only because SPEC §10.48 named it in prose, and there is no way to know how many more of that kind exist without deriving every constant independently.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter: a register is not a finding, and restating PHOTOS_WANTED_rev49 is exactly what the brief told me not to do. Two answers. It is not a restatement — PHOTOS_WANTED lists five photographs; this lists ~35 named constants and the specific quantity each one leaves open, which is the level at which the next revision actually works, and it separates them by what BLOCKS them, which no existing document does. And it changes the classification of two items: ledger finding 21 moves from (b) "needs the owner" to (c) "do it today", and W4 moves from purely (a) to partly (c). Second counter: my (c) list could be optimistic — "measurable from frames we hold" is easy to assert and hard to honour, and this project's history is of methods that produce a plausible number and fail their own calibration. That is why (c) leads with the two items where I have already done part of the work and printed the numbers (§8.1 re-derived, §8.4's window quantified) rather than with the ones I have only argued for. Third counter: the confidence is medium and should be, because the register's completeness rests on a text scan whose ceiling is stated above.

### 58. [MINOR] `def gutter()` is defined twice in t1_detail.py; the first definition is dead and still carries the drip-rail constant rev 16 retired
*The roof, lids and drip rail — roof skin/crown, roof aperture, raised mural lid (geometry, hinge, struts, junction), drip rail/gutter, bulb string, tail-board/r*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING.

**What is wrong.** t1_detail.py:944 and t1_detail.py:1717 both define a module-level `gutter()`. Python keeps the later one, so build.py:403 `A(D.gutter(), "paint")` calls the rev-16 version and the earlier one is unreachable. The dead copy is not a harmless stub: it still holds `z = zt - rt * 0.72` — the exact form the live version's comment says "would drag the drip rail 28 mm up the new roll" — and it sweeps only x 0.442…1.806 off ZT_CAB/RT_CAB, i.e. the cab, about 2.1 m short of the live run from T._aft(-1.880). Anyone who greps for the gutter, finds line 944 and edits it changes nothing while believing the drip rail moved; anyone who deletes the second one silently reverts the rev-16 refit and takes the bulb string with it, since t1_detail.bulb_string:2452 hangs off the same roll start.

**Evidence.** `grep -n '^def gutter' t1_detail.py` → 944, 1717. `grep -n '^def ' t1_detail.py | awk -F'[ (]' '{print $2}' | sort | uniq -d` → `gutter` and nothing else; the same check on t1_shell.py and t1_core.py returns nothing at all. So this is a single accident, not a house pattern where a later block deliberately supersedes an earlier one. The only caller is build.py:403.

**Its own refutation.** The obvious refutation is that this is cosmetic and changes no pixel today — correct, and it is filed as minor for that reason. The reason it is filed at all is that this repository's recorded failures are exactly this shape: the canvas ragtop shipping for three revisions after §0.2 retired it, and cal_gen.py:385 still asserting "the model has NO REAR VENTS" two revisions after rev 48 cut them. I also checked that the dead copy is not referenced by name anywhere (no `gutter_cab`, no aliasing), so deleting it cannot break a caller.

### 59. [MINOR] Six of the ten glass panes are force-flat-shaded but their faces tumble home 37.8 mm over the aperture height
*Glazing, seals, mirrors and wipers*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** build.py:45 `_FLAT_SHADED = ("glass_",)` and build.py:53 call `shade_flat()` on every object whose name starts with glass_. That is right for `glass_ws+/-1` and `glass_rear`, which are `solid_prism` slabs and genuinely planar. It is wrong for `glass_dm+/-1`, `glass_dv+/-1` and the three `glass_bay*` panes, which are built by `T.conform_solid` (t1_core.py:1160) and follow the flank: each face is a single 56- to 88-gon whose vertices lie on the curved skin. Flat shading a non-planar n-gon gives every triangle of its fan its own constant normal, so a 0.022-roughness pane's reflection breaks into facets instead of sweeping.

**Evidence.** Source: build.py:28-53 (the rev-14 note and _FLAT_SHADED), t1_shell.py:189-209 `side_glass()` -> `T.conform_solid`, t1_core.py:1160-1192 (single n-gon per face, `faces.append(tuple(range(n-1,-1,-1)))`). Curvature computed from the source LUTs with no Blender: flank_y = WX(x)*G(z), t1_core.py:1052; WX is flat at 0.8750 over x in [-1.700, 1.760] (t1_core.py:490) which covers the door and all three bays, so the whole variation is G(z) (t1_core.py:214). G(1.372) = 0.98742 -> y = 0.86399; G(1.775) = 0.94419 -> y = 0.82616. Delta = 37.8 mm over the 403 mm aperture height, and the local slope dy/dz goes from -0.046 near the sill to -0.164 near the head, i.e. the face normal swings 6.7 deg from bottom to top.

**Its own refutation.** Counter: rev 14 measured that forcing flat shading changed 88.7 % of pane pixels at mean |delta| 39.18 against a 4.19 null, so flat shading was a large improvement and reverting it would regress. Agreed -- and that is exactly why the fix is not 'revert'. The rev-14 defect was smooth shading averaging the face normal with the 90 deg rim normals; the correct treatment is smooth shading with the perimeter edges marked sharp, which fixes the rim without flattening a 6.7 deg swing. Second counter: is 6.7 deg visible? On the show side today it is not, because finding 2 leaves the panes with nothing to reflect; it will become visible the moment the set is fixed. That is why this is minor and should be done in the same pass as finding 2, not before it.

### 60. [MINOR] TWO DEAD BUILDERS IN t1_detail.py — a belt moulding nothing calls, and a gutter() silently shadowed by a second gutter() 773 lines later
*THE FIDELITY BAR AUDIT — painted-on / flat-sweep features vs real geometry with depth, across t1_detail.py, t1_shell.py, texgen.py and tex/, ranked by how visib*  
**confidence** high · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** t1_detail.moulding(z=1.372) (t1_detail.py:962-976) sweeps a 9 x 7 mm belt trim strip along the cab flank. `grep -rn moulding *.py` returns exactly one line — its own definition. It is referenced by nothing. That happens to be CORRECT (neither photographed vehicle carries a trim strip at the window-sill line) but it is a loaded gun: a future revision that 'wires up the unused moulding' adds a feature both photographs refute. Separately, t1_detail.gutter() is defined twice — at :944 and again at :1717 — and Python keeps the second, which is the one build.py:403 gets. The dead first copy still carries the pre-rev-16 form `z = zt - rt*0.72`, which the live copy's own comment (t1_detail.py:1725-1729) says 'would drag the drip rail 28 mm up the new roll'. Anyone who reads the file top-down reads the retired formula as the live one.

**Evidence.** t1_detail.py:962 `def moulding(z=1.372)`; `grep -rn moulding *.py` -> one hit, t1_detail.py:962. t1_detail.py:944 `def gutter()` and t1_detail.py:1717 `def gutter()`; build.py:403 `A(D.gutter(), "paint")` binds the later one. The two bodies differ only in their aft extent (0.442 -> T._aft(-1.880)) and in the roll-start expression, and t1_detail.py:1725-1729 documents why the earlier form is wrong.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter: this is source hygiene, not a fidelity defect, and reporting it inflates the list. I keep it because both instances are the exact failure shape rule 15 and the rev-49 ledger's 'a retraction that lands in a ledger and not in the source is half a retraction' were written about — a retired formula still sitting in the file where a reader will find it first. Second counter: perhaps moulding() SHOULD be called and its absence is the defect. I checked and it is not — neither ref_side.jpg nor IMG_2073 shows a trim strip at the window-sill line on the cab flank; both show plain painted metal between the glass and the two-tone break. So the correct action is to delete it with a note, not to call it.

### 61. [MINOR] The louvre aperture is a rectangle with a 1.1 px corner radius and no sheet return — it reads as a cut, not as a pressing, against the owner's own fidelity bar
*The flank, belt line and louvres — side elevation of the RED target bus (ref_side.jpg / ref_rear34.jpg), with geometry-only corroboration from the GREEN bus (IM*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING for the return itself; a raking-light frame settles how much depth to give it, and that is the same frame as PHOTOS_WANTED_rev48 item 3.

**What is wrong.** t1_detail.py:2213 builds the aperture outline as `T.rrect(x1 - x0, z1 - z0, 0.004, seg=4)` — a 4 mm corner radius, which is 1.1 px at the side camera's 271.19 px/m and 0.9 px at ref_side.jpg's 217 px/m. In the render the block's forward boundary is a dead vertical with the blade ends terminating flush on it, and there is no visible thickness, return or rolled edge anywhere round the opening. bus_model_ref.JPG is in this project specifically as the bar for "small features built as REAL GEOMETRY WITH DEPTH that self-shadows", and its own nose louvres are modelled slots with returns. This is the one place on the flank where the model cuts the shell and the cut has no edge.

**Evidence.** t1_detail.py:2207-2216 (louvre_cutters, rrect radius 0.004, blades inset 4 mm in x so their ends land on solid panel), probe_scratch/rev50_flank-belt_r49_louv_corner_x20.png (20x nearest-neighbour, rows 665-700 x cols 1125-1175: the left boundary is a single-pixel vertical, no radius, no return, blades stopping flush). Reference for comparison: probe_scratch/rev50_flank-belt_2073_block_x10.png (GREEN bus at 10x — the block outline is rectangular, which is correct and is NOT what I am objecting to; what differs is that each real slot closes at its own end into pressed metal rather than terminating on a cut line).

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter-argument: at 1600 x 1100 a 4 mm radius is one pixel, so nothing is visible and this is invisible pedantry. Partly true — which is why it is filed minor and not major. It will show in a hero at 4800 px, where 4 mm is 3.3 px and the missing edge return is 3-4 px of a feature that should self-shadow. Second counter-argument: the blades are inset 4 mm in x so the aperture's vertical edges are covered by blade ends and never seen — correct for the ends, and it is the TOP and BOTTOM edges (2 mm of pure hole, t1_detail.py:2208-2209) that read as a cut line, which is the same 2 mm margin the previous finding is about. Third counter-argument: I originally wanted to report the bright grey speckle inside the render's slots as a louvre defect. I killed it — the fraction of pixels with G/R > 0.50 is 0.351 inside the block against 0.283, 0.252 and 0.314 on three plain flank panels in the same frame, so it is the flank's general paint-wear texture and not a louvre problem.

### 62. [MINOR] THE V SWAGE'S SECTION IS AUTHORED AND ANCHORED NOWHERE -- and it shares its photograph with the louvres
*The nose and front: nose face and plan crown (W4), V swage, VW roundel and bars, headlamps and bowls, indicators, front blade/bumper, windscreen aperture and ru*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** medium · **blocked on** A RAKING-LIGHT FRAME OF THE NOSE. This is the SAME request as PHOTOS_WANTED_rev48 item 3, already open for the louvre pressing depth -- one frame closes two items, and that is worth saying in the photo list.

**What is wrong.** The pressed V swage is built as a 6.2 mm displacement along the vertex normal, blended by a tanh of scale 16 mm. Its maximum face angle is therefore 11.0 deg, spread over ~32 mm of panel. Nothing in the repo measures either number: not the 6.2 mm depth, not the 16 mm width. A real T1 V swage is a tight crease with a small fillet, and its width and depth are exactly the kind of quantity the owner's fidelity bar (bus_model_ref.JPG: features built as real geometry with depth that self-shadows) is about. Both photographed vehicles show a distinct tonal line riding the paint break, so the feature is real -- only its section is invented.

**Evidence.** t1_shell.py:2246-2249: `d = z - zV(y); s = 0.5 * (1.0 + math.tanh(d / 0.016)); step = -0.0062 * w * (1.0 - s); v.co = ... + v.normal * step`. Max |d(step)/dd| = 0.0062 / (2*0.016) = 0.194 -> 11.0 deg. Note the shell is SUBDIVIDED and applied at build.py:227-234 BEFORE `S.nose_shape(body)` at build.py:235, so the tanh is resolved at the dense vertex spacing and 11.0 deg is what actually ships. Photographic correlate that the crease is real: probe_scratch/rev50_nose-front_nol_vswage10x.png (RED, nolita) shows a continuous bright line hugging the break; probe_scratch/rev50_nose-front_wk_vswage9x.png (GREEN) shows a continuous dark line in the same place under overhead fluorescent.

**Its own refutation.** I FIRST WROTE THIS UP AS 'THE SWAGE IS TOO SHALLOW' AND THEN DROPPED THAT READING. Perpendicular luminance profiles on the red side of the break (probe_scratch/rev50_vswage_profile.py) give: ref_nolita_front34 +104% at d=1-2 px, +36% at d=3, -8% at d=4-8; out/r49s_hero34f +11% at d=1, +4.0% peak over d=3-10. That looks damning until the third arm is run: ref_workshop.jpg, whose flat overhead fluorescent is the closest lighting analogue to the white studio, gives only -2.5%. Render 4.0% against workshop 2.5% is AGREEMENT between comparable lighting regimes; the +104% is directional street light and is rule 7(d), not a defect. So the amplitude comparison proves nothing and only the un-anchored source constants survive.

### 63. [MINOR] THE HEADLAMP BORE IS CUT AT THE LENS RADIUS, SO THE BORE RIM -- NOT THE BEZEL -- IS WHAT SILHOUETTES
*The nose and front: nose face and plan crown (W4), V swage, VW roundel and bars, headlamps and bowls, indicators, front blade/bumper, windscreen aperture and ru*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING. Both numbers are in the repo and disagree with each other: the bore is cut at the LENS radius while build.py's own aperture chain says the APERTURE is 200 mm.

**What is wrong.** `headlamp_recess_cutters` bores at `r_lens = 0.0862` (172.4 mm), but the bezel `hl_ring` has its MAXIMUM radius R+0.0165 = 0.1027 (205.4 mm) at its REARMOST profile point, local x = -0.004, which lands at world x = 2.0975 -- 14 mm BEHIND the nose skin, which probe_rev45_nose raycast at 2.1116. The widest ring of the assembly is therefore buried and the outline the camera actually gets is a near-planar circle sitting in the panel, at whatever radius the ring has where it crosses the skin (~0.0985). That is precisely why the rendered lamp can only ever project as the badge's own ellipse. Separately, the bore itself is undersized: the aperture in ref_workshop measures ~0.197 m, and build.py's own cited chain gives 0.280/1.384 = 0.2023 m, against the 0.1724 m bored.

**Evidence.** t1_shell.py:466-475 -- `prof` built at `r_lens` (default 0.0862), `T.solid_prism((hl_x + 0.010, s*hl_y, hl_z), (0,s,0), (0,0,1), (-1,0,0), prof, 0.052)`. t1_detail.py:853-859 `ring_prof` -- max radius R+0.0165 at x=-0.004; ring placed at HL_X = 2.1015 (build.py:222), so its widest ring is at 2.0975. Nose skin at that station 2.1116 (t1_shell.py:427, probe_rev45's own raycast). Aperture measured in ref_workshop: 72 +- 4 px at a local scale of 365 px/m (anchored on ROUNDEL_D 0.280 over the roundel's 91 px, depth-corrected) = 0.197 +- 0.015 m. build.py:576 states D_roundel/D_aperture = 1.384 off the same frame, which with ROUNDEL_D = 0.2800 gives 0.2023 m -- and that figure is the project's own.

**Its own refutation.** Does the bore radius matter if the bezel covers it? The bezel OD 0.2054 does overhang the 0.1724 bore, so the bore is invisible where it is covered -- which is exactly the point: the assembly's outline collapses to the panel line and there is nothing proud to self-shadow. The reading that the aperture is ~0.20 m rests on ROUNDEL_D = 0.280 m, which was itself derived from this frame; if ROUNDEL_D is wrong every metric figure here scales with it. The RATIO statement (bore cut at the lens radius, ring's girth buried behind the skin) is scale-free and does not.

### 64. [MINOR] THE INDICATOR'S MOUNTING STATION IS A BARE LITERAL, TYPED TWICE, NOT TIED TO THE NOSE SURFACE
*The nose and front: nose face and plan crown (W4), V swage, VW roundel and bars, headlamps and bowls, indicators, front blade/bumper, windscreen aperture and ru*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING for the untethered literal. The exact skin x at (y=0.675, z=1.139) needs a raycast, which I could not run (no bpy this pass), so the '16 mm buried' figure is inferred from the one raycast the repo already publishes rather than measured.

**What is wrong.** IND_Y and IND_Z are both derived from the lamp (`IND_Y = HL_Y + IND_DY`, `IND_Z = HL_Z + IND_DZ`, build.py:452-455) precisely so they survive the open question about the lamp's absolute height. The X station is not: it is the raw literal `2.0960`, written out twice, with no comment and no relation to the panel it mounts on. This is SPEC 10.110's and 10.115's own defect class -- a part authored in its own local frame and never checked against the panel it is fitted to -- and it is the one axis in this assembly that nobody ever derived. Consequence: the plinth's widest ring (r = 0.0395 at local x = 0, i.e. world 2.0960) sits ~16 mm inside the skin, so only ~6.5 mm of the 22.5 mm plinth emerges and it emerges at r ~0.033-0.035, no wider than the lens's own maximum r = 0.0348. In every photograph there is a distinct chrome collar visibly WIDER than the amber lens.

**Evidence.** build.py:520-521 `D.place(ibase, loc=(2.0960, s * IND_Y, IND_Z))` / `D.place(ilens, loc=(2.0960, s * IND_Y, IND_Z))`. t1_detail.py:899-903 plinth profile runs r 0.0395 -> 0.0330 over local x 0 -> 0.0225; t1_detail.py:912-915 lens profile peaks at r 0.0348 at local x 0.042. Nose skin at the near lamp (y=0.545, z=0.933) is 2.1116; the indicator is at y=0.675, z=1.139, so its skin station is of that order. Photographic collar: probe_scratch/rev50_nose-front_nol_ind_grid2.png (dark collar and cast shadow round the pod); probe_scratch/rev50_nose-front_wk_indic8x.png (GREEN bus, pod removed, bare 27x29 px mounting hole with its fitting).

**Its own refutation.** SPEC 10.24 is already OPEN on the pod's depth and rev 10 explicitly reverted a deepening, so this could be re-litigation. It is not the same item: 10.24 is about the pod's LENS DEPTH; this is about its MOUNTING STATION being a literal rather than a derivation, which is the fix rev 10's own note said it wanted ('the fix is the pod's MOUNTING STATION, not its depth'). I also checked the obvious alternative -- that 2.0960 is derived somewhere else and only inlined here -- by grepping build.py for the value: it appears nowhere else and carries no comment.

### 65. [MINOR] The engine lid carries a second pressed crease ~59 mm below its upper shut line that the model does not build
*THE TAIL AND REAR — tail panel, engine lid + shut line, T-handle, 1963 plate, tail lamps, rear window aperture and what shows through it, rear bumper, tail boar*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING to establish that a second line exists and where it is. Its DEPTH is blocked on the raking-light frame already on the wanted list for the louvre pressing depth — the same photograph would serve both.

**What is wrong.** ref_rear34.jpg shows TWO parallel horizontal lines across the top of the engine lid, 20.5 px apart, both starting at the lid's show-side edge at x_img ~985. The model cuts one 5.5 mm gap_prism and nothing else — engine_lid_gap() (t1_shell.py:994) returns a single rounded-rect slot and there is no swage, rib or lip anywhere on the lid. This is exactly the class of feature bus_model_ref.JPG was supplied as the bar for: real pressed depth that self-shadows, not a painted line.

**Evidence.** FRAME: ref_rear34.jpg (RED, target). QUANTITY: geometry. 70-column luma mean over x 1000-1070 (probe_scratch/rev50_tail-rear_REF_lid_grid.png): two clean minima at y = 575.0 (65->48 DN) and y = 595.5 (57->46 DN), separated by 20.5 px. At the plate-derived 346.5 px/m for that column band, 20.5 px = 59 mm. The same feature is independently on the record already, unattributed: t1_detail.py:2556-2558 measures 'the two long body grooves at y ~ 575 and ~ 595' and uses them only to argue the rear panel is curved. RENDER out/r49s_rear.png over the same panel shows exactly one seam, at y 736.6, with plain red above and below it (probe_scratch/rev50_tail-rear_REN_lid_grid.png).

**Its own refutation.** (1) 'One of the two lines IS the shut line and the other is the body above the lid, so the model is only missing a body crease, not a lid crease.' Correct that I cannot say which; incorrect that it changes the outcome — the model has one line where the vehicle has two, whichever panel the second belongs to. (2) 'A concentric rounded-rect rib would be inset at the left edge too, and both lines start at x~985.' That is why I describe it as a horizontal crease running across, terminating at the shut line, rather than an inset rib — the pixels rule out concentricity. (3) 'JPEG ringing around a single strong edge can produce a phantom parallel line.' Ringing would sit within 2-3 px of the edge and be an overshoot (bright), not a 17-DN dark minimum 20 px away, and the same instrument reads only ONE minimum at the lid's lower seam.

### 66. [MINOR] No tyre valve stem anywhere on the model, though one is plainly visible on the target vehicle's rear wheel
*Wheels, tyres, hubcaps and stance*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING for existence and rough size. Its clock angle on each wheel is arbitrary and unmeasurable — pick one.

**What is wrong.** grep for 'valve' across t1_detail.py, t1_core.py, t1_shell.py and build.py returns nothing. The wheel assembly is tyre + rim barrel + rim disc + hubcap + emblem + ring, and no valve. On the photographed vehicle a valve stem projects through the rim and stands proud of the cream ring at about 1 o'clock on the rear wheel, overlapping the hubcap's edge — a small, self-shadowing piece of real hardware exactly in the class rule 3's fidelity bar names.

**Evidence.** PHOTOGRAPH ref_side.jpg (RED, target), crop (762,568)-(796,600) autocontrast at x14: probe_scratch/rev50_wheels-stance_ref_valve_x14.png shows a dark stub with a bend, about 6 × 3 px in the original ≈ 28 × 14 mm at 211.5 px/m, emerging from the brightest part of the cream ring and overlying the red dome's edge. The identical feature, unambiguous at higher resolution, appears on IMG_2073's front wheel (green bus, geometry) at the bottom of the white rim ring. Render: no such object at any clock position on any of the four wheels (probe_scratch/rev50_wheels-stance_rend_hubcap_x7.png, rev50_wheels-stance_rend_h34r_rearwheel_x5.png).

**Its own refutation.** Strongest argument against: at 6 px it could be dirt, a paint chip, or a JPEG artefact, and one blob is not a measurement. It survives on three counts. It has consistent shape (a stub with a bend, not a blob) and it is dark against the brightest region on the wheel, where JPEG artefacts are least likely. There is exactly ONE such object anywhere on that ring, at the position a valve occupies, and none elsewhere. And the same feature appears on a second vehicle's wheel in a much sharper frame. I have deliberately NOT claimed its clock angle or exact length — only that a valve exists and the model has none.

### 67. [MINOR] TIRE_W declares a 215-section tyre but the mesh delivers a 150 mm section, and nothing measures either
*Wheels, tyres, hubcaps and stance*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** A square-on front or rear ELEVATION photograph of the RED bus with both tyres visible against the flank. Without it section width and track cannot be separated from each other.

**What is wrong.** t1_core.py:81 reads `TIRE_W = 0.1550  # rev6: ~215 section on a 16in rim`. The comment and the constant already disagree by 60 mm. Worse, TIRE_W is not what the geometry uses: the built section is set by the sidewall profile in t1_detail.py:45-53, whose maximum half-width is 0.0752, i.e. a 150.4 mm section — so even the constant is 4.6 mm wider than the mesh it purports to describe. TIRE_W is referenced only by audit.py:177's inset row and probe_rev45_ground.py:158, never by a vertex. A 150 mm section under a 665 mm tyre on a 440 mm rim is a narrow tyre, and it is one reason the wheels read thin in the low and hero 3/4 frames (out/r49s_low34.png, out/r49shut2_hero34r.png).

**Evidence.** t1_core.py:81 (constant and comment); t1_detail.py:45-53 (`up` list, max y = 0.0752 → section 150.4 mm); t1_detail.py:55-61 (tread band spans y = ±0.0522, i.e. 104.4 mm of tread inside a 150 mm section). grep shows TIRE_W used only in audit.py:177 and probe_rev45_ground.py:158, so mesh and constant have never been reconciled. No frame in the set gives a section-width measurement: out/r49s_front.png is cropped below the wheels and there is no plan or elevation photograph of the RED bus.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest argument against: 150 mm may simply be right and only the comment stale, making this bookkeeping rather than fidelity. That is why I graded it minor and have NOT asserted a corrected value — I have no photograph that resolves a tyre's section width on this vehicle. What is not arguable is that three numbers describing one thing (comment 215, constant 155, mesh 150.4) disagree and nothing adjudicates them; on this project that pattern has already produced a stale literal at least three times (the dead RIM_R, the dead REF_PPM, the double-counted arch crown).

*Wheels, tyres, hubcaps and stance*

STATE.md:32's 'measured TRACK_F=1.3713 TRACK_R=1.3613' is a mesh read-back of the same constants plus the tyre wall, not a measurement of the vehicle. The build puts the front track 10 mm WIDER than the rear; nothing in the repository establishes that relationship, and track sets the wheel inset from the flank — AUDIT_RECOVERED.md:356 puts the built inset at 108/113 mm against a factory band of 121-128 mm, i.e. the wheels already sit slightly outboard of the only external figure anyone here has written down.

**Evidence.** t1_core.py:83-84 (bare literals, no comment); verify.py:48 `TRACK_F=1.369, TRACK_R=1.359` and verify.py:796 (the check loop); AUDIT_RECOVERED.md:356 (inset 108/113 mm built vs 121-128 mm quoted). grep for '1.3690' or 'TRACK_F' across the tree finds no probe, no photograph reference and no SPEC section deriving them — SPEC.md:9644 only names them as inputs. No frame constrains track: out/r49s_front.png is cropped below the wheel line, out/r49s_topdown.png is a 62 mm perspective from (2.60, 4.60, 6.40) rather than a plan ortho (studio.py:1370), and ref_nolita_front34.jpg is a 3/4.

I checked and it is not settled: there is no SPEC section, no probe, no ledger entry and no photograph behind these two numbers, and the one external figure anyone has recorded (the 121-128 mm inset in AUDIT_RECOVERED) does not agree with what they produce. I deliberately did NOT quote factory track figures from memory — I cannot verify them from anything in this repository, and doing so would be exactly the kind of plausible-but-unsourced number this project keeps having to retract.

### 69. [MINOR] The cab door handle assembly reads about 205 mm on the vehicle against a 120 mm built footprint
*Glazing, seals, mirrors and wipers*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** t1_detail.py:1859-1877 `handles()` builds an ice-pick pull as a 115 mm x 30 mm base plate centred at x 1.075 plus a lever spanning -0.048..+0.046 about x 1.060. The two overlap, so the combined fore-aft footprint is x 1.012..1.1325 = 120 mm. Two in-service frames of the target vehicle put the chrome assembly at about 205 mm.

**Evidence.** (1) ref_playa_34.png -- probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_ref_playa_cabgrid.png: the chrome handle runs x 248..276 = 28 px. Scale from the cab-door glazed span in the same crop, 102 px for the built 0.730 m -> 140 px/m, so 200 mm. (2) ref_nolita_flank.jpg -- probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_ref_nolflank_wide.png: handle x ~110..140 = 30 px; same ruler on the same frame gives 103 px for 0.730 m -> 141 px/m, so 213 mm. Mean 205 mm against 120 mm built. Both handles are on the same flank at the same station as the ruler, so the flank foreshortening cancels in the ratio.

**Its own refutation.** Counter 1: the bright chrome I measure may include the handle's own specular flare, which at 140 px/m would add a pixel or two at each end -- that is at most 30 mm of the 85 mm gap. Counter 2: the ruler is the built 0.730 m glazing span, which finding 8 shows is only cross-checked to 7 %; a 7 % error moves 205 mm by 14 mm and does not close the gap. Counter 3: a stock 1963 T1 ice-pick escutcheon really is a long part (170-200 mm), so 205 mm is the physically expected value and 120 mm is the anomaly -- which cuts in favour of the finding, not against it. Held at medium because both readings share one ruler.

### 70. [MINOR] The galley back-wall material seam is 100 mm aft (an instance of finding 1) and its rendered step is a third of the photographed one
*The counter, galley and props (counter slab / nosing / top, galley interior seen through the three serving bays, the equipment and counter-top props, the galley*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** The POSITION half is blocked on nothing. The CONTRAST half is partly blocked on the same lighting question as W6 -- the galley's only sources in the model are the roof aperture and the neutral studio, where the photograph's galley is lit by warm bounced daylight, so some of the missing chroma is not the albedo's to supply.

**What is wrong.** GAL_SEAM_X = -0.6140 (t1_detail.py:2880) should be -0.5155 under BAYS[2]. Separately, the seam that the photograph shows as an unmistakable vertical material change across bay 3 barely reads in the render.

**Evidence.** ref_side.jpg bay 3, rows 330-360: forward of the seam (x 595-622) sRGB (201.8,189.3,175.7) L 190.9 sat 0.129; aft of it (x 632-690) (174.6,175.1,174.9) L 175.0 sat 0.003 -- step 15.9 codes, 0.126 saturation. out/r49board_side.png, same z band (1.62-1.70) either side of the BUILT seam: forward (X -0.40..-0.58) (177.1,173.3,168.2) L 173.7 sat 0.050; aft (X -0.65..-0.83) (170.0,168.4,166.1) L 168.5 sat 0.023 -- step 5.2 codes, 0.027 saturation. So 33% of the luminance step and 21% of the chroma step.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter-argument: the chroma half is just W6's achromatic veil re-measured on a new surface, and reporting it as a counter finding double-counts a known-open item. That is why I have split it: the 100 mm is mechanical and independent of any light, and the step size is reported with the confound named rather than as a defect to be tuned away. Second: my sample bands could straddle the seam. They do not -- both are >=35 mm clear of the built seam at -0.614. Third: GAL_WHITE is deliberately near-neutral (0.7300,0.7300,0.7355) because the photograph measures the aft wall at S 0.003, and the render gives 0.023 -- the aft side is if anything too warm, so the shortfall is entirely on the cream side, which is consistent with a veil rather than with a wrong albedo.

### 72. [MINOR] The body paint's three finish constants are the only shading numbers on the vehicle's largest surface with no override and no derivation, and its Coat Roughness is 10x out of family with every other coated material in the file
*Materials and shading (t1_mats.py) — body paint, cream family, chrome/brightwork, rubber/tyre, glass, emissive bulbs, the WEATHER weathering/wear system, orange*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING to expose the levers (three lines). A DEMONSTRATED appearance defect would need a raking-light frame of the target's paint, which the owner cannot currently take.

**What is wrong.** t1_mats.py:1694-1702 sets Roughness 0.420, Coat Weight 0.02 and Coat Roughness 0.300 as hard literals, while the Specular IOR Level one line between them is `os.environ.get('T1_SPEC', 0.50)`. Their stated provenance is a reaction, not a measurement: 'the rev-3 values (rough .105, coat .75 @ .025) put a mirror clearcoat on the body ... Chalky finish restores the chroma.' Meanwhile simple() gives Coat Roughness 0.030 to every other coated material (t1_mats.py:714-716) — bumpercream, cream, capred, capwhite, countercream, countertan, roundelred — so the body paint carries a coat ten times blurrier than the bumper painted with the same cream, with no note anywhere. W6 has now consumed two revisions of LIGHTING sweeps (T1_CYCALB, softbox short axis, both axes, T1_SOFTEN, T1_SPEC); the one material constant that sets how the paint's specular is DISTRIBUTED has never been ablated and cannot be without editing a locked file, in a project whose standing rule (t1_mats.py:637, 1948-1952) is 'ablate a constant to zero and re-measure BEFORE scheduling a solve on it'.

**Evidence.** t1_mats.py:1694 `bsdf.inputs['Roughness'].default_value = 0.420`; :1699-1700 `float(os.environ.get('T1_SPEC', 0.50))`; :1701 Coat Weight 0.02; :1702 Coat Roughness 0.300. Same three literals repeated for `calidad` at t1_mats.py:1778-1783. simple()'s coat branch at t1_mats.py:714-716 hard-sets Coat Roughness 0.030. Grep confirms no T1_ROUGH / T1_COAT / T1_COATRG anywhere in the repository.

**Its own refutation.** I started this as 'the render has no specular highlight anywhere and the photographs plainly do' and I DROPPED that half after testing it with the project's own instrument. C*/(L*+16) binned by L* quintile: ref_rear34.jpg rear quarter + tail red (hue 15-55 deg, C* > 20, n = 63 969) falls 1.186 -> 0.897, i.e. -24 % into the top L* quintile; out/r49base_side.png red flank (n = 24 715) falls 0.780 -> 0.622, -20 %. The specular lobe's SHAPE matches the photograph; only the level is off, and the level is W6. So I am NOT claiming the paint needs a narrower lobe or a real clearcoat, and I am not proposing a value. What is left is strictly an ablatability and internal-consistency defect: three unmeasured literals, one of them 10x out of family, on the exact surface two revisions of W6 work have been about. Second counter: maybe roughness genuinely cannot move the veil, because in a near-uniform white environment the specular integral is F_avg x L_env regardless of lobe width — which is probably true and is precisely why the arm should be RENDERED once and recorded as dead, the way T1_CYCALB was, instead of being unreachable.

### 73. [MINOR] Three of the six cream albedos carry no derivation at all, and they make the front bumper the brightest and glossiest painted surface on the vehicle where the only frame that shows it makes it the dullest
*Materials and shading (t1_mats.py) — body paint, cream family, chrome/brightwork, rubber/tyre, glass, emissive bulbs, the WEATHER weathering/wear system, orange*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING for the documentation half. Settling the ALBEDO half needs a square-on frame of the front bumper against the nose panel under one light, which the owner cannot currently take.

**What is wrong.** t1_mats.py:1791-1798 and :1827 introduce wheelcream (0.7100,0.6900,0.6350), bumpercream (0.7550,0.7350,0.6800) and capwhite (0.8900,0.8880,0.8720) under a single line — '---- SPEC rev4 painted-not-plated / painted-not-timber additions ----' — with no measurement, no window, no reference frame and no error bar, in a file where CREAM, RED, COUNTERCREAM, COUNTERTAN, W_DUST_COL and W_DUST_COL_UP each carry a derivation paragraph. bumpercream is +22 % on CREAM in linear reflectance and capwhite is +44 %. Compounded by finish: bumpercream gets coat 0.30 at Coat Roughness 0.030 against the body's 0.02 at 0.300, so the bumper is both the brightest and by far the glossiest painted surface in the model.

**Evidence.** Same-frame ratio, ref_playa_34.png (= IMG_3842; the RED bus in the TARGET artwork state — Senor Tacombi script, scrollwork, Calidad burst — and the only frame in the set showing the front bumper and the nose cream panel together): bumper face rows 302-314 cols 12-60 L = 107.9; nose cream V rows 200-230 cols 40-62 L = 143.8; ratio 0.751. Against the lower nose cream (rows 240-262 cols 44-60, L = 165.9) the ratio is 0.651. out/r49s_front.png at the same two surfaces: bumper rows 1035-1060 cols 700-900 L = 148.5, nose cream V rows 640-700 cols 700-850 L = 143.0, ratio 1.038. Crop probe_scratch/rev50_materials_ref_playa_bumper.png shows the photographed bumper scuffed, dinged and grimy at both ends. Constants at t1_mats.py:1792, 1794, 1827; WEAR weights at t1_mats.py:681.

**Its own refutation.** This is heavily lighting-confounded and I file it minor for that reason. Outdoors a bumper blade faces a dark ground while the nose faces the sky; in the studio the bumper faces a 0.76-albedo cyclorama floor. So 0.751 is NOT an albedo ratio and I do not claim it is — rule 7(d) eats most of it. Second refutation: my boxes are placed in a 500x400 px frame and the bumper face I sampled includes its own shadow band, which biases the reference number DOWN, i.e. toward my conclusion — so I hold this at medium confidence and report the direction, not a correction. Third refutation: a bumper genuinely could be a different, later paint from the body, which would justify a different albedo — but then the file has to SAY so, and it says nothing. What survives all three is the documentation defect: these are the only undocumented albedos in a file that documents everything else, and they are the ones with the largest unexplained spread (CREAM 0.617 to capwhite 0.889, a 1.44x range).

### 74. [MINOR] The rig cannot read the nose crown, so W4 cannot be scored against these renders at all — 14.3 mm over 0.70 m is at most 3-7% of diffuse shading under any key, and the only nose source is head-on
*Lighting, shadow, camera and optics*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** small · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** W4 has been chased for five revisions as a geometry constant (bulge=0.019). But a crown of 14.3 mm over a 0.70 m half-width has an edge normal tilt of only 1.8 deg (cosine profile, h*pi/2a) to 2.3 deg (parabolic, 2h/a). Under the rig's only forward source - `nose`, a 3.2x2.6 m box at (10.6, 1.6, 1.5) aimed at (1.6, 0.0, 1.05) in studio.lighting(), i.e. essentially head-on to the front panel - a 2 deg normal tilt changes N.L by about 0.1%. Even under a 45 deg key it is 3-7%, the same order as the paint's own mottle. And no source in the rig is placed to lay a raking specular across the front panel, which is the channel that actually reads shallow curvature on sheet metal - the docstring's whole argument for the 16 m `strip` ("its reflection is a single unbroken highlight that ... pinches where the panel turns") is a FLANK argument; the nose gets a frontal fill instead, which is the one placement that erases a shallow dome. So changing `bulge` cannot produce a measurable difference in any current frame, and neither can leaving it wrong. That is a lighting problem standing in front of a geometry problem.

**Evidence.** out/r49s_front.png (front orthographic, 1600x1100): cream nose band rows 630-700, column-mean profile over cols 470-1120 runs 146, 147, 149, 147, 141, 148, 146, 144, 154, 157, 155, 149, 150 DN - flat to about +-5 DN with a slight monotone rise toward the off side and NO maximum at the vehicle centreline, which is the signature a crown would leave under a frontal source. The paint's own high-pass (5x5 box) sd on comparable cream is 4.27 DN, the same size as the entire lateral variation. Cross-check on out/r49s_detail_f.png (100 mm, f/6.3, inside the sharp zone): cream nose panel rows 500-640 cols 150-700 has a lateral column-mean ramp of 130.7 -> 132.3 DN end to end, 1.2% of its own mean. Geometry bound stated with its assumption: edge slope = h*pi/2a = 0.0143*1.571/0.70 = 0.0321 rad for a cosine crown, 2h/a = 0.0409 rad for a parabolic one; if the built profile is a high-power curve (z proportional to 1-(x/a)^p, p about 4) the edge slope rises to 0.082 rad = 4.7 deg and the bound roughly doubles - still single-digit percent. What would make this reading wrong: a raking source I have missed, or a crown concentrated in a narrow band near the centreline rather than distributed.

**Its own refutation.** Refutation 1: the eye integrates over area, so a smooth 3-7% ramp is perceptible even against 3% per-pixel noise - which is why I dropped the stronger claim ("invisible") and kept the weaker one (the shading channel is a weak reader and no source addresses the specular channel). Refutation 2: the owner's "the nose is too flat" may be about the SILHOUETTE in a 3/4 view, not about shading, in which case lighting is irrelevant. Possible, and it argues the same way: if the silhouette is the read, then the front elevation's flat shading is not the defect and the geometry work should be scored on outline, not on renders of the nose face. Either way the current scoring surface is wrong. Refutation 3: my front-ortho window might sit on the V-panel rather than the crowned cream. Checked against the marked render - rows 630-700 are the cream above the V and below the windscreen aperture. Refutation 4: an orthographic projection removes the perspective cue that would show a crown; true, which is why I re-ran it on detail_f, a 100 mm perspective view, and got 1.2%.

### 75. [MINOR] THE TYRE HAS FOUR CIRCUMFERENTIAL GROOVES AND NOTHING ELSE — no lateral tread pattern and a smooth sidewall, where the highest-resolution geometry frame shows shoulder sipes and a raised sidewall rib
*THE FIDELITY BAR AUDIT — painted-on / flat-sweep features vs real geometry with depth, across t1_detail.py, t1_shell.py, texgen.py and tex/, ranked by how visib*  
**confidence** medium · **effort** medium · **blocked on** NOTHING

**What is wrong.** t1_detail.tyre() (t1_detail.py:43-92) builds a revolve: a crowned tread band with four 8 mm circumferential grooves (t1_detail.py:56-63) and a smooth sidewall profile (:46-53). A revolve cannot carry a lateral pattern by construction. rev 44's own fidelity audit lists 'TYRE TREAD. Displaced or modelled, never a texture' as criterion 3 (probe_rev44_fidelity.py:16-17) and the build satisfies the letter of it — grooves are modelled, not painted — but not its intent: the tread reads as four rings on an otherwise blank drum in every view that sees over the arch.

**Evidence.** SOURCE t1_detail.py:56-63 (the seventeen-point tread list; every `d` flag is a circumferential groove, there is no angular term anywhere in the function) and :46-53 (sidewall, monotonic, no rib). PHOTOGRAPH IMG_2073 at 5x, probe_scratch/rev50_fidelity-bar_green_wheel_x5.png (GREEN bus — tyre tread is geometry, and in any case is a fitted part common to both): lateral sipes are individually resolved across the tyre's shoulder from about 10 o'clock to 2 o'clock, and the sidewall carries a raised circumferential rib above the bead. RENDER out/r49s_low34.png front wheel, probe_scratch/rev50_fidelity-bar_r49_tread_x5.png: the visible tread band is featureless apart from faint circumferential lines.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter: the tread is visible in almost no shipped view. True — the side elevation sees only the sidewall, and the tread occupies perhaps 40 x 12 px in low34. That is why this is MINOR and not major, and I say so rather than inflating it. Second counter: rule 26 — IMG_2073 is the green bus. Tyres are a fitted part, not bodywork, so strictly neither geometry nor artwork transfers; what transfers is only that a tyre of this era on this vehicle carries lateral tread, which ref_side.jpg's rear tyre corroborates weakly (its shoulder shows non-uniform dark structure but is too small to resolve). I therefore claim the ABSENCE of a lateral pattern in the build, which is a fact about the source, and offer the photograph only as motivation. Third counter: adding lateral tread costs a lot of geometry on four wheels — hence 'medium' effort and low rank; a normal-map on the tread band alone would violate the fidelity bar and should not be the fix.

The third frame CONTRADICTS it: in ref_nolita_flank.jpg (probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_ref_nolflank_wide.png) I cannot find a divider inside the door aperture at all -- the door glass is down and the only candidate post reads as the A-pillar. The most likely explanation is that the vent wing is pivoted open in that frame so its frame merges with the pillar, but I cannot prove it, and 'I cannot identify the members' is the honest state. The two frames I do trust agree only on DIRECTION (0.265 and 0.34, both > the built 0.20), not on magnitude, and both use the built 0.730 m span as their ruler. Do not move the divider on this alone; it wants the same darker, closer frame the decal work is waiting on.

### 77. [MINOR] The serving bays may be glazed after all: ref_rear34.jpg is an independent second target frame that SPEC 10.29's argument never used -- but this is owner-ruled and must not be applied
*Glazing, seals, mirrors and wipers*  
**confidence** low · **effort** large · **blocked on** A ruling from the owner, or one frame of the CURRENT-livery bus taken square-on to a bay at close range. The two states may simply differ: the Nolita frames show open apertures with a gold pinstripe frame, the current frames show a veil plus a bobble fringe.

**What is wrong.** t1_shell.py:154 `OPEN_BAYS = (0,1,2)` removes all three show-side panes. SPEC.md:1229-1238 already logs 'the serving bays may be GLAZED' as NOT APPLIED, blocked on 'a third method or a new photograph', because it contradicts a reading the owner settled himself. I am not reopening the ruling; I am recording that a target frame he may not have been shown carries the same signature at a completely different incidence angle, on a different day, outdoors -- which is a different failure mode from the camera-flare explanation SPEC offers.

**Evidence.** ref_rear34.jpg (TARGET frame) -- probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_ref_rear34_bays5x.png, 5x of box (530,255)-(780,400). The three bays are seen at grazing incidence and each carries a uniform pale veil with a green cast where foliage sits opposite, and the veil terminates exactly at the aperture rim inside the bobble fringe. SPEC's own arms were photometric and taken on ref_side.jpg only (interior floor never below 79/94/119, an 11:1 dynamic range). Grazing incidence is the geometric arm those two lack: a Fresnel reflection strengthens toward grazing, camera flare does not. CONTRARY EVIDENCE, and it is strong: ref_nolita_doorshut.jpg -- probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_ref_ds_door8x.png, 8x of box (100,120)-(260,200) -- shows the cook's forearm passing THROUGH the plane of bay 0 and a second arm through bay 1. In that state the bays are certainly open.

**Its own refutation.** The refutation is in the finding and it is decisive for the Nolita state -- a hand cannot pass through glass. So the only live hypothesis is that glazing was ADDED between the Nolita and Playa/target states, which is exactly the kind of single-chain claim this project has been burned by. I also could not separate 'glass' from 'insect screen' or 'clear plastic sheet', any of which produce the same veil and none of which is a 6 mm pane. Reported at low, marked blocked, and explicitly NOT proposed as a change.

### 78. [MINOR] THE ROOF PEAK VENT IS INVENTED DEPTH — 9 mm of bezel and 2 mm ribs that no frame in the project supports, and that render as three hairlines
*THE FIDELITY BAR AUDIT — painted-on / flat-sweep features vs real geometry with depth, across t1_detail.py, t1_shell.py, texgen.py and tex/, ranked by how visib*  
**confidence** low · **effort** small · **blocked on** A frame looking DOWN onto the roof peak above the windscreen, or a front three-quarter taken from above waist height. IMG_2073's roof is nearly edge-on, so it cannot exclude a 9 mm bezel.

**What is wrong.** t1_detail.roof_vent() (t1_detail.py:2660-2682) builds a 0.360 x 0.062 m bezel 9 mm proud plus two 0.330 x 0.006 m ribs standing a further 2 mm, and its own docstring says 'Schematic -- a raised bezel with two ribs ... FLAGGED as inferred: no reference we hold resolves it.' The fidelity bar cuts both ways: it asks for real depth on real features, and this is real depth on a feature nothing corroborates. In the shipped hero it contributes only three barely-visible hairlines on the roof — too shallow to read as a pressing, but present enough to be wrong if the vehicle has no vent there.

**Evidence.** SOURCE t1_detail.py:2660-2682 (bezel depth 0.0090, ribs 0.0060 at +0.0055 along the normal). RENDER out/r49s_hero34f.png, probe_scratch/rev50_fidelity-bar_h34f_roofvent_x7.png (7x): the whole assembly reads as faint outline strokes with no self-shadow. PHOTOGRAPH: the highest-resolution frame of the roof above the windscreen in the whole set is IMG_2073 (1400x933) — probe_scratch/rev50_fidelity-bar_green_roofpeak_x4.png shows the drip rail plainly (a deep gutter with its own shadow) and a smooth roof crown with no vent, no bezel and no ribs.

**Its own refutation.** Strongest counter, and it is why I rate this LOW and report it as a flag rather than a defect: IMG_2073's camera is close to the roof plane, so a 9 mm raised bezel would foreshorten into almost nothing and its absence there is not evidence. I am NOT proposing removal. Second counter: this could be re-litigation — the source already flags it as inferred, so nothing here is new information. That is fair; what is new is that the best geometry frame in the set has now actually been LOOKED AT for it and reported as inconclusive, so the next revision does not have to. Third counter: a bezel this shallow is invisible either way, so it cannot affect photorealism. Agreed for the side and rear views; the topdown view (out/r49s_topdown.png) is the one where an invented feature would show, and it is a shipped view.

## ALREADY RIGHT — DO NOT RE-LITIGATE THESE

**The flank, belt line and louvres — side elevation of the RED target bus (ref_side.jpg / ref_rear34.jpg), with geometry-only corroboration fr**
* THE LOUVRES SELF-SHADOW IN THE CURRENT SHIPPED RENDER, not just in rev 48's. out/r49board_side.png: block mean luminance 76.3 (rows 672-729, cols 1140-1215) against the adjacent panel at 88.9 (aft), 86.1 (below) and 110.3 (forward). Signed modulation is NEGATIVE, which is the sign ref_side.jpg has. rev 48's aperture fix holds at rev 49. Do not re-open the sign.
* SLOT COUNT AND UNIFORMITY ARE CORRECT. Render: 11 dark bands at a constant 5.600 px pitch, identical at three separate column windows. IMG_2073.jpeg (GREEN bus, geometry transfers): 11 dark minima at a constant 8.15 px pitch, at two separate column windows (rows 476, 483, 491.5, 500, 508, 516, 524, 532, 540.5, 548.5, 557). Structurally identical. Do NOT add or remove a slot row.
* THE BLOCK'S OUTLINE IS RECTANGULAR, NOT STAGGERED. I twice read a raked forward edge off crops and it was the flower mural. IMG_2073 numerically: slot rows 483, 491, 499, 507, 515, 523, 531, 539, 547, 555 start at cols 1140-1149 and end at cols 1180-1195, with no systematic taper. Refuted and dropped.
* THE BLOCK'S VERTICAL CENTRE IS RIGHT. Built centre 0.96675 m above ground against the header's measured centre 0.96670 — 0.05 mm. Only the phase and the extent are wrong (see finding 3), not the placement.
* THE BLOCK LENGTH AS BUILT IS CONSISTENT WITH ref_side.jpg; IT IS THE HEADER THAT IS NOT. Row-profile read of the three strongest slot rows gives cols 772-837 = X -1.2086..-1.4946, length 0.286, against the built 0.2952 and the header's 0.385. Do not "restore" 0.385.
* THE BELT LINE HEIGHT ON THE CAB DOOR IS RIGHT. ref_side.jpg at u = 190 (X = +1.563), redness-gradient break at z 1.1952 m above ground against t1_mats.z_belt(1.563) = 1.1963 — 1.1 mm. Ceiling: one usable column (the leaning man occludes u 195-330 and the nose curvature invalidates the flank map forward of u ~185), plus +-2.3 % of the 0.86 m lever = +-20 mm systematic.
* THE CREAM/RED SPLIT VISIBLE ALONG THE SERVING BAY IS THE COUNTER FASCIA BOTTOM, NOT THE BODY BELT, AND IT IS LEVEL. Measured with a redness gradient at 26 stations from X +0.7 to -1.8: z = 1.0916 +- 0.0018 m above ground, flat to 6 mm peak-to-peak, i.e. a ground-parallel shelf and not a body line (a body line must rise 47 mm over that span at RAKE_DZDX). flank_compare.py:461-463 already says this for the lockup columns; it now holds over the whole flank. Anyone measuring "the belt" aft of the cab door in ref_side.jpg is measuring the counter.
* REAR WHEEL ARCH WIDTH AND STATION. Reference cols 652 -> 853 through the SPEC 10.35 map = X -0.656..-1.566, width 0.910 m, centre -1.111. Built ARCH_W_REAR = 0.920 centred on X_AXLE_R = -1.100. 10 mm on width, 11 mm on centre, both inside the +-0.03 the source declares.
* REAR ARCH-TO-TYRE GAP. Built as ARCH_R - TIRE_R = 0.3735 - 0.3325 = 41.0 mm, which is the rev-6 measurement verbatim. Nothing to change.
* THE GREY SPECKLE INSIDE THE RENDER'S LOUVRE SLOTS IS NOT A LOUVRE DEFECT. Fraction of pixels with G/R > 0.50: 0.351 in the block against 0.283, 0.252 and 0.314 on three plain flank panels in the same frame. It is the flank's general paint-wear texture, present everywhere. Refuted and dropped.

**The nose and front: nose face and plan crown (W4), V swage, VW roundel and bars, headlamps and bowls, indicators, front blade/bumper, windsc**
* ROUNDEL_Z_AG = 1.0170 IS CORRECT -- DO NOT MOVE THE ROUNDEL. Independent read of ref_workshop.jpg gives the roundel 0.174-0.180 m above the headlamp centre (depth-corrected, cy 395-412) against the built 0.189 m and probe_rev44_lampmove's chain B of 0.1758 +- 0.0185 m. All three agree inside one sigma. build.py:614's 'moving geometry on a 0.7-sigma difference is what this project calls laundering' still stands after rev 45's HL_DROP.
* IND_DZ = 0.206 +- 0.010 IS CORRECT. My first read said 0.167 m (3.8 sigma low) and I RETRACT IT -- it was scaled by a calibration that mistook build.py:572's '0.149 m below the BELT' for 'above the lamp'. Re-anchored on ROUNDEL_D over the roundel's 91 px vertical and depth-corrected for the pod being 0.130 m nearer, the same pixels give 0.197 m, 0.9 sigma. IND_DY = 0.130 +- 0.035 likewise reads 0.15 m, inside.
* ROUNDEL_D = 0.2800 REPRODUCES. Running build.py:576's own pose-free method on my own pixel reads of ref_workshop.jpg (roundel 91 px, near lamp 75.6 px, far lamp 60.6 px vertical) gives D_roundel/D_aperture = 1.35 against its published 1.384. The instrument used for every finding above is calibrated against the project's own number.
* THE FRONT BUMPER BLADE HEIGHT IS RIGHT. Measured in ref_workshop.jpg against the near headlamp aperture as the local ruler (74 px = 176 mm): blade 45 px = 107 mm, against BUMP_PROFILE's 113 mm and t1_detail.py:360's cited 0.110 +- 0.010. No action.
* THE BULLET INDICATOR IS THE RIGHT TYPE -- do not revisit the rev-10 'flat oval standing 15 mm proud' proposal. A proud amber bullet on a chrome plinth is confirmed on the RED bus in ref_side.jpg, ref_nolita_front34.jpg and ref_playa_34.png, and the GREEN bus's bare 27x29 px mounting hole in ref_workshop.jpg is consistent with the built 79 mm plinth covering a 68 mm hole.
* THE V SWAGE IS A REAL PRESSED FEATURE AND THE PAINT BREAK FOLLOWS IT -- confirmed on BOTH vehicles and in two lighting conditions (bright line in ref_nolita_front34, dark line in ref_workshop, both riding the break exactly). Only its SECTION is unanchored; its existence and its registration to the paint are not in doubt.
* THE V APEX TERMINATES AT THE BUMPER LINE, correctly. Render out/r49s_hero34f.png and out/r49s_front.png put the cream V's apex at the bumper's top edge; ref_playa_34.png and ref_nolita_front34.jpg do the same. No action.
* THE FRONT WHEEL ARCH'S PLAIN CIRCULAR CUTTER IS SUPPORTED. t1_shell.py:357 explicitly excluded the front arch when the REAR arch's shape was corrected away from a circle, leaving it unmeasured -- but ref_nolita_flank.jpg gives the front arch a rise/half-width of ~0.49 against 0.50 for a semicircle. Low priority; the current build is defensible.
* THE RENDER IS INTERNALLY CONSISTENT -- the defect in finding 1 is in the MODEL, not in the rendering or the camera. out/r49s_hero34f.png's lamp-vs-roundel foreshortening difference is 2.0 deg against a ray-spread allowance of 3.8 deg at f~1600 px, i.e. exactly what a planar nose predicts. Head-on (out/r49s_front.png) the roundel reads 0.975 and the bezel 1.048, both circles, again exactly as a planar nose with axis-parallel lamps predicts.
* THE VW GLYPH'S PROPORTIONS CANNOT BE ADJUDICATED FROM ANY FRAME WE HOLD. I built an ink-per-row profile normalised to the roundel's own vertical extent (probe_scratch/rev50_vw_rows.py) to test whether the render's V/W crossing sits too high. The render resolves cleanly (minimum at t=0.492, maximum at t=0.712); ref_nolita_front34's roundel is 54 px tall with ~3 px strokes and returns a flat, blur-inflated profile with no usable minimum. The measurement is NOT AVAILABLE at the resolution we have -- do not spend a revision on it without a closer nose frame.
* THE HEADLAMP APERTURE DIAMETER IS CLOSE. Measured 0.197 +- 0.015 m in ref_workshop.jpg against a bore cut at 0.1724 m; the gap is real (see finding 6) but small, and the LENS at 172 mm is within a few percent of a T1's ~178 mm. Do not treat this as a large error.

**THE TAIL AND REAR — tail panel, engine lid + shut line, T-handle, 1963 plate, tail lamps, rear window aperture and what shows through it, re**
* THE T-HANDLE'S PLACEMENT RELATIVE TO THE PLATE IS CORRECT. ENGLID_HANDLE_DROP = 1.274 * PLATE_OUTER_H gives 0.774 plate-heights below the plate's lower rail; ref_rear34.jpg reads 41.6/59.6 = 0.698 at x=1125 — 13 mm. The rev-15 decision to express it as a ratio of PLATE_OUTER_H rather than an absolute is vindicated. Do not touch it; it moves for free when the lid moves.
* THE ENGINE LID'S OUTLINE SIZE IS CORRECT. Height: photographed 177.5 px = 3.045 PLATE_OUTER_H against ENGLID_GAP's built 0.5055/0.16823 = 3.005 (+1.3%). Only its z STATION is wrong.

My independent R-B boundary extraction gives 64 px vertical against the rev-15 measurement's 69.06 px, i.e. 0.185-0.196 m against the built TAIL_LAMP_OD = 0.1956. Its apparent aspect (1.56) falls inside build.py:582's own 1.189-1.847 control band. Do not re-litigate 'half size'.

* THE REAR BUMPER IS CORRECTLY ABSENT. build.py:364-366 comments out `bumper(False)` on SPEC rev6 sec.2.4 (removed after the conversion) and `bumper_irons` is called for the FRONT only. ref_rear34.jpg shows the tail's lower edge running down to a rolled return and shadow with no blade. TRUNK_OPEN_DEG = 0.0 (t1_shell.py:1289) with the swing SKIPPED rather than run at zero, and the plate and handle correctly no longer joined to SWUNG. Verified in out/r49shut2_hero34r.png and out/r49s_rear.png. Do not propose reopening the lower bay.
* THE TAIL BOARD'S RIM IS DELIBERATELY ON ONE LONG EDGE ONLY, AND ITS RED/BLACK SPLIT RENDERS CORRECTLY. tail_board_edge (t1_shell.py:1658-1680) reasons it out: bulbs on the near/show-side edge, rim on the other; red over the tip half, near-black over the base half. out/r49s_rear.png shows exactly that split (red x 905-925 over y 195-295, dark below to 360). Not a defect.

* THE ENGINE-LID SHUT LINE'S CONTRAST IS NOT A DEFECT. The render bottoms at L 12.2 against the photograph's 42.3, but a 5.5 mm gap is 1.9 px in ref_rear34.jpg and 2.5 px in the render; convolving the render's 3 px slot with the photograph's PSF accounts for the whole difference. Raised and dropped — do not spend a revision on 'the seams are too black'.

**The roof, lids and drip rail — roof skin/crown, roof aperture, raised mural lid (geometry, hinge, struts, junction), drip rail/gutter, bulb **
* THE ROOF APERTURE'S SURVIVING SIDE STRIPS MATCH THE OWNER'S SETTLEMENT — do not re-open. t1_shell.py:1094-1096 records him settling "roughly 0.3 m" of roof each side. In PLAN the built strips are only 0.162 m (show) and 0.182 m (off), which looks like a 1.7x miss; measured the way a strip of roof is measured — as arc from the aperture edge to the drip rail — they are 0.286 m and 0.306 m. I computed this by hand from ZT_ALL/CR_ALL/RT_ALL/WX/G at the lid station (Yt = 0.7273, reproducing the SPEC comment's own 0.7273 exactly) and the gutter's z from t1_detail.py:1730-1733. I raised this as a finding and then killed it.
