The two frames are not comparable on the roof. I raised this and dropped it.
* THE DRIP-RAIL BULB STRING IS EMISSIVE. t1_detail.py:2421 still claims "Rendered as unlit pearl glass: this project has no emissive material", which is STALE — t1_detail.py:3365 hands the string the "bulb" key and t1_mats.py:1880 builds it as `emissive("bulb", (1.000, 0.760, 0.442), strength=9.0)`. The comment should be corrected, but the geometry and material are right. The bulbs still read near-achromatic in the render (peak (245,239,233), B/R 0.955, against (255,225,165), B/R 0.647 in ref_side.jpg) and are NOT clipped — that is W6's achromatic specular veil, not a bulb defect.
* BULB PITCH AND SIZE. BULB_PITCH 0.0286 and BULB_R 0.0110 (t1_detail.py:2440) put 22 mm bulbs on a 28.6 mm pitch; at ref_side.jpg's 211.5 px/m that is 6.05 px pitch and 4.65 px bulbs, which is what the photograph shows (probe_scratch/rev50_roof-lids_REF_boardbot10x.png). The +5 % extent overrun is declared at t1_detail.py:2433-2435. No finding.
* THE BUILD FAITHFULLY EXECUTES ITS OWN LID CONSTANTS. The mural face in out/r49board_side.png measures cols 546-1082 x rows 150-435 = 537 x 286 px; removing the 10 px rake tilt gives 537/276 = 1.946 against the 1.938 predicted from (LID_X0-LID_X1+2*inset) / ((LID_W-2*inset)*sin104°). The 537 px also reproduces the declared ortho scale exactly (1600/5.90 = 271.2 px/m; 1.974 m x 271.2 = 535). The lid's errors are in its CONSTANTS, not in its code path.
* THE MURAL IS ON THE CORRECT FACE AT THE CORRECT OFFSET. `_lid_face(..., off=-(LID_T + 0.0016))` (t1_shell.py:2014) puts the decal 1.6 mm below the solidified underside, clear of `_lid_panel`'s pressed seams which only dip to -0.0028 (t1_shell.py:1175). The rev-11 poke-through is genuinely fixed.
* ONE ROOF CUTTER, EXPRESSED IN TERMS OF THE LID. roof_cutters (t1_shell.py:1114-1156) issues exactly one aperture and derives it from LID_X0/LID_X1/LID_Y_HINGE/LID_W, so moving the lid moves the hole. Its rev-16 prism-centring fix (origin at (zlo+zhi)/2 because solid_prism extrudes about its origin) is correct, and the assert against cutting the header rail is real.
* THE STRUT TIP LANDS ON THE LID BY CONSTRUCTION. t1_shell.py:2109-2114 uses the hinge origin `zh` rather than roof_z at the strut's own x, so the tip is on the lid's plane analytically rather than by luck. That reasoning is sound and should survive any change to the strut's foot.
* THE PROP COUNT IS THE OWNER'S. Two struts are built, one near each end, inset 160 mm (t1_shell.py:2102-2103), matching his rev-37 verbatim count. The rev-45 guard that each foot lies outside the aperture's y band, and the rev-44b guard that each rod is inside the lid's x-span, both do what they say.
* THE ENGINE LID IS SHUT AND ONLY THE REAR WINDOW IS OPEN in out/r49shut2_hero34r.png, matching the rev-49 ruling. I did not propose reopening it.
* THE TAIL BOARD IS ATTACHED AT THE DRIP RAIL AND IS NOT THE 'La Santa' SIGN. Both settled; I took no position on its width, lateral centring or the declared 80 mm footing question, which t1_shell.py:1585-1613 states honestly and blocks on a photograph. My finding about it is only about the z DATUM and the guard.

**Wheels, tyres, hubcaps and stance**
* RIM_R = 0.2198 is CORRECT. The render's cream circle measures 219.5 mm outer radius (720-ray luminance half-crossing on out/r49board_side.png at the exact 271.186 px/m), and in ref_side.jpg the rim flange fits a circle at R = 46.494 px over 356 rays with rms 0.527 px, which is 0.2198 m at the project's own 211.5 px/m. Exact on both sides. Do not touch it.
* TIRE_R/RIM_R = 1.5127 is CORRECT, and by extension TIRE_R = 0.3325 is correct. Measured on ref_rear34.jpg (RED, target, rear wheel) along the ellipse's MAJOR axis, which is unforeshortened: cream ring outer edges at rows 663 and 803 (centre 733, semi 70.0 px), tyre crown at row 627 (106.0 px) → 1.514, a 0.1% match. I spent most of this survey trying to break this and could not. The apparent shortfall in ref_side.jpg is tyre squat, not tyre size.
* ARCH_R = 0.3735 is CORRECT. In ref_side.jpg the rear arch lip at the crown is at row 524.5 (red/dark step, stable to ±0.5 px across x = 735..765) and the hub is at 604.26, so the lip stands 79.76 px above the hub = 1.7155 rim radii against the built 1.6993 — 1%. This independently corroborates the rev-16 LOFT trace's 0.3726 ± 0.0052.
* The arch-to-tyre gap is CORRECT and inside SPEC §2's 41 ± 8 mm. Measured straight off the ortho image rather than off the mesh: out/r49board_side.png gives 40.1 mm at BOTH crowns (arch lip row 771, tyre top row 781.9, 271.186 px/m), corroborating verify's 39.7 / 40.7 by a wholly separate route. The photograph implies 44.6 mm (79.76 − 70.33 px). No action needed.
* The wheels correctly do NOT rake, and they correctly touch z = 0. build.py:870-873's exemption of the wheels from the step-8b shear is right and its stated reason (shearing them would swing the hubcap glyph 1.9° off vertical) is sound. Measured on the render, the tyre's lowest row is 962-963 against the computed z = 0 row of 962.2. In the model's own frame there is no float — the float is the missing squat (finding 2), not a placement error.
* BLACKWALL is correct — SPEC r4's ruling holds. The bright ring in ref_side.jpg is the painted rim, not a whitewall band: its outer radius fits at 46.494 px = 0.2198 m = RIM_R exactly. A whitewall would overhang the flange and the fitted radius would exceed RIM_R. It does not.
* The hubcap emblem is correct as built and rev 44's spine fix is holding. The photograph shows a white VW inside a closed cream annulus on a red dome (probe_scratch/rev50_wheels-stance_ref_hubcap_x7.png); the render shows the same; and t1_core.py:939-1005's fixed-point solve puts all six terminals on the band circle with the convergence asserted rather than typed. The rev-17 defect ('the V stops 4.28 mm short of the ring') is CLOSED — do not re-report it.
* CAP_R = 0.1345 itself is correct; the cap is not too small. Photographed red-dome/rim-circle ratio 0.6446 against a built intent of 0.6233 — within the blur systematic, and the dome's own shading biases the photographic half-crossing inward, so the true photographed value is if anything closer to the build. Finding 1 is an occlusion, not a size error. Do not shrink or grow CAP_R.
* The five rim vent holes are correctly NOT scaled by S — t1_detail.py:151-158's reasoning is sound and their radius of 0.0235 at r = 0.118 is fine. What is wrong is the CAP's depth, not the vents' size. Scaling them was never the issue and un-scaling them is not the fix.
* The wheelbase is consistent with the photograph. The rear hub sits at u = 749.36 in ref_side.jpg; at the project's 211.5 px/m a 2.400 m wheelbase puts the front hub at u = 241.8, and the front cream ring in that frame is centred at u ≈ 241. X_AXLE_F − X_AXLE_R needs no work.
* The wheels are placed straight (steer = 0) and the far-side mirroring (build.py:352-355, negate y then fix_normals) is correct — the far-side hubcap glyph reads the right way round in out/r49s_low34.png.
* DROPPED, and recorded so the next revision does not chase them: (a) 'the rendered tyre is too light' — tyre/cream luminance 0.204 photographed vs 0.275 rendered, a real 35% gap but a white-studio-versus-street lighting difference (rule 7d) already covered by W6; (b) 'the sidewall is blotchy' — low-frequency sd/mean 0.148 photo vs 0.182 render, only 23% and the photographic window includes the arch shadow gradient, so not significant; (c) 'TIRE_R is 62 mm too big' — refuted by ref_rear34's unforeshortened axis, see finding 2; (d) 'the 17.75 mm/m rake is too shallow' — my coarse rocker-versus-hub instrument said 25 ± 5 mm/m and my sub-pixel version of the same instrument said 12.8 ± 1.2 mm/m and returned 13.2 on the render where the truth is 17.75, so the instrument is biased and I have no working rake measurement. I did NOT reproduce t1_detail.py's rev-15 'cream-ring D / tyre D = 0.660' off ref_side.jpg, because the tyre's outer boundary there is against dark on every side — but I am not calling it wrong, since ref_rear34 independently confirms the ratio it produced.

**Glazing, seals, mirrors and wipers**
* THE BOBBLE FRINGE ON THE THREE SERVING BAYS IS CORRECT. I first read the white bead ring in probe_scratch/rev50_glazing_REN_bayrim.png as a defect. It is t1_detail.py:2387 `bobble_fringe()`, deliberate, and it matches ref_side.jpg (rev50_glazing_ref_side_bay2_9x.png, bay3_9x.png), ref_rear34.jpg (ref_rear34_bays5x.png) and ref_playa_34.png. Do not touch the bay chain; only ADD the windscreen one.
* THE MIRROR HEAD DIAMETER IS RIGHT. Built 131 mm circular (t1_detail.py:1745, revolve to r = 0.0655). Measured off ref_playa_34.png (rev50_glazing_ref_playa_mirror.png, 12x): head outline 15 px wide x 21 px tall at ~140 px/m = 107 mm minor (foreshortened, a lower bound) x 150 mm major. 131 mm sits inside that. Do not resize it.
* THE MIRROR SHAPE IS RIGHT, AND ref_workshop.jpg's BIG RECTANGULAR MIRRORS ARE A TRAP. The workshop shell carries tall rounded-rectangle elephant-ear heads on long raked arms (rev50_glazing_ref_ws_mirror_grid2.png, ref_ws_farmirror.png, measured ~59 px tall at ~266 px/m = 222 mm, aspect ~1.55). The in-service red bus carries a small ROUND head. That is conversion-stage hardware, the same class SPEC 10.75/10.93 already ruled does not transfer. Do not rebuild the head as a rectangle.
* THE MIRROR IS MOUNTED ON THE A-PILLAR FORWARD OF THE CAB DOOR, NOT ON THE DOOR. The built root x = 1.815 is forward of the door's leading edge (1.756) and ref_playa_34.png puts the bracket on the pillar. Only the arm's ANGLE and the root's HEIGHT are wrong.
* THE MIRROR IS PRESENT ON THE TARGET VEHICLE. It is absent from all four Nolita frames (rev50_glazing_ref_ds_cab9x.png, ref_nolflank_wide.png, ref_nol34_apillar.png, ref_f34b_cab7x.png) and present in ref_playa_34.png, which is the current livery. Do not delete it on the strength of the Nolita frames.
* THE CAB DOOR GLAZING IS DIVIDED INTO A VENT PLUS A MAIN PANE. Confirmed independently in ref_nolita_doorshut.jpg (rev50_glazing_ref_ds_doorgrid.png, a full-height bright post between two glazed panes) and ref_playa_34.png (rev50_glazing_ref_playa_cabgrid.png). Only the divider's STATION is in question, not its existence.
* FLAT SHADING IS CORRECT FOR glass_ws+/-1 AND glass_rear. Both are `T.solid_prism` slabs (t1_shell.py:37, 244) and are genuinely planar; build.py:45 is right for them. The finding applies only to the six `conform_solid` panes.
* THE GLASS BSDF's IOR (1.47), ROUGHNESS (0.022) AND BASE COLOUR (0.780, 0.845, 0.815) ARE FINE. t1_mats.py:1816. The pane's problem is what it can see, not what it is made of. Only `spec=0.35` is worth questioning, and I could not confirm Blender 4.x even applies Specular IOR Level under Transmission Weight 1.0.
* THE BAYS CARRY RUBBER SURROUNDS ON BOTH FLANKS. t1_shell.py:214 `bay_seals()` issues a ring for every aperture on both sides including the open show-side hatches. Only their WIDTH (7.5 mm) is in question and only the REAR aperture is missing one entirely.
* THE WIPER ENGINEERING IS RIGHT, WHICH IS WHY DELETING THEM IS SAFE. t1_detail.py:1781-1830 puts the blade in the windscreen plane off S.WS_DIR/S.WS_N, the spindle on the measured cowl normal, and registers all three in VISIBILITY_WATCH. The rev-? fix for the buried wipers was correct as engineering; the parts should simply not be on this vehicle. Keep wiper_pivot/wiper_boss, delete wblade/wiper_arm.
* THE REAR PANE IS GLAZED AND HINGED OPEN AS THE REV-49 RULING REQUIRES. out/r49shut2_hero34r.png shows the engine lid shut and only the rear window open. I am not proposing to reopen that; the rear-seal finding is about the missing rubber, not the pose.
* THE GLASS IS NOT UNIFORMLY DARK EVERYWHERE. On the show side in out/r49s_hero34f.png the far windscreen pane reads mean 160 codes with a max of 246 against a cream cowl at mean 144 -- it is catching a softbox and reads as glass. The defect is direction-dependent, worst on the off flank and on any pane whose mirror ray leaves the ground plane.

**The counter, galley and props (counter slab / nosing / top, galley interior seen through the three serving bays, the equipment and counter-t**
* THE BAY-INTERIOR CONTRAST ITEM (galley_dressing section 6b) IS NOW CLOSED -- DO NOT SPEND ANOTHER REVISION ON IT. Measured inside the cut edge with a 5% inset, aperture boxes taken from the model's own stations: render bay1 L 155.6 sd 24.09, bay2 158.8 sd 23.66, bay3 180.3 sd 22.90 against ref_side.jpg's 149.1 sd 34.13, 156.8 sd 25.06, 174.8 sd 23.38. The published gap (bay1 15.3 vs 28.4, bay2 10.2 vs 24.7) is gone; bay 2 is within 1.4 sd-units and bay 3 within 0.5. Bay 1's residual 24.1 vs 34.1 is at the section's own declared ceiling -- it states that ~37% of bay 1's photographed variance is the man standing in it. Run at BOTH resolutions: downsampling the render from 268.9 to the photograph's 211.5 px/m changes nothing (24.09/23.66/22.90 -> 24.09/23.66/22.90).
* The three serving apertures are geometrically correct in the shipped render. Bay pitches measure 168.0 and 173.5 px against the model's 0.625 and 0.645 m -> 268.8 and 269.0 px/m (0.1% apart, so the side camera is effectively orthographic), and the aperture widths measure 137-140 px against BAY_W 0.5155 m = 138.6 px.
* The counter slab's thickness renders correctly: brass-nosing top to cream/red break measures 29 px at 268.9 px/m = 107.8 mm against the built CNT_ZT-CNT_ZB = 107.0 mm.
* CNT_NOSE_F is right. The brass caps 0.207 of the slab edge in the render (gold band 6 px of a 29 px edge) against 0.208 measured in ref_side.jpg (5 px of 24 px at x 600). Do not make the nosing deeper.
* `countertan` IS reaching the counter top and IS reading tan in the views that see the top. out/r49s_counter.png band-following sample: top/fascia luminance ratio 0.835 against 0.890 from ref_side.jpg's own founding crops and 0.902 from ref_rear34.jpg. out/r49s_low34.png shows a plainly warm tan top plane. counter_top() and t1_mats.counter_tan() have both shipped -- the 'empty until t1_mats ships countertan' comment at t1_detail.py:3360 is stale.
* gal_warmer is correctly sized and its knob is correctly placed. Width 269 mm from image x 641-698, and the round latch measures at image (668, 402) -> z 1.314 against the built gal_warmer_tap at z 1.3100. Its height carries the DECLARED 54 mm parallax absorption and is consistent with it (photographed 175 mm + 54 = 229 against 241 built).
* The hanging tools in bay 3 have the RIGHT polarity, contrary to how they look. Rendered they read L 189.1 / 192.1 against a bay-3 wall at 172.2; photographed 186.8 / 188.5 against a wall at 176.0. Both are BRIGHTER than the wall by a similar margin. I raised this as a candidate and it did not survive.
* The menu card's band structure renders as designed -- dark script logo band, red header, striped warm body, red footer, cream head margin -- and matches ref_side.jpg's layout at 13x. The only unbuilt element is the thin red KEYLINE that frames the card on all four sides in the photograph; at 20 px card width that is ~1 px per side and I am not calling it a defect.
* The tail-run bottles' longitudinal station is corroborated: after the rev-16 tail shift they sit at X -1.625..-1.820, which is exactly where ref_side.jpg's condiment rank ends (-1.58 to -1.63). The rev-16 mechanism that carries them with X_TAIL is working.

**Decals, signwriting and typography**
* THE BURST'S SPIKE PROFILE ON THE RED BUS IS RIGHT — do not reopen it. I measured tooth PITCH along the burst's left edge (a spatial frequency, so blur cannot move it), 8x LANCZOS, boundary = the leftmost (R-G)>T pixel per row: ref_side.jpg 12 tips over 64 src px = 5.33 px pitch; ref_rear34.jpg 9 tips over 70 px = 7.78; the build's texture downsampled to the photograph's 102 px burst width 10 tips over 66 px = 6.62. The build sits BETWEEN the two red frames. LEDGER_rev48's ruling survives. I also tried area/ellipse and solidity and abandoned both: the white type bites the red mask and any closing big enough to fill it also erases the 2-4 px teeth.
* cal_gen's RED (214,46,30) at gradient bias 0.00 — the core renders in the same red family as the coachwork (G/R 0.215 vs t1_mats 0.250), and the render's burst core is red, not peach. The rev-45 bias fix holds.
* The burst's type angle and layout: -19.7 deg, two lines, '100%' over 'Calidad', counters punched. Matches ref_side.jpg and ref_rear34.jpg at 8x.
* The star BAND's x-extent: the build's band position #0 lands at (cx - 0.64 bw, cy - 0.46 bh); the photographed mark is at (-0.657 bw, -0.423 bh). The band's horizontal placement is right — only its scale and the SECOND, spurious star below the burst are wrong.
* LINE_GAP = 0.43 sits inside the red bus's own hand-read bound of 0.25-0.47. Blocked on the darker exposure, as recorded. I did not attempt to re-derive it and I did not touch the green bus's 0.244/0.376.
* The measured GOLD AREA of the flank rosettes is right. My independent gate (G/R>0.42, ref_side.jpg) gives ~3500 mm^2 at X = -1.688 against FLANK_ROSETTES's 3564 mm^2 — 2 %. Only the area -> drawn-diameter step is wrong.
* The header's word RUNS reproduce the photograph. FRESH 0.1150-0.2375, JUICES, 0.2454-0.3858, GOURMET TACOS 0.3896-0.6792, & 0.6908-0.7383, TORTAS 0.7529-0.8938 all land within a pixel of lid_gen's own rectified measurements, and the tight 'JUICES,GOURMET' spacing is MEASURED (photograph 9 px of 2400; build 9.1) — not a defect.
* script_gen's silver-albedo reasoning is sound and its near-white texture value is INTENTIONAL: a rendered ratio between a near-mirror metal and a diffuse dielectric is not an albedo ratio (script_gen.py:678-702). Do not 'darken the silver to match the photograph'.
* TARNISH_K's warm brown is right. At 11x the photograph's 'Señor' is warm brown-olive with silver highlights, not the green-black the record once carried.
* 'Tacombi' itself is right and was never the problem. At the identical measured ink bbox the T's stem and swash, the rolled swash terminal above the c, the spiral counters in a/c/o, the m's three feet, the b's flag and the i's fused head all correspond to the photograph. Do not spend a revision on it.
* The hubcap VW emblem's size: render 21.7 px at 271.19 px/m = 80 mm against ref_side.jpg's 18 px at 219 px/m = 82 mm. CAP_EMBLEM_D = 0.317 x CAP_D holds.
* The flower mural and the yellow menu strips as a composition are as ruled. My finding on the top strip is about ONE unclamped font scale inside them, not about the mural, the strips' existence, the side strips' sequence, or the vignettes.

**Materials and shading (t1_mats.py) — body paint, cream family, chrome/brightwork, rubber/tyre, glass, emissive bulbs, the WEATHER weathering**
* apply_weather links WEATHER's Metallic output ONLY when wear > 0 (t1_mats.py:1143-1144), so silver_script's Metallic 0.780 and every unworn material survive the splice intact. I went looking for a clobber here and there isn't one.
* round_edges (t1_mats.py:2102-2140) re-routes any existing Normal source into the Bevel node's own Normal input rather than discarding it, so WEATHER's orange-peel bump composes with the fold radius instead of being replaced; the radius is expressed as t1_shell.GAPW/2, derived not typed. Correct as built.
* THE TYRE/RUBBER SPECKLE IS MOSTLY MONTE-CARLO, NOT THE SHADER. I started a finding on it and dropped it: base-vs-board pair difference over tyre windows gives sd 4.9-6.0 DN (per-image ~3.5-4.2 DN) against a 5-px high-frequency residual of 8.6 DN. Do not respec dust_film() on the strength of what the tyre looks like at 96 spp.
* THE BULB EMISSION IS RIGHT IN HUE. Render bulb peaks (top 1 % of a drip-rail band, out/r49s_counter.png) RGB (249.2, 243.8, 236.7); ref_side.jpg bulb strings (255, 244, 231) and (255, 247, 231). emissive('bulb', (1.000,0.760,0.442), strength 9.0) needs nothing. Nothing clips in the render's bulbs.
* THE CREAM READING NEAR-NEUTRAL GREY IN A WHITE STUDIO IS CORRECT AND MUST NOT BE 'WARMED UP'. CREAM is locked at sRGB(206,208,200) with G > R; every photograph's warm cream is the illuminant, and this file's own von-Kries gain at t1_mats.py:428-431, (0.9676, 0.7784, 0.4976), puts the reference illuminant's blue at half its red. Measured render fascia (190.5, 189.0, 186.3) against ref_side.jpg's (214, 191, 170) is the light, not the paint.
* THE RED'S SPECULAR STRUCTURE IS NOT WRONG, ONLY ITS LEVEL. C*/(L*+16) by L* quintile falls 1.186 -> 0.897 (-24 %) across ref_rear34.jpg's rear quarter and 0.780 -> 0.622 (-20 %) across the render's flank. The lobe shape matches; do not chase 'the paint needs more gloss'.
* MONTE-CARLO NOISE IS NOT A CONFOUND ON CREAM IN THE SHIPPED SIDE RENDER. r49base_side minus r49board_side over the counter fascia is sd 0.465 DN, i.e. <=0.33 DN per image (0.25 % of a 189 mean). Denoising is on (studio.py:1144-1146, OPENIMAGEDENOISE, RGB_ALBEDO_NORMAL). Texture-residual work on cream in out/ is not noise-limited.
* NEITHER DUST BRANCH REACHES THE COUNTER OR THE FLANK ABOVE z 0.48 m. `hgt` is zero above W_DUST_Z_HI = 0.480, `upn` is zero on a horizontal normal, and W_DUST_FAC_UP is retired to 0.0 — so W_DUST_* is not the lever for anything seen on the counter, and rev 29's retirement is intact.
* THE ORANGE-PEEL SCALE IS PHYSICALLY SANE. W_PEEL_SCALE 220 on Object coordinates is a 4.5 mm base cell with a 2.3 mm second octave at Detail 2, inside the real automotive long-wave/short-wave band, and metric because it is fed Object rather than Generated. The rev-3 anisotropy bug is genuinely fixed.
* silver_script's tarnish->roughness map survives the weather splice: t1_mats.py:1110-1122's rev-12 re-route is live, so the leaf's 0.520 -> 0.205 ramp reaches Roughness through the group rather than being replaced by a flat 0.260.
* Subsurface Weight is 0.0 on every material by construction in _nt() (t1_mats.py:695), not by a per-material assignment that could be missed.
* The main shell's paint is CLEAN and should stay that way — nose cream 0.01 % and cab side cream 0.00 % dark-speck coverage in out/r49s_front.png. Whatever is done about finding 1 must not touch the shell, which is the one surface where the Pointiness design works as intended (flank pw 0.5002 against the 0.520 gate, t1_mats.py:230-242).

**Lighting, shadow, camera and optics**
* THE CONTACT SHADOW'S MAGNITUDE IS DELIVERED ON THE DIRECT PATH AND MATCHES ITS DECLARED TARGET. out/r49s_hero34f.png, ground window rows 950-972 x cols 700-780 just outboard of the near front tyre = 226.09 DN against open ground (rows 930-990, cols 1150-1400) 255.00 -> 0.8866; tightened to rows 947-955 -> 0.8463. SPEC 10.116.5 claims 0.8729. The gain works. Do not re-tune T1_SHADOW - finding 2 is about the shadow's EDGE, not its depth.
* THE HERO CAMERA ELEVATIONS ARE RIGHT AND 'the reference-photo angle' IS A TRUE CLAIM. Computed from studio.views(): hero34f 8.37 deg above its target, hero 8.37, hero34r 9.44, front34 6.94, low34 1.01. The one camera this project ever RECOVERED from a photograph, playa_ref at loc (-4.829, 2.222, 1.900) -> tgt (-0.55,-0.10,1.28), is 7.25 deg. The studio heroes bracket it. The camera HEIGHT differs (3.1-3.8 m vs 1.90 m) only because the distance is 3x larger; the angle, which is what the eye reads, matches. Do not move the heroes down.
* THE COMPOSITOR BLOOM IS CONTRIBUTING NOTHING AND IS NOT THE W6 VEIL. Two independent proofs: (a) the flank P1 measured on r49shut2_hero34r WITH the full FX chain on is 0.4536, identical to the published 0.455 measured on a side ortho with T1_FX=0 - a live veiling glare would have separated them; (b) scanning up from the roof at four columns each in r49s_front, r49s_rear and r49base_side, the backdrop returns to exactly 255.0 within 2-5 px of the last subject pixel, with no halo at any radius out to 200 px. AUDIT_rev43's 'the shipped frame carries no veiling glare at all' holds on the direct path too. Do not spend a revision ablating T1_BLOOM against W6.
* THE CHROMATIC ABERRATION IS INSIDE A DEFENSIBLE BAND ON BOTH PATHS. Measured on 11 achromatic shadow-terminator crossings in out/r49s_low34.png (sub-pixel 50% crossings per channel, radii 465-860 px): |R-B| = 0.0012*r px, i.e. about 1.15 px at the 971 px frame corner. post.py's CA_COEF=0.0011 gives 2*0.0011*r = 2.1 px at the same radius. post.py's own note says 'a good 78 mm prime gives 1-2 px there'. Both paths are admissible; they differ by about 2x, which is worth unifying but is not a defect.
* POST.PY'S HEURISTIC BACKDROP MASK IS NOT MISCLASSIFYING ANYTHING ON TODAY'S GEOMETRY. I ran post.backdrop_mask itself (level 250, chroma 3, border-connected, erode 2, feather 1.5) on four shipped frames and compared against the bit-exact-white border-connected set: r49shut2_hero34r 13 px, r49s_hero34f 21 px, r49s_low34 76 px, r49s_counter 13 px called backdrop but not bit-exact white - 0.001-0.004% of frame. No bulb specular from the festoon string or the new rev-49 tail board touches the frame border. AUDIT_rev43's line-163 concern is real in principle and not live in practice.
* THE DEPTH OF FIELD IS PHYSICALLY CORRECT EVERYWHERE, AND ITS INERTNESS ON THE HEROES IS PHYSICS, NOT A BUG. hero34f: 78 mm f/8 focused 13.71 m on ARCH_F, hyperfocal 25.43 m, sharp 8.92-29.50 m; the whole vehicle sits 11-18 m from that camera, so the largest defocus circle on it is 0.0137 mm = 0.61 px at 1600 px wide (1.15 px at a 3000 px delivery) - below the 1.50 px reconstruction filter. detail_f (100 mm f/6.3 at 3.60 m, band 3.38-3.85 m) and counter (90 mm f/6.3 at 5.30 m, band 4.72-6.02 m) do deliver real defocus and it is plainly visible in out/r49s_detail_f.png. The module docstring's founding line 'A pinhole renders the tail as sharp as the near arch. No lens does that' overstates it for f/8, but the simulation is right and should not be 'fixed' by opening the aperture.
* THE SHADOW CATCHER IS WRITING REAL, STRUCTURED ALPHA - the rev-12 'alpha is identically zero' line stays retracted. The pool in r49s_hero34f runs from 209 DN immediately below the front tyre through 216/220/225/229/233 to 241 DN about 160 px (roughly 0.76 m) out; the near-field occlusion gradient exists and is monotone. The defect is at its outer boundary, not in its absence.
* THE 90 m FLAT CYCLORAMA (not a curved cyc wall) IS THE RIGHT CALL AND ITS DOCSTRING'S REASON IS SOUND - a curved wall sits between the subject and any orthographic camera placed outside it, which is what blanked the front elevation. The front, rear and side orthos in out/ all render cleanly with the backdrop coming from the compositor. Do not reintroduce a wall.
* THE INSTRUMENTATION IS NOT THE PROBLEM: T1_SOFTEN, T1_CYCALB, T1_CATCH, T1_SHADOW, T1_SHADOW_FLOOR, T1_FX, T1_BGW, T1_CA, T1_VIG, T1_GRAIN, T1_BLOOM, T1_FSTOP, T1_FILTER, T1_EXP, T1_VT and T1_NOCABFILL all exist as real environment switches with sane defaults, so every claim in this dimension is one variable away from an A/B.

**THE FIDELITY BAR AUDIT — painted-on / flat-sweep features vs real geometry with depth, across t1_detail.py, t1_shell.py, texgen.py and tex/,**
* SHUT LINES ARE REAL CUT GEOMETRY, NOT PAINTED LINES. t1_shell.door_gaps() (:808), cargo_door_gaps() (:978) and engine_lid_gap() (:994) all use T.gap_prism booleans through the shell, and split_trunk_lid() (t1_shell.py:2003) separates the engine lid as its own object. In probe_scratch/rev50_fidelity-bar_h34r_tail_x3.png the lid line reads as a genuine dark gap with rounded corners. Do not re-open this.
* THE CAB DOOR HINGES ARE REAL PROUD HARDWARE, correctly built and correctly derived. t1_detail.py:1559-1594: two butt hinges with a barrel (R 0.0112, 76 mm long) and a leaf, standing 13.5 mm proud, seated on the body by T.flank_y and axis-aligned to the door's own shut-line rake, placed at the quarter points of that edge rather than typed. Nothing to do here.
* THE NUMBER PLATE IS RIGHT, INCLUDING BEING EMPTY. t1_detail.plate_1963() (:2552) builds four chrome rails as real prisms with an EMPTY aperture and puts a schematic '1963' on the TOP RAIL. probe_scratch/rev50_fidelity-bar_ref_plate_x6.png confirms exactly that in ref_rear34.jpg — an empty chrome frame with '1963' embossed on its top rail and dark lettering on the bottom rail. I went looking for a missing plate face and there is none to add.
* THE SCROLLWORK, THE SENOR TACOMBI SCRIPT AND THE CALIDAD BURST ARE CORRECTLY TEXTURES. tex/swirl.png, tex/senor.png, tex/calidad.png on conformed panels 1.6-4.0 mm proud (build.py:826-841, t1_detail.conform_panel_true at :2090). On the vehicle these are signwritten paint with no relief — ref_side.jpg and ref_rear34.jpg show the yellow scrollwork and the flowers as flat colour with no shading break of their own. Texture is the RIGHT choice here; this is not a fidelity-bar violation and should not be converted to geometry.
* THE TYRE'S TREAD GROOVES ARE MODELLED, NOT PAINTED. t1_detail.py:56-63 cuts four circumferential grooves 8 mm deep into a crowned band. The lateral pattern is missing (my finding 5) but the grooves themselves are real geometry and rev 44's criterion 3 is met on that point.
* THE VW ROUNDEL AND ITS GLYPH ARE REAL MITRED PRISMS WITH DEPTH, AND THEIR PROUDNESS IS GUARDED. t1_detail.roundel() (:929), vw_logo_fit() (:1916), draped onto the nose by T.drape_x with build.py:759-767 asserting every front-face vertex is between 0.5 mm and 30 mm proud. probe_scratch/rev50_fidelity-bar_ov_detf.png shows the badge standing off the nose with its own wall and shadow.
* THE HUBCAP EMBLEM RING AND GLYPH ARE COPLANAR REAL GEOMETRY sized as a fraction of the cap (t1_detail.py:274-327, CAP_EMBLEM_D = 0.3170*CAP_D, CAP_RING_BANDFRAC = 0.093). That work is sound; my finding 1 is about the cap being OCCLUDED, not about the emblem, and the emblem is the one part of the wheel that renders correctly.
* THE COUNTER NOSING IS A REAL SWEPT ROLL, NOT A PAINTED GOLD STRIPE. t1_detail.counter_nosing() (:3381) sweeps a six-point profile with a crown, a face and a drip, carrying its own brass material. Its depth fraction (CNT_NOSE_F) is measured. No action.
* THE BAY AND WINDSCREEN SEALS ARE REAL RINGS WITH THICKNESS, not painted outlines: t1_shell.bay_seals() (:214) and windscreen_seals() (:41) use T.conform_ring at 7.5 mm width and 9.0 mm thickness. Visible as real rubber in probe_scratch/rev50_fidelity-bar_ov_detf.png.
* THE MISSING REAR BUMPER IS CORRECT AND RULED. build.py:364-366 keeps it commented out per SPEC rev6 sec.2.4 ('removed after the conversion'), and probe_scratch/rev50_fidelity-bar_ref_tailwide_x2.png confirms there is no rear bumper on the vehicle. Do not add one.
* THE WHEEL-HOUSE LINERS ARE REAL SHELLS THAT FOLLOW THE FLANK SKIN, not a fixed-y skirt: t1_detail._arc_liner() (:1217) sweeps the arch sector only (not a full revolve) with its outboard face on T.flank_y. The docstring records both earlier failures and both were caught by looking. Sound.
* THE LOUVRES SELF-SHADOW AND THE APERTURES ARE REAL CUTS, as rev 48 built them; I re-checked the block in out/r49shut2_hero34r.png (probe_scratch/rev50_fidelity-bar_h34r_flank_x2.png) and it reads as a dark slot band with negative modulation. The rev-49 survey's findings 3, 4, 5 and 9 already cover everything still open on the louvres and I found nothing to add to them.

**THE RECORD — every pose choice, every unmeasured constant, and every claim standing as fact without a measurement**
* THE TAIL BOARD'S HEIGHT CHAIN CLOSES, TWICE, INDEPENDENTLY. Photographed base 1.747 ± 0.027 m against the roof skin at the solved station 1.7497 = 2.7 mm; the chord then lands the tip at 2.2001 against a measured 2.184 ± 0.030 = 16 mm, inside the band. The station is derived from T1_body's own vertices at run time, not typed. Do not re-open this; the 80 mm inconsistency is genuinely dissolved (only the stale sentence at t1_shell.py:1827 remains).
* ref_rear34.jpg REALLY CANNOT CLOSE THE BOARD'S WIDTH — I looked, not just computed. Crop (930,0)-(1200,260) at 4× (probe_scratch/rev50_the-record_rear34_tailboard.png) shows the board's mitred corner, its bulb-lined long edge running down-left, and its SHORT (width-direction) edge running down-right and exiting at the frame's right border. The source's claim at t1_shell.py:1563 survives being looked at.
* "EVERY FRAME IN THE PROJECT IS OF THE SERVING SIDE" IS TRUE. Verified on a contact sheet of all nine distinct frames (probe_scratch/rev50_the-record_contact_sheet.png). ref_nolita_flank.jpg is NOT mirrored — TACOMBI.COM and 267 ELIZABETH STREET read left-to-right, the cab door and front wheel are at the left, the nose is off-frame left. The off-side grading E is correctly blocked.
* THE FIVE BYTE-IDENTICAL DUPLICATE PAIRS ARE EXACTLY AS RECORDED. md5 confirmed all five: IMG_3842=ref_playa_34, IMG_2054=ref_nolita_flank, IMG_2053=ref_nolita_front34b, IMG_2060=ref_nolita_front34, IMG_3840=ref_nolita_doorshut. Rule 13's checksum half is discharged; only the same-photograph-at-two-resolutions case (finding 4) was missed.
* TRUNK_OPEN_DEG's OWN BLOCK (t1_shell.py:1289-1302) IS THE MODEL RETRACTION. It names the ruling verbatim, names the inference it refutes, names the rule that was broken, explains why 0.0 SKIPS the swing rather than running it at zero, and keeps the capability one constant away. The two stale copies in finding 9 are elsewhere; this site is right.
* SPEC §8.1's SUBSTANCE HOLDS AT TWICE THE RESOLUTION. Re-derived on ref_playa_34.png: luminance rises monotonically 80→124 from the hub out to r=17 with saturation 0.87-0.95, then the pale annulus at lum 167 / sat 0.32. There is NO dark rubber between the hubcap and the pale annulus, which is the whole of the blackwall-plus-painted-rim argument. The lock is right; only its provenance record is deficient.
* verify._bounds() IS CORRECTLY BUILT. It reads VERTICES rather than ob.bound_box (which goes stale after in-place edits, and had been hiding 295 mm of glass_rear), and it excludes via the t1_shell.SWUNG / NOT_BODYWORK registries that parts join themselves rather than an enumerated list. Its own docstring records both defects it was built to fix. Do not re-litigate.
* THE LENGTH ROW IS ALREADY HONEST ABOUT ITS FORWARD END. verify.py:766-768 states plainly that row 1's L cannot carry the measurement because X_NOSE has never been measured, and puts the real, dimensionless measurement in row 1b (rear overhang / wheelbase = 0.3412 ± 0.0015 in the image → 0.3221 in the world). Do NOT report "the length is unmeasured" as a new finding; report only that the headline "4.065 vs spec 4.055" is quoted without that caveat.
* THE FOUR "DECLARES ITSELF" ROWS THAT EXIST ARE REAL GUARDS, NOT TAUTOLOGIES. Each greps the constant's own following comment block for its marker string (verify_clone.sh:395, 425, 456, 469). They are the right pattern; the defect is that the pattern was not extended (finding 7).
* THE TAIL BOARD'S RIM IS ON THE FAR LONG EDGE AND THE RENDER CONFIRMS IT. tail_board_edge sets ey = TB_Y_CENTRE − TB_WIDTH/2, and probe_scratch/rev50_the-record_render_tailboard.png (out/r49d_hero34r.png at 4×) shows a thin red line on the far edge only, with the bulb string on the near edge. Source and render agree; the docstring's "which long edge" reasoning is sound.
* THE STAY IS BUILT TO ITS MEASURED LANDING AND REPORTS ITS RESIDUAL RATHER THAN FORCING IT. t1_shell.py:1847-1849 pins zb = 1.5780 and xb = X_TAIL (both measured) and lets the angle come out, logging it against the measured 77.5° with the depth-plane ambiguity named as the cause. That is the correct handling of an unmeasurable; only the "80 mm" sentence above it is wrong.
* STATE.md's PROVENANCE TABLE IS ITSELF HONEST — it prints its own generating commit, its own timestamp and "working tree DIRTY — this state is not committed". That table is the ONLY reason the staleness in finding 6 is detectable at all. Keep it; regenerate the file.

## EACH SURVEY'S OWN CEILING

**The flank, belt line and louvres — side elevation of the RED target bus (ref_side.jpg / ref_rear34.jpg), with geometry-only corroboration fr**  
Three things would make this survey wrong. (1) EVERY absolute height I quote rides on K_T = 215.5 px/m carried off the rear hub by the SPEC 10.35 map's depth ratio, with the hub at z = TIRE_R = 0.3325 by construction. flank_compare.py:218-232 records that the two calibrated instruments disagree by 2.3 % at the hub AND that the sign of the disagreement is wrong, so one of them is out. I then found the error is not a pure scale: applied to the SPEC 10.34 drip-rail fit (v = -0.04409u + 332.301) the same instrument makes the drip rail FALL 42 mm going aft where a body line must RISE 47 mm — an 89 mm differential error at z 1.78, which scales to about 47 mm at the fascia's z 1.09. That control KILLED a finding I had already written up: "the counter fascia is level in the photograph (1.0916 +- 0.0018 flat) and raked in the render (1.0850 -> 1.1283, slope 0.0164 m/m = RAKE_DZDX), a 34 mm differential at the tail". The claimed effect and the instrument's own demonstrated error are the same size, so I dropped it. Differences taken at ONE station (the block's own height, the strip's brightness ratio, the slot pitch) are largely immune; anything quoted as a slope or an absolute height along the flank is not. (2) I also dropped "the belt line sits ~157 mm too high above the louvre block", read off IMG_2073's white/green break sitting 8.5 px above an 81 px block: the identity of that white band (body belt vs counter fascia) cannot be settled in that frame, and reading it as the fascia bottom reproduces ref_side's independently measured 1.091 exactly. Under rule 26 the green bus's paint break is artwork and cannot carry a belt-line number anyway. (3) The whole survey collapses if rev 10's camera recovery for ref_side.jpg (-4.829, +2.222, 1.900) or the SPEC 10.35 flank map is wrong. The map has exactly ONE out-of-sample validation — the rear arch's aft foot at -1.5615 against an independently measured -1.560 — and that point is at u = 852, just past the louvre block, which is the only reason I was willing to quote block stations at all. Finally: I ran nothing that imports bpy, rendered nothing, and edited no project file; all scratch is under probe_scratch/ prefixed rev50_flank-belt_ (crops) and rev50_ (scripts). I re-listed out/ at the end and picked up the newly-landed r49s_hero34f.png, which corroborates finding 1 from a second viewpoint.

**The nose and front: nose face and plan crown (W4), V swage, VW roundel and bars, headlamps and bowls, indicators, front blade/bumper, windsc**  
Everything here rests on one identity: that a circular feature's image aspect ratio equals |cos(angle between its axis and the ray to it)|. That holds for a planar circle whose rim is the visible boundary, and the perspective correction is negligible at these radii and depths (< 0.1%). It FAILS if the real T1 headlamp aperture carries a raised, rolled flange whose outer edge silhouettes as a convex torus -- in which case the aperture reads round from any angle, finding 1 collapses from 'the nose is not crowned' into 'the bezel is not proud enough', and the fix is a different one in the same subsystem. Only a grazing frame across the front face separates them, and no frame we hold does. Second ceiling: every METRIC number in findings 4, 6 and 7 is scaled by ROUNDEL_D = 0.280 m, which was itself derived from ref_workshop.jpg -- if the roundel is not 280 mm, all of them move together (the ASPECT RATIOS in finding 1 and the source facts in findings 2, 3, 5 and 7 do not). Third: the ray-spread window that finding 1 turns on depends on the focal length of a camera nobody recorded; I bounded it over f = 800..3000 px and the finding survives at every value, but it would not survive a fisheye. Fourth: I could not run Blender this pass, so no number here was taken off the mesh -- the model side is read from source constants and from the shipped renders, and two of those constants (the nose skin's x at the indicator station, and the exact panel normal at the lamp) are inferred from probe_rev45_nose's published raycast rather than measured afresh."

**THE TAIL AND REAR — tail panel, engine lid + shut line, T-handle, 1963 plate, tail lamps, rear window aperture and what shows through it, re**  
EVERY VERTICAL FIGURE IN THIS SURVEY IS SCALED BY ONE OBJECT: the 1963 plate frame, whose outer height 0.16823 m is itself a rev-15 derivation (PLATE_ASPECT 1.9616, with a Monte-Carlo 16-84% band of 1.12-1.66 on the rectification route and a wheel-protractor route that gave 1.962 +- 0.034). If PLATE_OUTER_H is materially wrong, my millimetre figures scale with it — though the two findings that matter survive that: the T-handle-outside-the-lid is two constants in the repository with no photograph involved, and the rear aperture's darkness is measured against the model's own side bays in one render. THE SURVEY WOULD BE WRONG WHOLESALE IF the plate is NOT on the engine lid's vertical centreline region and I have mis-assigned which panel each seam belongs to — i.e. if the lines at y 575 / 595.5 / 752.5 are not the lid's outline. I tested that with the lid's own height (3.045 vs 3.005 plate-heights, +1.3%) and it held; nothing else I could run would have caught it. SECOND CEILING, and I want it on the record because I hit it and stopped: the plate ruler DOES NOT survive extrapolation upward. Carried 0.6-1.0 m above the plate it puts the rear aperture's top at z 1.85, which is above the vehicle's own locked drip-rail datum of 1.7485 and therefore impossible; it also puts the counter 180 mm above CNT_ZT, one of the project's most carefully re-derived constants. Some combination of the tail panel's lean and my ruler is failing over that span. So I DROPPED two findings I had numbers for — 'the rear aperture is 0.42 m tall against the built 0.34' and 'there is an unbuilt glazed panel above it' — because both depend on that extrapolation. They are not refuted; they are unmeasurable with the instruments I have, and the next revision should not resurrect them without a vertical ruler that lives in the UPPER half of the tail. THIRD: I did not resolve whether the counter's tail run should project 0.29 m past the OFF-side flank as it does (t1_detail.py:1063-1068 declares the whole tail wrap INFERRED, not measured); ref_rear34.jpg cuts off before the off-side corner and no other frame we hold sees it, so it stays open."

**The roof, lids and drip rail — roof skin/crown, roof aperture, raised mural lid (geometry, hinge, struts, junction), drip rail/gutter, bulb **  
Everything I measured on the RED target comes from ref_side.jpg, which SPEC.md:4694 records as the worst frame this project owns — 2.32 bits/px at JPEG DC quantiser 4, against 9.28 for ref_rear34.jpg. Two things would make this whole survey wrong. (1) THE CAMERA. Every metric claim about the lid rests on a pinhole model of that frame with no recovered distance and no recovered distortion; I sidestepped the distance by quoting a ratio and by bracketing over 6-13 m, but if the frame carries significant barrel distortion or a large yaw, the taper of finding 1 and the width of finding 5 both move — although I checked that every distortion and pitch I can name biases AGAINST my conclusion, not for it. A single frame of that bus with any object of known size at the board's depth collapses both brackets at once. (2) THE OCCLUDERS. This frame has a vendor standing in the roof opening, a man leaning on the flank and a palm crossing the board, and one of my four candidate findings died precisely because a pale band turned out to be the vendor's shirt — I found that only by cropping to 12x and LOOKING. Any edge I fitted through rows the vendor touches inherits that risk. Finally, my two source-level findings (the degenerate lid rails and the tautological tail-board guard) do not depend on any photograph and would survive both; they depend instead on my reading of Blender's `validate()` and of the loft's crown, and I could not run Blender to watch either one, so they are argued from t1_core.py:485-487 and from the ortho render rather than from a printed vertex list."

**Wheels, tyres, hubcaps and stance**  
Everything photographic here rests on treating ref_side.jpg as NEAR-AFFINE at the rear wheel. I justified that two ways — the rim flange fits a circle to 0.527 px rms over 356 rays (a perspective ellipse at that obliquity would not), and the sunlit ground row is essentially the same (668 vs 668-670) under both axles — but I did not model the projection. If the frame carries a material perspective term at wheel scale, the hub-height / rim-radius ratio that drives finding 2 acquires an unmodelled correction and several of the 30 mm of squat could be projection rather than rubber. Second, every metre figure I quote on that frame uses 211.5 px/m, a scale I inherited from the project (LOFT sec.0.4's k_t = 215.5 ± 3.0 at u = 749) and cross-checked against the 2.400 m wheelbase but did not derive; all the dimensionless ratios survive its being wrong, none of the millimetre figures do. Third, finding 2's ground row is a single luminance step at row 668 ± 1.5 in a region where tyre, arch shadow and ground shadow are all the same tone — I proved the step is straight over 125 px and therefore a shadow edge, and argued from the sun geometry that the contact must lie at or above it, but a photograph of this vehicle in flat light, or any frame shot level with the wheel centre, would replace that argument with a measurement. Finally, findings 5 and 6 are absences of evidence rather than measured errors, and both are closed by the same missing frame: a square-on front or rear ELEVATION of the RED bus with the tyres visible against the flank.

**Glazing, seals, mirrors and wipers**  
Three things would make this whole survey wrong. (1) EVERY millimetre figure I quote is scaled by a ruler taken from the model itself -- the cab-door glazed span 0.730 m, the windscreen pane's 0.335 m vertical projection, REAR_H 0.340 m, the aperture height 0.394-0.403 m. None of those has a measurement provenance in SPEC (rev 23 records the door's rear x had none, and the vent outline and Z_SILL/Z_HEAD are likewise unattributed). If the ruler moves, every millimetre here moves with it. The findings that survive a bad ruler are the scale-free ones -- the mirror arm's drop in head-diameters (1.14 vs 0.17), the vent fraction (0.27-0.34 vs 0.20), and the presence/absence findings (wipers, rear seal, windscreen fringe) -- and those are the ones I would act on first. (2) RESOLUTION. ref_playa_34.png is 500 px for a 4 m vehicle (~140 px/m) and REFERENCE_FRAMES_rev45.md:77 explicitly warns it is unusable for anything needing better than 1 px; ref_nolita_doorshut.jpg is 480 px. A 10 mm part is 1.4 px there. I have leaned on those frames for absence (a 300 mm wiper arm is 42 px long and cannot hide) far more than for size, but every size number off them carries +/-2 px = +/-14 mm at best. Only ref_workshop.jpg and ref_rear34.jpg are high-resolution, and the first is the wrong vehicle stage and the second is a rear 3/4. (3) I DID NOT RUN BLENDER, so the two causal claims are read off source rather than measured: that the 0.05 world plus a compositor-only backdrop is why the panes carry no reflection, and that flat-shading a 6.7 deg normal swing facets the reflection. Both are physically forced by what studio.py and build.py actually do, but neither has an A/B behind it, and finding 2's fix collides head-on with W6's locked-down world strength -- if the honest answer turns out to be that no admissible world setting both saturates the paint and lights the glass, then the glazing problem is not a glazing problem at all and belongs entirely to whoever rebuilds the rig as a dome."

**The counter, galley and props (counter slab / nosing / top, galley interior seen through the three serving bays, the equipment and counter-t**  
Everything here rests on ONE image-to-model map per frame. For the render it is image_x = 800.7 - 268.9*X and image_y = 637.0 + 268.9*(1.2540 - Z), anchored on the two bay pitches and on the counter's own gold line; it is validated by reproducing the slab thickness to 0.8 mm and the bay widths to 1%, and if the side camera is not as close to orthographic as its two agreeing pitches suggest, every aperture-fraction figure I quote moves. For ref_side.jpg it is the source's own 211.9 px/m from the gal_warmer conversion, which the source itself says falls to ~195 px/m at the rear panel -- so every X I convert AFT of the warmer is a bound with a stated span, not a point, and the condiment rank's aft end (-1.58 to -1.63) is the widest of those spans. The whole survey would also be wrong if ref_side.jpg's apertures are not the ones REF_MEASUREMENTS sec.4/5 measured (I took x 323-430 / 455-564 / 588-699 on trust, not by re-fitting the cut edges), because every u-fraction in findings 1, 2 and 6 is computed from them. Finally, the ~106 mm frame error (finding 1) is the load-bearing claim, and it depends entirely on rev 13's ruling that REF's X = (495.8 - u)/211.5 is 100 mm aft; if that ruling is ever overturned, finding 1 inverts and it is the APERTURES that are 105 mm forward of the fit-out, not the fit-out that is aft of them. I did not run Blender and did not re-render anything, so no arm here is an ablation -- every render figure is read off the already-shipped frames in out/."

**Decals, signwriting and typography**  
Everything here would be wrong if the two admissible RED CURRENT frames are not measurable where I measured them. Both are blown in the highlights — 60.8 % of the white lettering in ref_side.jpg is clipped — so I deliberately took NO reading off white type: every number above comes from a red/gold chroma difference against a cream or red ground, where both frames still carry 60-100 DN of signal. The second exposure is that all absolute mm figures on the flank ride on a px/m I inherited (194.8 rear panel / 211.5 mid-body, cal_gen.py:29-35) and cross-checked only against TIRE_R = 0.3325, itself a rev-6 project measurement — so every millimetre figure carries about ±6 % from scale alone, and any finding that depended on a factor smaller than 1.15 I dropped rather than reported (that is what killed the spike-depth and the header-face-weight claims). The third and largest: findings 3 and 4 are measured in TEXTURE space (tex/lidmural.png, tex/calidad.png, tex/swirl.png) rather than in a render, because I am forbidden to run Blender. A defect that the material, the UV mapping or the lighting introduces, hides or already compensates would not show up in a texture-space measurement — I corroborated 2, 3 and 4 in the shipped frames by eye and 4 by a render-space measurement at the verified 271.19 px/m, but a texture that is right and a render that is wrong (or the reverse) is a failure mode this survey cannot see. Fourth: I never re-derived senor_trace's segmentation, so finding 1 rests on the source's own declaration that its breaks are tarnish artefacts, not on my having proved the paint is continuous — I looked for that proof in the blue channel and did not get it.

**Materials and shading (t1_mats.py) — body paint, cream family, chrome/brightwork, rubber/tyre, glass, emissive bulbs, the WEATHER weathering**  
Every photographic half of this survey rests on comparing local-median residual statistics between a Cycles/AgX+Punchy render at 271.2 px/m and 2007-era camera JPEGs at 211.4 px/m — two different resolutions, two different tone curves, and one of them with unknown in-camera noise reduction. If ref_side.jpg's counter-fascia band happens to be a smoothed or locally clipped region of that JPEG, its 2.5-3.4 DN row sd is an artefact and the 30x coverage gap shrinks. I checked for clipping (mean 194, no values near 255) but I cannot check for NR from a single frame, and that is the single assumption that would most damage findings 1 and 2. What protects finding 1 is that its core is an INTERNAL control — one material, one frame, 0.01 % against 22.44 % — which needs no photograph at all; if the photographic half failed, the mechanism would survive and only the claim about how much chipping belongs there would fall. Finding 3's arithmetic assumes T1_SOFTEN and T1_CYCALB changed only the light and not the exposure compensation or the view transform; if either arm also moved the film settings, my d(G/R)/dR is not a level slope and the 15-34 % range is meaningless. Finding 5 is lighting-confounded by construction and I have said so. Finally, every 'the photograph shows clean paint' statement is a statement about the vehicle ON THE DAY OF THAT FRAME: ref_side.jpg, ref_rear34.jpg and ref_playa_34.png are all the target artwork state, but nothing in the repository establishes they are the same month, and a working food truck can be repainted between them — so a chipped model is not refuted by a clean photograph if the photograph is the newer one. And I ran no Blender: every claim about what a node does is read off the source, not off a rendered pass, so an error in my reading of the WEATHER graph would propagate straight into finding 1's mechanism (though not into its measurements)."

**Lighting, shadow, camera and optics**  
Everything here was measured on 8-bit display-referred PNGs after AgX + Punchy, with no Blender run - so I could observe what the pipeline DID and read what it is instructed to do, but never what a change would produce. Three specific ways the whole survey could be wrong. (1) If hero.py is no longer the delivery path - if the owner now judges only direct build.py renders - then finding 1 drops from blocking to a documentation defect and finding 3 becomes the important one; the two are complementary precisely because NEITHER path today delivers the full designed optics chain (direct: shadow yes, vignette/grain zero, bloom dead; stitched: vignette/grain/CA yes, shadow entirely absent). (2) Finding 4 accuses AgX-Punchy of a level-dependent chroma response while itself measuring through AgX-Punchy; PyOpenColorIO is not installed on this box, so I could not apply datafiles/colormanagement/luts/AgX_Base_sRGB.cube directly and settle it numerically. If the transform turns out to be materially flatter in chroma than I assume, finding 4 collapses from 'the instrument is confounded' to 'a caveat worth a line in the probe header' - one render at T1_EXP -1.0 prices it exactly. (3) My contact-shadow numbers all come from views where the ground is visible and lit the same way; the ratio statistic (terminator gradient over interior gradient) is designed to cancel exposure and shadow depth, but if some other stage - OpenImageDenoise's receptive field, or the 1.50 px filter interacting with a near-binary alpha - is what steepens the outer edge rather than the floor plus power, then the mechanism I name is wrong even though the measured edge is real. The one render that separates them is T1_SHADOW=1.0 T1_SHADOW_FLOOR=0 on low34: if the rim survives with the gain off, the compositor is innocent.

**THE FIDELITY BAR AUDIT — painted-on / flat-sweep features vs real geometry with depth, across t1_detail.py, t1_shell.py, texgen.py and tex/,**  
Four things would make this whole survey wrong. (1) I NEVER RAN BLENDER, as instructed, so every model-side number is either read off a source constant or measured in a shipped PNG. The hubcap finding's analytic half assumes T.revolve's profile convention is (along_axis, radius) — I verified that in t1_core.py:682-700 and in rim()'s own `prof = [(y, r*S) ...]` line, and the prediction then matched the render to 0.3 px, which is the best evidence I have that I read the convention correctly. If it were reversed, finding 1's mechanism would be wrong even though the rendered five-petal silhouette would still be a defect. (2) EVERY RENDER MEASUREMENT USES ppm = 271.1864 and the origin (800, 962.203) for the side view, inherited from the rev-49 survey's parse of studio.views()['side'] rather than re-derived. Findings 1 and 2 both quote metres through it. Their DIMENSIONLESS halves — the m=5 harmonic, the visible/built radius ratio 0.892, and the sign of the handle against the belt — do not use it at all and survive if it is wrong. (3) TWO FINDINGS LEAN ON THE GREEN BUS (IMG_2073) for a quantity I have argued is geometry: the cab-door handle's station and the tyre's lateral tread. Rule 26 says paint does not transfer, and the handle's station is measured AGAINST a painted break, so its magnitude inherits that risk in full; I have said so in the finding and kept only the sign as high-confidence. If the green bus's two-tone break sits materially lower than the red bus's, the 0.37 m shrinks — but it cannot change sign, because ref_side.jpg puts the RED bus's own handle below its own break. (4) THE TAIL-LINE COUNT (finding 4) rests on one JPEG of one 3/4 view at 353 px/m. I built no positive control for the trough detector on a frame where the answer is known — the render has only one line there, so it cannot serve as a control for finding two. That is the weakest instrument in this report and it is why finding 4 is medium, not high. Finally: I edited no project file; all crops and scripts are under probe_scratch/ prefixed rev50_fidelity-bar_ (51 files), and I re-listed out/ at the end — no new renders had landed since I started."

**THE RECORD — every pose choice, every unmeasured constant, and every claim standing as fact without a measurement**  
Four things would take most of this survey down. (1) I could not run Blender, so every claim about the BUILT model is read off source text and off renders somebody else made. \"mesh objects 221 is stale\" is an inference from counted mesh-creating calls, not a count; \"the board stands 4.0 mm clear\" is read off the expression `z0 = _seat + _hang + 0.0040`, not off a log line. If `audit.py` is run and prints 221, finding 6's sharpest number goes. (2) My central identity claim — ref_source.jpeg IS ref_playa_34.png — rests on correlation (0.9853 at zero shift), a crop-and-scale optimum at exactly scale 1.00 offset (0,0), and a side-by-side look at the badge. It does not rest on EXIF or on the owner saying so. If they are two exposures from one tripod position, the \"nine frames not ten\" count falls; the part that matters — a 4.1×-area copy of that view is in the repo and the five M-graded locks can be re-derived on it — survives either way. (3) Everything about SPEC is text-matching in a 671 KB file of which I read perhaps 4 %. A section I did not open could correct SPEC §0.2's fish-eye row, or state §8.2/8.3/8.5's coordinates, and both of those findings would collapse to \"the correction is in the wrong place\". I mitigated this by running verify.py's own retired-value table against the sections rather than trusting my reading, but the table has only 19 rows. (4) Every guard-coverage claim is a grep. A guard that reaches the tail board through a name I did not search for — an object-name loop, a NOT_BODYWORK iteration, a row in a file I did not open — would not appear in my results; I searched verify.py, verify_clone.sh, bootstrap.sh and all ten build sources for `tail_board`, `TB_`, `T1_NOTAILBOARD`, `tb_bulbs` and `board`, which is the reachable set I can defend. Finally, the whole survey is about the RECORD, so none of it moves a vertex: if the owner's next word is that the model looks wrong somewhere, every finding here is correctly outranked.

---

## CRITIC AND SYNTHESIS

{
 "verdict": "REFUTED",
 "reasoning": "The finding is broken on all four of its load-bearing legs. I did not run Blender; render numbers come through the ortho parsed from studio.views()[\"side\"] (ortho=5.90 on the 1600 px long axis, tgt z 1.52, +X to screen-left), i.e. z = 1.52 + (550-row)/271.19 px/m. That mapping is self-calibrating: the render's silhouette bottom at X +0.40 falls at z 0.331 AG against t1_core.ZB(0.40) - rake_drop(0.40) = 0.3300 \u2014 1 mm. If ortho_scale applied to the short axis instead, every render z would scale by 1100/1600 and that agreement could not have happened.\n\n1. THE ROCKER IS MODELLED, AND THE SOURCE SAYS SO IN FOUR PLACES THE FINDING DID NOT LOOK AT. t1_core.section() (line 250) builds the flank in five runs and run B is literally \"# B bottom roll\": qbez((Yb,Zb),(Ybs,Zb),(Ybs,zb0)) with zb0 = Zb + rb, plus run A, a bottom flat carrying bcrown=0.012. build_kombi() (line 485) passes RB_ALL(x), and RB_ALL (line 516) is 0.122 m over the whole main run (-1.900..+1.500), tapering to 0.085 at both ends. So the flank does not end in a flat cut at ZB; it rolls under through a 122 mm radius and closes on a crowned bottom pan. The project also NAMES this feature the rocker outside comments, which a grep of only t1_core/t1_shell/t1_detail/build missed: folk_gen.py:1337 `def rocker_band(x)`, folk_gen.py:471/509 `sill_z()` parsed from ZB's own knots, audit.py:108 \"rocker (body min z)\", audit.py:175/408 \"rocker to ground\" published into STATE.md every build.\n\n2. THE INSTRUMENT IS THE ARTEFACT: A SIDE ORTHO IS THE ONE VIEW IN WHICH A TURN-UNDER CANNOT BE SEEN. The orthographic projection of a rolled section onto XZ is, necessarily, a straight silhouette at min z = ZB. \"Straight silhouette therefore flat cut\" is a non-sequitur. Looked at in the view where a roll self-shadows \u2014 out/r49s_low34.png, the LOW 3/4, crop probe_scratch/rev50_atk_low34_sill_x4.png (orig 730-1120 x 760-880, x4) \u2014 the flank between the wheels plainly rolls under into a rounded shaded rocker with a contact shadow, the same shape ref_workshop.jpg shows on the GREEN shell. The finding's second viewpoint, r49s_hero34f, is a HIGH 3/4 looking DOWN, where a roll is occluded by the body above it; that is why it shows nothing, and it is not corroboration.\n\n3. THE \"BRIGHT NEUTRAL TRIM STRIP\" IS AN ACHROMATIC SPECULAR ON THE RED PAINT'S ROLLED LIP, AND THE AUTHOR'S OWN DISCRIMINATOR IS BACKWARDS. He rejects specular because \"a specular on red paint under a neutral sun retains a red bias\". A specular from a dielectric clearcoat is achromatic by construction \u2014 this project's own W6 headline is that the excess G/R is \"an achromatic specular veil\". Test on a second frame of the SAME vehicle in the SAME artwork state, ref_rear34.jpg, where the same lip is resolved over more pixels and viewed obliquely (probe_scratch/rev50_atk_rear34_sill_x5.png, 13 stations u 580-700, brightest row in a +/-7 px window about the traced lip): line R 221+/-8 G 164+/-11 B 143+/-11, G/R 0.69-0.79, against the paint 6 px above at R 195 G 76 B 48, G/R 0.35-0.42. The G and B offsets over the local diffuse are +89 and +97 \u2014 equal within noise, R suppressed only because it is near the top of its range. That is the additive signature of an achromatic highlight, and it reads visibly PINK, continuous with the red paint. ref_side.jpg col 470 gives the same thing at 1 px: paint (111,12,6) at row 596, ramp (133,79,51) at 601, peak (168,143,113) at 602 \u2014 offsets +57/+131/+107, G and B again together. So G/R 0.797 is not evidence of metal; it is what this project already knows a highlight on this paint does.\n\n4. THE GREEN-BUS LEG IS OVERSTATED AND ITS OTHER HALF REFUTES THE FINDING. The title claims \"both vehicles show a bright neutral trim strip\". The author's own crop (probe_scratch/rev50_flank-belt_workshop_sill_x4.png) shows no strip of any kind on the green shell \u2014 it shows a flank turning under into a shadowed rocker, which is the feature the model HAS.\n\n5. \"RAKE_DZDX IS MEASURED OFF A FEATURE THE BUILD DOES NOT CONTAIN\" IS CONTRADICTED BY THE FILE THE FINDING CITES. t1_core.py:143-145: \"RAKE_Z0 is re-anchored in the same solve, not carried over: the model's rocker sat -0.0088 above its hub at the front axle and +0.0704 at the rear\". METHOD 4's \"rocker\" is the body's lower edge, SPEC 10.100.4 states flatly \"the rocker is t1_core.ZB, the under-body / sill bottom edge\", and the build was re-anchored against a measurement of its own rocker. The feature is contained, named and guarded.\n\n6. REPRODUCED AND ALREADY-RULED CHECKS. I reproduce the author's strip height with my own window \u2014 col 470, row 602 -> 0.3539 m AG against his bulk 0.3437-0.3620, so his number is real; it is the identification that fails. His \"15-25 px of dark rocker/underbody\" is the under-vehicle void: at col 470 the ground at the flank plane is row ~675 (vhub 606.4 + 0.3325*kv 205.5), the dark runs 604-648 and the paving beyond the bus rises into frame from 650, which is the geometry of looking under a bus, not 100 mm of extra bodywork. SPEC 10.106.7 already retracted a ~49 mm version of exactly this claim, ruled \"the body's lower edge relative to the axle is UNMEASURED\", and wrote the rule about far sides. He does clear that rule here (the transition is unclipped) \u2014 but clearing it only makes his number good, not his identification.\n\nWHAT I DROPPED. (a) I could not use the render's own lower-flank darkening (R 195->142, -27% over the bottom 100 mm at X +0.80) as proof the roll shades: t1_mats W_DUST_Z_HI/LO = 0.480/0.300 puts a road-film tide-line ramp over exactly that band in true above-ground metres, so geometry and texture are not separable there. The roll's existence rests on the source constants and on low34, not on that gradient. (b) I re-derived the height sub-claim the author dropped and it does not close cleanly \u2014 model ZB_AG at the two axles (X +1.302 / -1.099 by inverting the flank map) sits 14.7 / 17.3 mm below METHOD 4's photographed rocker-above-hub, while t1_core:143 says RAKE_Z0 was re-anchored to remove that. I am NOT reporting that as a defect: it comes off the authored LUT rather than the built mesh (no Blender), my axle stations come from my own inversion of the flank map, and the two residuals agreeing to 2.6 mm could equally mean the re-anchor targeted a different pair of stations. It is a lead for a separate probe, not part of this verdict. (c) The one genuine render/photo difference that survives is photometric, not geometric: the photographed lip carries a hard highlight 2.6x (ref_side) / 1.6x (ref_rear34) the paint above it, while the studio ortho's last un-antialiased row is 1.0x. Under a 255-white cyclorama floor a downward-rolling lip is uniformly lit and cannot form a discrete glint; under a low sun over sunlit pavement it must. That is rule-7(d) lighting, it lives inside W6's achromatic-specular lever, and it is not a missing rocker.",
 "corrected_claim": "REFUTED. The rocker IS modelled: t1_core.section() run B builds a bottom roll of radius RB_ALL = 0.122 m over the whole main run (-1.900..+1.500 m, tapering to 0.085 at both ends) closing on a bottom pan with bcrown 0.012, and the project names the feature outside comments in folk_gen.rocker_band()/sill_z() and publishes \"rocker (body min z)\" and \"rocker to ground\" from audit.py into STATE.md every build. The straight silhouette at ZB in out/r49board_side.png is what a rolled section must project to in an ORTHOGRAPHIC side elevation \u2014 the one view in which a turn-under is invisible \u2014 and the roll is plainly visible, shaded and self-shadowing, in out/r49s_low34.png (crop probe_scratch/rev50_atk_low34_sill_x4.png), matching ref_workshop.jpg's green shell. The claim that RAKE_DZDX is fitted to a feature the build lacks is contradicted by the cited file itself (t1_core.py:143-145 re-anchors RAKE_Z0 against \"the model's rocker\"; SPEC 10.100.4 defines the rocker as t1_core.ZB). CEILING: render z from studio.views()[\"side\"] ortho 5.90 on the 1600 px axis, z = 1.52 + (550-row)/271.19, validated by the silhouette bottom at X +0.40 landing on ZB - rake_drop to 1 mm; reference photometry is a per-pixel additive-offset test needing no scale or datum, so it does not inherit the SPEC 10.35 flank map. This would be wrong if the shell's bottom roll were being collapsed at subdivision time in a way the shipped low34 render does not reveal \u2014 which I cannot exclude without running Blender. Two things NOT covered by this refutation and left open: the studio's 255-white cyclorama floor cannot reproduce the hard bottom-lip glint the street photographs show (2.6x in ref_side, 1.6x in ref_rear34, against 1.0x in the render) \u2014 a lighting/W6 matter, not missing geometry; and the model's ZB reads 14.7 mm (front axle) / 17.3 mm (rear axle) below METHOD 4's photographed rocker-above-hub on my own inversion of the flank map, which is the height sub-claim the finding's author already dropped and which needs a built-mesh measurement, not an authored-LUT one, before it can be called anything."
