 "reasoning": "I attacked this six ways. The OBSERVABLE survived everything I threw at it; the FRAMING around it did not.\n\nWHAT I COULD NOT BREAK\n\n1. The pixel reads reproduce with my own windows and my own instrument. On ref_workshop.jpg (GREEN bus, geometry-admissible under rule 26, lamp REMOVED so the outline is a bare aperture), I re-cut both features at 8-10x with coordinate grids and READ them (probe_scratch/rev50_att_roundelwide8x.png, rev50_att_neargrid10x.png). By eye the roundel is an obvious tall ellipse and the bore is an obvious circle. By hand: roundel left x=276/right x=337/top y=502/bottom y=593 -> 61x91; bore left 383/right 456/top 592/bottom 665 -> 73x73. Direct conic fits with a threshold sweep (probe_scratch/rev50_att_conic.py) give roundel axis ratio 0.653-0.661 over L<150..190 and bore 0.904-0.931 over (G-R)<12..20, both windows asserted not to touch their boxes. So 0.657 +- 0.005 against 0.92 +- 0.02, a 1.40x difference between two circles the build puts on one plane with one normal.\n\n2. It is not a small-azimuth artefact. Independent of the roundel, the front face spans ~290 px (silhouette x~220 to the front corner x~510) against ~570 px of flank (to the tail at x~1080) on a 3.77 m flank vs 1.75 m width, which needs azimuth >= 42 deg and, once the flank's recession is allowed for, 45-55 deg. That matches the roundel's own 48.9 deg. There is no pose in which the nose is nearly head-on and the roundel is merely mis-measured.\n\n3. The \"roundel sits on rolled-back metal, so its obliquity is elevation not azimuth\" escape fails on the ellipse ORIENTATION. The roundel's major axis fits at 90-101 deg (near vertical), i.e. its minor axis - which lies along the image projection of the surface normal - is within ~10 deg of horizontal. That is the signature of an essentially horizontal panel normal seen at large azimuth, and it is well determined because the ratio is far from 1.\n\n4. The author's own strongest refutation (a proud convex rim silhouetting round from any direction) does not reach this frame, and I looked for it rather than assuming. In rev50_att_neargrid10x.png there is no proud painted lip outboard of the dark rim - flat green right up to it - and the visible interior wall lies on the far side of the bore, the signature of a RECESSED bore, not a proud one. Arithmetically a proud tube would need L*sin(psi)/2R = 0.34, i.e. ~82 mm of standing flange at psi=49, to lift 0.66 to 1.00. Nothing like that is in the pixels.\n\n5. THE DECISIVE TEST, WHICH THE FINDING DID NOT RUN AND WHICH DISSOLVES ITS OWN WEAKEST LINK. I replaced the focal-length \"window\" argument with a projectively exact one that needs no focal length at all (probe_scratch/rev50_att_homog2.py). If the nose is a plane, plane->image is a homography; every homography carrying the roundel circle onto the measured roundel conic is A^-1 . M . S with M in PO(2,1), so the search is four numbers (three for M, one for the unknown aperture radius), exhaustive over ALL camera poses and ALL focal lengths - the permissive limit. Result on the photograph: best possible flat-plane fit rms 13.24 px on 5 observations, the lamp's semi-minor missing by 15.5 px on an observed 34.5 (45 %). CONTROL, same procedure, same code, on out/r49s_hero34f.png where the nose IS flat by construction: rms 0.22 px. Sixty times better. So the instrument works, and no camera at any f can put those two conics on one plane.\n\n6. The source really does build it flat there. t1_core.build_kombi lofts to a front-most ring at x=2.108 with half-width 0.7244 and caps it with a PLANAR Coons patch, so both lamps (y=0.545) and the roundel (y=0) sit inboard of the cap's edge on one plane; t1_shell.nose_shape:2346's only forward term gives bulge(0)-bulge(0.545) = 8.3 mm, a 1.9 deg normal; t1_detail.headlamp is a revolve about +X placed by build.py:506 with no rotation.\n\n7. Rule-26 and owner-ruling checks are clean: the surviving evidence is on the GREEN frame, the quantity is geometry, no duplicate is counted as corroboration, and nothing here re-adopts a retired identification (the retired items are the stars, the flower lid, La Santa, the over-rider bar, the lower bay).\n\nWHAT I DID BREAK\n\nA. THE NOVELTY CLAIM, \"AT LAST\", IS FALSE. REF_MEASUREMENTS.md:490 already records \"headlamp aperture centre (419, 629) width 76.9 px height 75.6 px -> circular\" and, at :466-468 in the same section, \"Lateral scale varies by more than 2:1 across the front panel (I fitted a projection model and it did not close, which is itself evidence that the panel is not planar).\" Same frame, same feature, same inference, present since the reference file was written. The genuinely new part is the SAME-FRAME ROUNDEL as a paired control, which sharpens it into a ratio; that is a sharpening, not a first.\n\nB. \"BLOCKED ON: NOTHING\" AND \"HANDLE\" OVER-CLAIM. The finding's own body refuses to invert the observable into a value for `bulge` and says separating the three causes needs a grazing frame nobody holds. It therefore does not unblock W4, which was already owner-reported, confirmed and open for five revisions. Severity \"blocking\" is inherited from W4, not established here.\n\nC. \"ALL THREE ARE ABSENT FROM THE BUILD\" IS FALSE FOR ONE OF THE THREE. t1_detail.headlamp's ring_prof spans x -0.004..+0.0235 and is placed at HL_X=2.1015 while the nose skin at the lamp is x=2.1116 (the raycast published in that same function), so the bezel's front face stands 13.4 mm proud of the sheet metal and the domed lens 10.4 mm. \"Not proud enough\" may be true; \"absent\" is not.\n\nD. \"THREE INDEPENDENT PHOTOGRAPHS\" IS ONE. The two Nolita frames are disowned two paragraphs later by the finding itself, and correctly so. The headline count should be one, and the quoted spread \"1.36-2.05x\" takes its upper end from a frame the finding does not stand behind.\n\nE. THE QUOTED NUMBERS ARE BOUNDING BOXES, WHICH IS THE WRONG COMPARAND. \"74x70 = 1.06\" is a bbox of a heavily tilted ellipse (major axis 30 deg from horizontal), which inflates it past 1.0; the true axis ratio is 0.90-0.93. The honest pair is 0.657 vs 0.93 = 1.42x, not 1.06/0.659 = 1.61x.\n\nF. THE WINDOW MARGIN IS OVERSTATED. The finding bounds f at 800-3000 px and calls the photographs \"3.5-7x outside\". Two independent lines put f BELOW that floor: REF_MEASUREMENTS.md:466's own \">2:1 lateral scale across the front panel\" requires the camera ~1.1-1.5 m off the near corner, and the roundel/aperture major-axis ratio (92.4 px per 0.280 m against 74.5 px per D_a) gives f ~ 560-660 px unless the aperture is >= 0.190 m. At f=560-660 the window is 12-14 deg against a measured 25.8 deg - about 2x outside, not 3.5-7x. (This is now moot for the verdict, because the homography test in 5 removes f from the argument entirely - but the published margin is wrong.)\n\nG. THE RENDER HALF IS WEAK EVIDENCE. \"In the shipped render the two read the SAME\" compares a ~28 deg pose against a ~49 deg photograph, and the lamp/roundel aspect ratio is itself pose-dependent even on a correctly crowned nose: a 20 deg crown would show 1.12 at the render's pose against the built 1.02, ~5 px on a 50 px bezel. The photograph is the discriminator; the render is not.\n\nWHAT I ADD (with its ceiling). An independent corroborator in the same frame that the finding does not use, and which is PHOTOS_WANTED_rev49 sec.3's \"Method 2, the silhouette corner-wrap on ref_workshop.jpg... IT HAS NEVER BEEN RUN\": the FAR headlamp bore is cut by the vehicle's own front-corner silhouette. A (G-R)>=12 green mask puts the paint's left edge at x~218-230 in rows 520-544 and x~222-228 in rows 616-636, but at x~232-238 across rows 548-612 - the bore band - i.e. the bore has eaten the silhouette and there is no painted panel outboard of it at all (probe_scratch/rev50_att_fargrid8x.png, rev50_att_farwide5x.png). On the built nose - flat cap to |y|=0.724, then WX rolling to 0.875 - roughly 0.22 m, ~45 px, of panel would stand outboard of the far bore at this azimuth. CEILING: single-threshold mask on a dark, low-contrast region at the silhouette; the ~45 px is from a hand perspective sketch at theta=49 deg, not a fitted camera; and the far bore is NOT measurable as an ellipse - the author's clipped 0.346 is a crescent and should never be quoted.\n\nWHAT I DROPPED. My first flat-nose fit (probe_scratch/rev50_att_flatfit.py) and my first homography fit (rev50_att_homog.py) both had more free parameters than observations or failed to converge, and one of them silently leaked its roundel blob to the whole window because the seed landed in a bright gap in the glyph - it reported 76x106 and would have published a \"ratio 0.684\". That is exactly this project's failure mode, so the final script asserts the seed is in the mask and asserts the blob does not touch the window edge. I also dropped an argument from the lamp ellipse's major-axis ANGLE: at ratio 0.93 the orientation of a 73 px blob is not determined well enough to carry weight, so the homography residual should be read on the axes (9.4 px rms without the angle term, still 40x the control), not on the angle.",
 "corrected_claim": "SHARPENED, NOT NEW: on ref_workshop.jpg the near headlamp APERTURE is circular while the same-frame VW roundel is a 0.66 ellipse, and no flat nose can produce both. This is W4's known symptom made quantitative; it is not a handle and it unblocks nothing.\n\nTHE OBSERVABLE (stands). On ref_workshop.jpg (GREEN bus, GEOMETRY quantity, admissible under rule 26; lamp REMOVED so the outline is the bare aperture, not a bezel), direct conic fits with a threshold sweep give: VW roundel axis ratio 0.653-0.661 (L<150..190, bbox 61-64 x 91-93), near headlamp aperture 0.904-0.931 ((G-R)<12..20, bbox 73-75 x 70-73). A 1.42x difference between two circles build.py places on one plane with a common normal (roundel y=0 z=1.1025, lamp y=0.545 z=0.9330). ONE photograph, not three: the two Nolita frames die to the proud-bezel argument and must not be counted.\n\nTHE PROOF THAT NO CAMERA CAN DO IT (new, and it retires the focal-length argument). Because plane->image is a homography and every homography carrying the roundel circle onto its measured conic is A^-1.M.S with M in PO(2,1), the flat-nose hypothesis can be searched exhaustively in four parameters over ALL poses and ALL focal lengths. Best flat-plane fit to the photograph: rms 13.24 px on 5 observations, the lamp's semi-minor short by 15.5 px on an observed 34.5. Same code on out/r49s_hero34f.png, where the nose is flat by construction: rms 0.22 px. probe_scratch/rev50_att_homog2.py, rev50_att_conic.py.\n\nCORROBORATION IN THE SAME FRAME (Method 2, never previously run). The FAR headlamp bore is cut by the vehicle's own front-corner silhouette: the green mask's left edge sits at x~218-230 above the bore and x~222-228 below it, but at x~232-238 across it, with no painted panel outboard of the bore at all - where the built nose (flat cap to |y|=0.724, then WX rolling to 0.875) would leave ~0.22 m, roughly 45 px, of panel there.\n\nWHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT SAY. It says the build's front end is missing something real, and the source confirms the build has nothing to produce it: t1_core.build_kombi caps the x=2.108 ring with a PLANAR Coons patch spanning |y|<=0.7244, t1_shell.nose_shape:2346 gives only 8.3 mm between the two stations (a 1.9 deg normal), and t1_detail.headlamp is a revolve about +X placed with no rotation. It does NOT apportion between panel plan-crown (W4 proper), outboard splay of the lamp axis, and a proud aperture flange, and it must not be inverted into a value for `bulge`: inverting it gives a 34-39 deg normal swing, a ~1.1 m plan radius and a 160 mm sagitta, which is a Beetle nose, not a Bus nose. Note also that the build's bezel is NOT absent - ring_prof stands 13.4 mm proud of the nose skin and the lens 10.4 mm - so of the three candidate causes only two are genuinely missing.\n\nCEILINGS. (1) NOT NEW: REF_MEASUREMENTS.md:490 already records this aperture as \"width 76.9 px height 75.6 px -> circular\", and :466 already concludes from the same frame that \"a fitted projection model did not close, which is itself evidence that the panel is not planar\". The paired same-frame roundel is the only new element. (2) The homography residual is a JOINT statement about planarity AND the lamp's built offsets (dy=0.545, dz=-0.16948); with those offsets free the test becomes vacuous (7 parameters, 5 observations), so it tests the BUILD against the photograph, not planarity in the abstract. (3) Bounding-box aspects must not be quoted: the lamp's ellipse is tilted ~30 deg, which inflates its bbox past 1.0. (4) The lamp ellipse's ANGLE carries no weight at ratio 0.93 on a 73 px blob; the residual without it is 9.4 px rms, still 40x the control. (5) The far-bore silhouette reading is a single-threshold mask on a dark region at the silhouette, and the ~45 px shortfall is a hand sketch at theta=49 deg, not a fitted camera; the far bore itself is unmeasurable as an ellipse and the clipped 0.346 must never be quoted. (6) Camera azimuth ~45-55 deg is corroborated independently of the roundel (front face ~290 px against ~570 px of flank), so this is not a near-head-on artefact. (7) The render half of the comparison is weak: the lamp/roundel ratio is pose-dependent, and at the render's ~28 deg pose even a 20 deg crown would show only 1.12 against the built 1.02.\n\nSTATUS. Severity is INHERITED from W4, not established here. BLOCKED ON is not \"nothing\": stating the defect is unblocked and always was, but separating the three causes still needs one frame from beside the front corner at nose height looking across the front face at grazing incidence, or straight down onto the badge. This finding does not reduce W4's blocked-ness; it gives it a number and a control."
}

 "verdict": "PARTIALLY_STANDS",
 "reasoning": "THE CORE SURVIVES AND I STRENGTHENED IT; THREE ATTACHED CLAIMS DO NOT.\n\nWHAT I REPRODUCED. Own instrument (sub-pixel half-crossing tracker, re-seeded per row, 2-sigma clipping), ref_side.jpg \u2014 the RED target, current artwork, and the lid attitude is a pose/geometry quantity, not paint, so rule 26 admits it. Fore edge x = +0.15528*y + 304.31 (n=193, rows 58-257, rms 0.34 px) against their +0.1511*y + 306.1; aft edge x = +0.05983*y + 738.87 (n=175, rows 30-261, rms 0.58) against their +0.0585*y + 739. Taper -0.0955 px/row = -0.0225 %/row against their -0.0216. Fully reproducible.\n\nWINDOW IS PART OF THE MEASUREMENT. The fore edge is stable (four sub-windows: +0.1521/+0.1543/+0.1562/+0.1547; quadratic sagitta 0.04 px). The aft edge is NOT (+0.0729/-0.0484/+0.0572/+0.0371; sagitta -1.05 px, bowed by the stay crossing it below row 250). A second, seedless estimator (hard YEL threshold, first/last run per row) gives -0.0134 to -0.0337 %/row over T=55..100. So the RATE is only bounded to a factor of 2.5. The SIGN is invariant across 2 estimators x 4 thresholds x 4 windows, and survives even the flattest aft slope.\n\nTHE CONTROL THE FINDING DID NOT HAVE, AND IT HOLDS. True verticals in the same frame: lamppost both edges (rows 10-469) +0.04165/+0.03655 at x 48.7/66.2, rms 0.24/0.35; concrete column both edges (rows 8-249) +0.02028/+0.01678 at x 772/802, rms 0.29/0.26; background doorway jamb +0.0150 at x 808, rms 0.25. Slope varies with x at -2.96e-5/px -> vertical VP (1381, +33968). Independent cross-check on the vehicle itself: its three bay pillars average +0.023 at mean x 563; that VP predicts +0.0242. Board VP = (1011, +4553) outer pair, (1168, +5650) inner pair (their inner fit had a bad seed and locked onto the border lettering; re-seeded it agrees in sign). BOTH VPs lie BELOW the frame, so tan(alpha+theta) and tan(90+theta) are both positive and alpha < 90 follows ORDINALLY \u2014 no focal length, no camera distance, no px/m. To rescue 104 deg the camera needs >=14 deg of DOWNWARD pitch, which would force a lamppost-minus-column slope difference of +0.145; measured +0.0214. 6.8x out, ~7 sigma. And a 14 deg down pitch is independently excluded by the frame: the counter top and the caddy tops are visible (camera above ~1.15 m) while the roof plane reads edge-on (camera below ~2.0 m).\n\nTHEIR REFUTATION (a) UPGRADED FROM HAND-WAVE TO NUMBER. Barrel distortion is not merely \"pushing the other way\", it is absent: the lamppost's quadratic sagitta is +0.19 px over 459 rows at x=57, 57 px from the frame edge. No distortion field that flat can manufacture a 0.096/row differential.\n\nHOW THE ERROR GOT IN (diagnosis the finding lacks). SPEC.md:4386 records 104 deg as \"measured at 211.5 px/m\" beside the 1.11 m width \u2014 but any route through the board's projected height measures W*sin(alpha), and sin 104 = sin 76 exactly, so that route is DEGENERATE about 90 deg and cannot have chosen the sign. t1_shell.py:2103-2108's justification is equally blind: the underside normal is (0, sin a, -cos a), whose +Y component is sin a, identical at 76 and 104. Both rakes face the mural at the show side; they differ only by tilting it 14 deg up or 14 deg down, and the ref_side camera sits BELOW the board (4-8 deg off-normal at 76 deg, 20-24 deg at 104 deg).\n\nNOT A REOPENING OF ANYTHING RULED. SPEC 10.26's rev-11 vanishing-point fit is of the lid's LONG (fore-aft) edges against the vehicle's fore-aft VP (-10509, 733) and validates the hinge AXIS, not the rake. (Its y=733 would imply a 16 deg UPWARD pitch at f=1200, which this frame's verticals contradict; that VP's y is not usable, being 11000 px outside the frame.) The owner's rev-11 words \"the roof lid opens toward the passengers' side\" and SPEC.md:135 \"swings up and over the counter\" are both consistent with alpha<90. The owner's standing mural ruling is about the ARTWORK (\"the flower mural with yellow menu strips on the raised roof lid is CORRECT as built\"), not the rake. No retired identification is being re-adopted.\n\nWHAT I REFUTED.\n(1) \"All three in-service frames tip it over the counter\" \u2014 FALSE. Only ref_side.jpg carries a measurement. The \"support rod passes in front of the board's face\" cue in ref_rear34.jpg and IMG_2073.jpeg does NOT discriminate the rake: a stay footed on the SHOW side lies on the camera's side of the board's plane at BOTH rakes (at 104 deg the board sits at y <= -0.545 and a rod from y=+0.6 to the free edge is nearer the camera wherever it overlaps; at 76 deg likewise). That cue establishes which side the stay's FOOT is on \u2014 survey finding 48, a different object. IMG_2073 is the GREEN bus; an open ANGLE held by a prop is a POSE, and rule 26 transfers geometry, not pose, unless the stay is shown to be a fixed-length pinned link, which no frame shows. So the corroboration count is ONE frame, not three.\n(2) The out/r49s_counter.png consequence is MISATTRIBUTED. I rebuilt studio.py:1397's camera (loc 3.40,5.20,1.98; tgt -0.55,0.75,1.26; 90 mm; 1600x1100 on a 36 mm sensor) in bare numpy and validated it against the render itself \u2014 it predicts the board's in-frame aft cut at px 539/1600 = 404 in a 1200-wide resize, and the render's sliver ends at 410. With the projector verified: the hinge edge does not move with alpha at all, and the free edge projects at py -683 (104 deg), -736 (76 deg), -716 (68 deg) \u2014 correcting the rake pushes the board slightly FURTHER out of that frame. The board is out of the counter view because a 90 mm lens is aimed 6.9 deg DOWN at z=1.26 from z=1.98 while studio.py:1373-1374 declares the view \"looking slightly up at the mural board\". Real defect, different dimension, not a consequence of LID_OPEN_DEG.\n(3) \"1.63 m away from the counter\" / \"leans over the serving counter like an awning\" \u2014 overstated. With LID_W=1.110 and the hinge at y=-0.545 the free edge cannot reach the counter at ANY alpha: its extreme is y=+0.565 (flat), still inboard of the show-side roof edge Yt~0.727 and short of a counter that cantilevers outboard of the flank. At 76 deg it lands at y=-0.277, i.e. 0.537 m nearer the show side than built but still over the roof aperture. SPEC.md:135's own phrase carries the same error; that is a separate prose defect.\n(4) MAGNITUDE IS NOT NEW AND NOT TIGHTER. Solving tan(alpha+theta)/tan(90+theta) = (4553-384)/(33968-384) = 0.1179 gives alpha = 80.4/75.9/69.5/63.0 deg for f = 800/1200/1800/2500 px \u2014 compatible with their 61-78 deg, but entirely dependent on an unknown focal length, and widening to roughly 55-85 deg once the taper's own factor-2.5 spread is carried. AUDIT_rev43 already declared this ceiling at [63,76]. Their claimed \"26-43 deg error and 0.6-0.8 m error in Y\" should be stated as a bound, not a value.\n\nCEILING ON MY OWN WORK. No render of the model exists at ref_side.jpg's own camera, so this is photograph-only; the aft-edge fit is the weak leg and I could not stabilise it past +/-0.06 in slope; LID_W is itself disputed by survey finding 49 and alpha and W are not separable without a camera distance, so a fix must set the sign first and treat the angle as bounded, not measured. Blender was not run; PIL/numpy only.",
 "corrected_claim": "THE MURAL LID'S RAKE HAS THE WRONG SIGN. t1_shell.py:1078 LID_OPEN_DEG = 104.0 puts the free edge past vertical at y = -0.545 + 1.110*cos104 = -0.8135, on the OFF side of the hinge and outboard of the off-side roof edge; ref_side.jpg requires alpha < 90, i.e. the free edge on the SHOW side of the hinge. BLOCKING; the sign needs no further photograph.\n\nTHE EVIDENCE, one frame only. ref_side.jpg (RED target, current artwork; camera unambiguously on the show side \u2014 three open hatches, counter, Senor Tacombi script, 100% Calidad all in frame). The board's two end edges converge DOWNWARD: fore x = +0.15528*y + 304.31 (n=193, rows 58-257, rms 0.34 px, quadratic sagitta 0.04 px, stable at +0.1521/+0.1543/+0.1562/+0.1547 across four sub-windows); aft x = +0.05983*y + 738.87 (n=175, rows 30-261, rms 0.58 px, UNSTABLE at +0.0729/-0.0484/+0.0572/+0.0371 by window \u2014 the stay crosses it below row 250). Board vanishing point (1011, +4553) outer pair, (1168, +5650) inner pair. In the SAME frame, true verticals \u2014 lamppost both edges rows 10-469 (+0.04165/+0.03655 at x 48.7/66.2, rms 0.24/0.35), concrete column both edges rows 8-249 (+0.02028/+0.01678 at x 772/802), background doorway jamb (+0.0150 at x 808) \u2014 put the VERTICAL vanishing point at (1381, +33968); the vehicle's own three bay pillars (mean +0.023 at x 563) predict +0.0242 from it and so cross-check it. Both vanishing points lie below the frame, therefore tan(alpha+theta) and tan(90+theta) are both positive and ALPHA < 90 FOLLOWS ORDINALLY \u2014 no focal length, no camera distance, no px/m, no scale of any kind.\n\nWHY THE READING IS WRONG IS BOUNDED, NOT ASSUMED. Rescuing 104 deg needs >=14 deg of downward camera pitch, which would force a lamppost-minus-column slope difference of +0.145 against +0.0214 measured (6.8x, ~7 sigma), and is separately excluded by the frame itself (the counter top and caddy tops are visible, so the camera is above ~1.15 m; the roof plane reads edge-on, so it is below ~2.0 m). Barrel distortion is measured absent, not argued away: 0.19 px of quadratic sagitta over 459 rows at x=57. Two independent estimators (tracker; seedless threshold mask at T=55..100) agree in sign; the rate is bounded -0.013 to -0.034 %/row.\n\nHOW 104 GOT IN. SPEC.md:4386 records it as \"measured at 211.5 px/m\", but that route measures W*sin(alpha) and sin104 = sin76 exactly \u2014 degenerate about 90 deg, so it never determined the sign. t1_shell.py:2103-2108's justification is likewise blind: the underside normal is (0, sin a, -cos a), whose +Y component is sin a, identical at both roots; the two rakes differ only in tilting the mural 14 deg up versus 14 deg down, and this camera is BELOW the board.\n\nMAGNITUDE \u2014 A BOUND, NOT A VALUE. alpha = 63-80 deg for f = 800-2500 px, widening to roughly 55-85 deg once the taper's factor-2.5 spread is carried. This is NOT tighter than AUDIT_rev43's declared [63,76] ceiling and must not be published as a measured angle. LID_W (disputed by survey finding 49) and alpha are not separable without a camera distance, so the fix is: make the constant acute and record the angle as BOUNDED, not measured \u2014 the same declaration TRUNK_OPEN_DEG already carries.\n\nWHAT DOES NOT STAND. (a) \"All three in-service frames\": only ref_side.jpg carries a measurement. The rod-in-front cue in ref_rear34.jpg and IMG_2073.jpeg is non-discriminating \u2014 a stay footed on the show side lies on the camera's side of the board plane at BOTH rakes; it establishes which side the stay's FOOT is on (survey finding 48), not the rake. IMG_2073 is the GREEN bus and a propped-open ANGLE is a pose, which rule 26 does not transfer. (b) The out/r49s_counter.png consequence is misattributed: with that camera reconstructed and validated against the render (predicted in-frame cut 404/1200 vs 410 observed), the hinge edge does not move with alpha and the free edge projects HIGHER at 76 deg (py -736) than at 104 deg (-683) \u2014 correcting the rake leaves the board just as far out of frame, marginally more. That board is out of frame because a 90 mm lens is aimed 6.9 deg DOWN at z=1.26 while studio.py:1373-1374 declares the view \"looking slightly up at the mural board\" \u2014 a real defect of the counter VIEW, not of LID_OPEN_DEG. (c) \"1.63 m from the counter / leans over the serving counter\": with LID_W=1.110 the free edge cannot reach the counter at any angle (extreme y=+0.565, still inboard of Yt~0.727). Correct wording: the free edge must cross to the SHOW side of the hinge and comes to rest over the roof aperture, about 0.54 m nearer the show side than built. SPEC.md:135's \"up and over the counter\" carries the same error and needs the same correction.\n\nNOT A REOPENING. SPEC 10.26's rev-11 vanishing-point fit was of the lid's LONG edges against the vehicle's fore-aft VP and settles the hinge AXIS, not the rake. The owner's rev-11 \"opens toward the passengers' side\" and his standing ruling that the flower mural on the raised lid is correct as built are both consistent with alpha < 90 and neither closes this."
}

I have enough. Writing the critique.

# COMPLETENESS CRITIQUE — rev 50 survey

Everything below was measured with bare python3 (PIL/numpy/scipy) on files already on disk. No Blender. Crops written to `/home/user/combi_render/probe_scratch/rev50_crit_*.png` and read as images.

---

## 1. WHAT NO DIMENSION COVERS AT ALL

### 1.1 THE VEHICLE'S RELATIONSHIP TO THE GROUND — it removes 7–9 % of the light from the floor it is standing on

This is the largest single "render, not photograph" signal in the shipped frames and it is nobody's dimension. "Lighting, shadow, camera and optics" owns the *contact shadow* — the pool beside the tyres — and explicitly closes the magnitude question. Nobody measured the ground **under the body between the axles**, which is what the eye actually reads as "planted".

Measurement (window: for each column strictly between the near wheels, find the lowest pixel with L < 200 — unambiguously vehicle, since the shadow never goes below 210 — then sample the band 10–45 px below it; the band is 1.2–5.5 % of the vehicle's own image height so it scales across frames):

| frame | under-body band | frac bit-exact 255 | ratio to open ground (255.00) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `out/r49s_hero34f.png` (THE DELIVERY FRAME) | 236.3 DN | 0.233 | **0.927** |
| `out/r49s_low34.png` | 231.4 | 0.060 | 0.907 |
| `out/r49shut2_hero34r.png` | 231.3 | 0.170 | 0.907 |
| `out/r49s_front34.png` | 217.1 | 0.000 | 0.851 |
| `bus_model_ref.JPG` — **the owner's own fidelity bar**, ground under the body between the wheels | **63.0 DN**, p05 41.3, no pixel above 250 | 0.000 | **0.247** |
| `ref_side.jpg` rows 610–650, cols 420–680 (under-vehicle band) vs lit ground at rows 670–690 (~120 DN) | 7–30 DN | — | **0.06–0.25** |

A studio white-cyc product render of a bus and an outdoor street photograph of *this* bus agree at 0.06–0.25. The delivery frame is at 0.93. That is not a white-studio-vs-street lighting difference (rule 7d) — the two controls sit on opposite sides of that divide and give the same answer.

Look at it: `probe_scratch/rev50_crit_underbody_h34f.png` and `rev50_crit_underwin.png` against `rev50_crit_fidbar_under.png`. In ours you see straight under the sill to white paper; in the bar you see a dark undercarriage, a rocker rail, a chassis rail and a soft pool that grades outward.

Two separate causes, both uncovered:

* **No cavity term in the lighting.** `studio.py:429` sets the world to **0.05** — cut from 0.30 → 0.17 → 0.05 with the stated reason *"a bright white world dumps achromatic fill into every shadow and desaturates the paint (SPEC rev4 sec.3)"*. That is W6's cause being fought by deleting the ambient hemisphere. With six discrete rectangular softboxes and no dome, a crevice is either lit or black; it never gets the soft occlusion gradient a photograph has.
* **No cavity term in the shading.** `t1_mats.py:903` takes `Geometry → Pointiness` and uses **only the convex half**. There is no concave branch anywhere in `apply_weather` — nothing puts grime in a shut line, a drip rail, an arch-lip corner, under the counter nosing, around the badge, or in the louvre slots. `W_DUST_*` is retired and gated below z 0.480 regardless. In `probe_scratch/rev50_crit_detf.png` the roundel meets the nose on a perfectly clean line; on a 60-year-old bus that junction is the dirtiest 3 mm on the panel.

Related and also uncovered: **there is no undercarriage**. `t1_detail.galley()` builds `van_floor` at z 0.540, 40 mm thick, `FLOOR_W = 1.200` — **AUTHORED**, 550 mm narrower than the 1.750 m body. No chassis, no exhaust, no axle beams, no fuel tank. `optics-6` has been open in `studio.py:76` **since rev 12** — seven revisions — and the survey's twelve dimensions between them closed it.

### 1.2 EVERY DUPLICATED PART IS A BIT-IDENTICAL CLONE — same clock angle, same dirt

`build.py:346-360` places all four wheels with `D.place(o, loc=(x, s*tr/2, T.TIRE_R))` — **no rotation about the axle**. So all four hubcaps are at the same clock angle, all four sets of five rim vents are at the same clock angle, and — because `WEATHER`, `MOTTLE` and the dust noises are all fed **Object** coordinates (`t1_mats.py:638`, `:906`) — every duplicate carries a bit-identical wear field.

Measured on `out/r49board_side.png`, crops centred on the two hub centres (front 873.3, 446.3; rear 873.3, 1097.1 = front + 2.400 m × 271.186 px/m), high-pass = image minus a 9 px box:

```
FRONT vs REAR wheel      HF corr  0.9007
  tyre annulus only (62<r<86 px)  0.7709
  hubcap dome only  (r<45 px)     0.9199
CONTROL front vs itself, +5 px    0.0255
CONTROL two plain flank patches  -0.3079
```

The HF field decorrelates completely within 5 px, so 0.90 at Δx = +1 px is real registration, not autocorrelation. Look at `probe_scratch/rev50_crit_wheelphase.png`: the five vent notches, the VW glyph, the dome's specular streak and the dust are in the same place on both wheels.

This is a class, not one part: both headlamps, both indicators, both tail lamps, both cab-door hinges, the three bay seals, the two lid struts, the bulb string's repeats. It is exactly the "asymmetry and imperfection" axis the brief names and no dimension owns it. The fix is two lines (a per-instance random rotation and a per-instance `MOTTLE_OFS`/object-space offset) and the machinery for the second already exists — `MOTTLE_OFS` was built at rev 20 as "a rigid TRANSLATION of the mottle's sampling point... (0,0,0) is an exact no-op", which is precisely a per-instance seed with the control already written.

### 1.3 REFLECTIVE SURFACES HAVE NOTHING TO REFLECT — and it is not only the glass

Glazing raised this for glass. It applies to every specular surface on the vehicle and it is visible as a *shape*, not a level. `probe_scratch/rev50_crit_front_ws.png` (windscreen at 2.5×): the right pane carries a **pure-white rectangle with knife-sharp vertical and horizontal edges and zero falloff** — the specular image of a rectangular area light in a perfectly planar pane at roughness 0.022. The left pane carries the same as a hard-terminated bar. `rev50_crit_detf.png`: the headlamp lens is a flat dark disc with one hard white blob; the chrome bezel is a thin ring with a single specular and no environment in it at all.

Glazing's own already-right note — *"The pane's problem is what it can see, not what it is made of"* — is half wrong. A hard-edged rectangle is a joint failure of what it sees (five rectangles and a 0.05 void) **and** what it is (perfectly flat, roughness 0.022). Period glass is neither.

### 1.4 NOBODY OWNS "WHAT PHOTOGRAPH ARE WE TRYING TO BE?"

Computed straight from `studio.views()` and the vehicle's own bounds (X_nose +2.160, X_tail −2.151, y 0.875, z 1.20):

| view | lens | far/near depth ratio |
|---|---|---|
| `hero34f` (the declared delivery frame) | 78 mm @ 15.2 m | **1.279** |
| `hero34r` | 76 mm | 1.273 |
| `front34` | 76 mm | 1.275 |
| `low34` | 78 mm | 1.312 |
| `playa_ref` — **the only camera this project ever recovered from a photograph of this vehicle** | 36 mm @ 4.9 m | **2.323** |

The delivery frames render the bus with **1.8× less perspective divergence** than the one pose solved from a photograph of it. Every reference frame is a 3–6 m phone snapshot; every delivered frame is a 15 m product shot on a white sweep. Those are two different photographic genres, and every colour, level and "does it read" argument in 49 revisions has been fought across that unacknowledged gap. Lighting cleared it by comparing *elevation angles only* — see §2.1.

### 1.5 A WHOLE SUBSYSTEM IS OUTSIDE EVERY LOOP

`playa_env.py` — **1695 lines**, self-contained, every number traced to a measurement of `ref_rear34.jpg`, with the reference camera recovered and self-confirmed on a feature the solve never saw ("it predicts the bus's far top corner at image column 554.6 and the cream lid panel is read at 555"). Last touched at **rev 10** (`git log` shows one commit, `b540abd`). It appears in no verifier, no ledger since rev 45 except in passing, and in **none of the twelve dimensions**. No playa frame has ever been rendered into `out/`. `studio.py:1391` says of it: *"This is the frame Donald wants the owner to remember standing in."*

---

## 2. `already_right` CLAIMS THAT WERE ASSERTED, NOT CHECKED

**2.1 Lighting — "THE CONTACT SHADOW'S MAGNITUDE IS DELIVERED ON THE DIRECT PATH AND MATCHES ITS DECLARED TARGET… Do not re-tune T1_SHADOW."**
It was checked against **SPEC 10.116.5's own number (0.8729)**, not against a photograph. The target itself was set (`studio.py:783`) to *"0.871 — THE WEAKEST PHOTOGRAPHED READING"* out of four (0.3049 / 0.7300 / 0.6950 / 0.8713, mean 0.650). Calibrating to the member of the set that demands the smallest change is the same laundering the comment is arguing against. And §1.1 shows the under-body pool is 0.907–0.927 in every shipped frame. **What would test it:** the §1.1 band statistic, run against `bus_model_ref.JPG` (0.247) and against the four photographed readings as a *range*, not a floor.

**2.2 Lighting — "THE HERO CAMERA ELEVATIONS ARE RIGHT… The camera HEIGHT differs (3.1–3.8 m vs 1.90 m) only because the distance is 3× larger; the angle, which is what the eye reads, matches. Do not move the heroes down."**
Elevation is not what the eye reads on a vehicle; near-to-far scale convergence is, and it is set by distance/focal length. §1.4 gives 1.28 vs 2.32. **What would test it:** the far/near ratio above, or the two wheel diameters in `ref_rear34.jpg` (equal objects, so the ratio *is* the depth ratio).

**2.3 Materials — "THE TYRE/RUBBER SPECKLE IS MOSTLY MONTE-CARLO, NOT THE SHADER… Do not respec dust_film() on the strength of what the tyre looks like at 96 spp."**
**Refuted by its own arithmetic.** It reports per-image noise 3.5–4.2 DN against a 5-px HF residual of 8.6 DN. Variance share of noise = (4.2/8.6)² = **0.238** — i.e. 76 % of the HF residual is texture. My independent route agrees to two figures: the tyre annulus correlates at **0.771** between two wheels 2.4 m apart, so the noise variance share is **1 − 0.771 = 0.229**. Two routes, same answer, opposite conclusion to the one published.

**2.4 Materials — "The main shell's paint is CLEAN and should stay that way — nose cream 0.01 % dark-speck coverage in `out/r49s_front.png`."**
The statistic is a *dark-speck threshold count*; it is structurally blind to a low-contrast, large-cell blotch, which is what is actually visible. Compare `probe_scratch/rev50_crit_ren_roof.png` (render, tail cream) with `rev50_crit_r34_roof.png` (`ref_rear34.jpg`, the same panel on the target vehicle): the reference roof is smooth, glossy, warm and unblotched; the render's carries a heavy speckle field. **What would test it:** band-limited variance (15–150 mm on the vehicle) inside a window verified by eye to contain only cream — not a threshold count. I tried this and my own windows failed inspection; see §6.

**2.5 Roof — "THE ROOF APERTURE'S SURVIVING SIDE STRIPS MATCH THE OWNER'S SETTLEMENT."**
The build fails the owner's "roughly 0.3 m each side" in plan (0.162 / 0.182 m) and passes only after the metric is re-expressed as arc from the aperture edge to the drip rail (0.286 / 0.306 m). Arc is defensible, but the re-expression happened *after* the plan number failed, and the agent says so ("I raised this as a finding and then killed it"). **What would test it:** ask the owner which he meant, once, in the photo-request file — it is one sentence.

**2.6 Glazing — "THE GLASS BSDF's IOR, ROUGHNESS (0.022) AND BASE COLOUR ARE FINE… I could not confirm Blender 4.x even applies Specular IOR Level under Transmission Weight 1.0."**
"I could not confirm" is an unchecked claim wearing an already-right label, and §1.3 shows roughness 0.022 on a mathematically planar pane is *visible* as a hard-edged rectangle. **What would test it:** measure the 10–90 edge width of the softbox reflection in the pane against the same reflection in `ref_playa_34.png`'s windscreen.

**2.7 Wheels — "The wheels correctly do NOT rake, and they correctly touch z = 0. Measured on the render, the tyre's lowest row is 962–963 against the computed z = 0 row of 962.2."**
That compares the model to the model. It is a rendering-pipeline check, not a fidelity check, and it is presented in the same list as photographic corroborations. Same class: "TRACK_F and TRACK_R… the verifier checks them against copies of themselves" (which that agent *did* flag — see §4).

---

## 3. FINDINGS THAT CONTRADICT EACH OTHER

**C1 — Lighting vs Lighting.** Blocking: *"The stitched delivery frame has NO contact shadow — hero.py's T1_FX=0 gates out the whole of SPEC 10.116."* Already-right: *"THE CONTACT SHADOW'S MAGNITUDE IS DELIVERED ON THE DIRECT PATH AND MATCHES ITS DECLARED TARGET… Do not re-tune T1_SHADOW."* Both are about the shipped picture. They cannot both be acted on.

**C2 — Roof vs The Record, on the tail board's foot.** Roof: *"The tail board's foot is buried 63–75 mm inside the roof crown."* Record: *"THE TAIL BOARD'S HEIGHT CHAIN CLOSES, TWICE… photographed base 1.747 ± 0.027 m against the roof skin at the solved station 1.7497 = 2.7 mm… Do not re-open this."* 63–75 mm versus 2.7 mm on the same joint. One of these is measuring the board's *foot vertex* and the other the *chord's base station*; nobody says which, and rev 50 will either fix a non-defect or ignore a real one.

**C3 — Flank vs Materials, on the speckle.** Flank: *"THE GREY SPECKLE INSIDE THE RENDER'S LOUVRE SLOTS IS NOT A LOUVRE DEFECT… it is the flank's general paint-wear texture, present everywhere. Refuted and dropped."* Materials, blocking: *"the curvature edge-wear system degenerates into an all-over grey lichen speckle… 22.4 % coverage."* The same observation; "it's everywhere" was the *evidence* for one and the *dismissal* for the other.

**C4 — Wheels vs Fidelity bar, on the hubcap.** Wheels: *"CAP_R = 0.1345 itself is correct… Do not shrink or grow CAP_R"* and *"The five rim vent holes are correctly NOT scaled by S… scaling them was never the issue."* Fidelity bar: *"EVERY HUBCAP RENDERS AS A FIVE-PETAL FLOWER… the five vent holes are the only places the cap still shows."* Same defect, two incompatible fix directions (raise the cap dome vs lower the rim disc) and opposite verdicts on whether the vents are load-bearing.

**C5 — Glazing vs Lighting, on the environment.** Glazing: *"The glazing has nothing to reflect: the white backdrop is a compositor fill with zero scene presence and the world is at 0.05."* Lighting: *"THE 90 m FLAT CYCLORAMA… IS THE RIGHT CALL… Do not reintroduce a wall."* The only fix for the first is forbidden by the second.

**C6 — Nose, internally, on W4.** *"W4 HAS A PHOTOGRAPHED HANDLE AT LAST: the headlamp reads as a CIRCLE in every photograph and as the badge's own ELLIPSE in the render"* (blocking) sits next to *"BOTH HEADLAMPS AND BOTH BULLET INDICATORS ARE PLACED WITH ZERO ROTATION — their axes are exactly +X"* (major) and *"W4's FIX IS CAPPED BY THE SAME FRAME."* **The ellipse is confounded**: a lamp whose axis is +X on a flat nose and a lamp whose axis is +X on a crowned nose both render elliptical from a 3/4. Crowning the nose without splaying the lamp axes will not fix it; splaying the axes alone might. Two fixes, one piece of evidence, not separated.

**C7 — The Record vs the repository, on STATE.md.** *"STATE.md… was last written at rev 45."* The file on disk (mtime 08:53:39 today, after the survey was written at 08:49) carries `generated 2026-08-21 08:53:39 UTC / git commit e19b46f / rev 49e`. The finding was true when made and is now stale. Whoever writes rev 50 must not re-fix it.

---

## 4. THE SYSTEMIC PATTERN

**The acceptance tests are anchored to the project's own constants, not to a photograph — and "0 fail, 0 warn / 113 PASS" is being read as evidence about the bus.**

Instances, from four different agents plus my own reading:
* the contact shadow verified against SPEC 10.116.5's own 0.8729 (§2.1);
* eight `verify_clone.sh` rows reading their acceptance figures out of `STATE.md`, which `audit.py` writes from the mesh;
* four "declares itself" rows that grep a constant's own following comment for a marker string;
* `TRACK_F`/`TRACK_R` "checked against copies of themselves";
* the tyre's z = 0 row checked against the computed z = 0 row;
* `verify.py:766` honestly recording that row 1's length "cannot carry the measurement because X_NOSE has never been measured" — while the headline "length 4.065 vs spec 4.055" is quoted everywhere without it;
* the tail board, the newest and least-measured object, with **zero rows in either verifier**.

Two corollaries that explain most of the individual findings:

**(a) When a metric fails, the window or the metric moves.** Roof strips re-expressed as arc (§2.5). W6's P1 measured on the flank (3.49 σ) with the same-frame tail control (1.46 σ) left unused. The shadow target set to the weakest of four readings. The burst spike pitch accepted because the build "sits BETWEEN the two red frames" — a two-sided bracket of width 46 % passes almost anything.

**(b) A finding is attributed to the last thing that was looked at, without a control that separates causes.** The lamp ellipse (flat nose vs unrotated lamp axis, C6). The tyre speckle (noise vs texture, §2.3 — where the agent's own numbers separate them and were read backwards). "The speckle is everywhere therefore it is not a defect" (C3).

**The process fix is one rule, and it is cheap:** every row in `verify.py` / `verify_clone.sh` must name the **reference frame and the pixel window** its acceptance number came from, or be tagged `SELF-CONSISTENCY, NOT FIDELITY`. Rows that cannot name a frame are not failures — they are honest — but they must stop counting toward "113 PASS". Today the two kinds are indistinguishable in the same list, which is why twelve agents could each report subsystem-level defects while the verifier reported zero.

---

## 5. THE SINGLE HIGHEST-VALUE THING, NOT ON THE LIST

**Render `playa_ref` — the one camera recovered from a photograph of this vehicle — into `playa_env`, and compare it to `ref_rear34.jpg` pixel-registered.**

Everything needed already exists and has been dormant since rev 10: the camera (`studio.py:1391`, 36 mm at (−4.829, 2.222, 1.900), self-confirmed on an unseen feature), the environment (`playa_env.py`, 1695 lines, every mass placed by inverting that camera so each plant is at its measured depth), the foreground culling (`_cull_foreground`), and the rig characterised *by measurement* as "one large low lateral area source, dark absorbing surround; no sun, no sky, no dapple, no fog." One `hero.py playa_ref --scene playa` produces it.

Why this one frame is worth more than any item on the list:

1. **It is the only fidelity test this project can perform.** Every other comparison puts a 78 mm/15 m white-sweep render beside a 36 mm/5 m street snapshot and argues about the difference (§1.4). This one puts the render and the photograph on the *same camera, same subject pose, same background* — subtract them and what is left is the model.
2. **It is the W6 experiment rev 49 concluded is the only one that works.** Rev 49's own finding: the cyclorama albedo is dead (2–5 %), a bigger softbox is dead (0.7 %), and *"the only thing that works replaces the directional rig with a diffuse dome."* `playa`'s rig is exactly that, and it is already tuned against measurement (`FOLIAGE_GAIN`, "foliage median linear luminance must land at 0.104–0.113 × the cream"). And W6's cause — the achromatic veil from two big white sources — is absent by construction because there are no big white sources.
3. **It closes `optics-6` (open since rev 12, §1.1) for free.** In `playa` the ground is a real lit surface, not a shadow catcher matted over a compositor fill, so the under-body occlusion and the contact darkening come out of the physics instead of an alpha power curve. No `T1_SHADOW` gain, no `T1_SHADOW_FLOOR`, no `T1_FX` gate.
4. **It gives the glass, the chrome and the clear coat something to reflect** (§1.3), which is the only fix for the hard-edged rectangles and it is forbidden in the studio scene by C5.
5. **It is the frame the owner is being asked to recognise.** `studio.py:1391` says so. Nine distinct frames of his bus exist and every one of them is the bus *in a place*, in service, with a woman at a table and palms behind it (`probe_scratch/rev50_crit_rear34.png`). Forty revisions of measurement have been spent making a model that is only ever shown floating on white paper.

**Ceiling on this recommendation.** It costs one long render and it may fail: `playa_env` has not been built since rev 10, so the API may have drifted, the vegetation may need re-gaining against the current AgX/Punchy chain, and `_cull_foreground` is untested at rev 49's geometry. That is the point — it is a live, unrun subsystem inside a project whose standard is "any single measurement off is unacceptable," and nobody in twelve dimensions looked at it. If it builds and the subtraction against `ref_rear34.jpg` is clean, it replaces most of the argument in this repository with a residual image.

**Runner-up, if only one small thing can be done:** give every duplicated part a per-instance rotation and a per-instance object-space texture offset (§1.2). Two lines, `MOTTLE_OFS` already exists as the no-op control, and it removes the one defect in these renders that a viewer clocks instantly without knowing why.

---

## 6. WHAT I DROPPED (refuted before reporting)

* **"The render is over-sharp; its MTF does not match the photograph's."** Measured the 10–90 rise across the rim/tyre boundary, 240 radial rays, sub-pixel, at matched physical scale: photograph `ref_side.jpg` 3.06 px = **14.4 mm** on the vehicle; render side-ortho 3.23 px = **11.9 mm** (rear) and 2.13 px = 7.9 mm (front). Ratio 1.2–1.8 with p25–p75 spanning 1.85–10 px. The instrument does not discriminate — the edge is not a step (the tyre shoulder curves away) and `ref_side.jpg`'s blur includes its own downsample. **No finding.**
* **"The cream mottle is far stronger than the photograph's."** My band-limited variance instrument returned 11.5–21.1 % for the photographs, which is impossible for clean paint. I marked the windows and looked (`probe_scratch/rev50_crit_win_PH_ws.png`): every one straddled a seam, a window frame, a background wall or a fire extinguisher. The number is dead. The *qualitative* difference between `rev50_crit_ren_roof.png` and `rev50_crit_r34_roof.png` is real and I report it as a look, not a figure — and `MOTTLE_M`/`MOTTLE_AMP` are a declared, ablatable, SPEC-10.38/10.49-grounded map that I have no admissible measurement to reopen.
* **"The side ortho shows zero under-vehicle shadow (255.00 at every row)."** True but void: `studio.views()["side"]` is orthographic at z 1.52 looking horizontally, so the ground plane is edge-on and the far wheels project exactly onto the near ones. There is genuinely nothing to see. §1.1 uses the perspective delivery frames instead.
* **"The wheels' dirt is identical, therefore the wear system is broken."** Over-claimed on first pass: the 0.90 whole-wheel correlation is driven substantially by *geometry* (vent holes, rim band, emblem) rendering identically at identical phase. I re-ran on the **tyre annulus only** (62 < r < 86 px), which carries no azimuthally-varying geometry — 0.771, matching Materials' own noise fraction. The clone claim stands; the "it's all texture" claim is scoped to the annulus.
* **The record agent's STATE.md finding** — not mine to report, but it is now stale on disk (C7) and rev 50 should not spend a line on it.

# REV 50 — RANKED WORK LIST

*Synthesis of twelve subsystem surveys (78 findings), two adversarial verdicts, and a completeness critique. No Blender was run. Everything below was read with `cat`/`sed`/`grep` or measured with bare python3 (PIL/numpy/scipy) on files already on disk. Rows marked **†** I re-measured or re-read from source myself this pass; crops written to `probe_scratch/rev50_syn_*.png` and read as images.*

---

## 1. THE HEADLINE

**The geometry is now closer than the presentation.** The residual shape errors are mostly tens of millimetres on parts nobody looks at twice — but four things that have nothing to do with measurement are what make every shipped frame read as a render: the vehicle barely darkens the ground it stands on (`optics-6`, open since rev 12, and there is no undercarriage to cast into it); its largest surface is a paint with Coat Weight 0.02 and Coat Roughness 0.300 that delivers chalk where every photograph of both buses shows polished enamel; every duplicated part — four hubcaps, two headlamps, two indicators, two tail lamps — is a bit-identical clone at the same clock angle carrying the same dirt (I measure front-to-rear wheel high-pass correlation **0.68–0.71** against a +5 px control of **−0.01**); and every specular surface on it has a white void and six rectangles to reflect.

**Two shape defects are big enough to see across the room and both are one constant each**: `LID_OPEN_DEG = 104.0` tips the mural lid 26–43° *away* from the counter, opposite to what its own comment and SPEC §135 say it does; and the hubcap dome is buried in the rim disc from r = 0.120 m outward, so all four wheels render a five-petal flower (m=5 harmonic 0.050–0.056 in the render against 0.012–0.022 in `ref_side.jpg`, synthetic-circle control 0.0000).

**W4 and W6 are not going to close this revision and the survey should stop implying otherwise.** The adversary's homography test settles that *no* camera at *any* focal length can put the workshop frame's roundel (axis ratio 0.657) and headlamp aperture (0.92) on one plane — flat-plane best fit 13.24 px rms against a 0.22 px control on the render — so W4 is real, quantified and confirmed; but apportioning it between panel crown, lamp splay and a proud aperture flange still needs one grazing frame nobody holds, and W6's only surviving lever (a diffuse dome) is already built, tuned and un-rendered in `playa_env.py`. Render `playa_ref` and both stop being arguments.

---

## 2. WHAT IS ALREADY RIGHT — DO NOT RE-LITIGATE

**Owner rulings, closed** (restated only so nobody reopens them): the RED bus is the target and geometry — not paint — transfers between vehicles; the marks above the Calidad burst are STARS; the flower mural with yellow menu strips on the raised lid is correct as built; "La Santa" is a detached ground sign, retired; the bulb-lined board *at the tail* is on the vehicle and was built at rev 49; the over-rider bar and posts were withdrawn at rev 37; the rear bumper was removed at the conversion and must not return; **the engine lid is SHUT and only the rear window is open** (rev 49).

**Locked constants, verified this pass or last:** `bulge = 0.019` is the only forward-bulge term and `V_POW_Z = 0.60` is a paint curve — do not touch it. `ROUNDEL_Z_AG = 1.0170` (three independent chains inside one sigma). `ROUNDEL_D = 0.2800` (the survey's own instrument reproduces `build.py:576`'s published D_roundel/D_aperture 1.384 at 1.35). `IND_DZ = 0.206` and `IND_DY = 0.130` (a "3.8 σ low" reading was retracted as a calibration error). `CAP_R = 0.1345` is correct and must not be shrunk — the flower is the rim disc's fault, not the cap's. `ARCH_W_REAR = 0.920` at `X_AXLE_R = −1.100` (10/11 mm, inside declared ±0.03). Rear arch-to-tyre gap 41.0 mm. Front bumper blade height (107 mm measured vs 113 mm built). The bullet indicator is the right *type*. The V swage is a real pressed feature and the paint break registers to it.

**Verified geometry that has been doubted and should not be again:** † **the rocker IS modelled** — `t1_core.section()` run B is literally `# B bottom roll`, a quadratic Bézier of radius `RB_ALL(x)` = **0.122 m** over the whole main run (−1.900…+1.500), tapering to 0.085 at both ends, over run A's crowned bottom pan; and the project names it outside comments in `folk_gen.py:1337 rocker_band()`, `audit.py:108/175/408`. The louvre slot **count** is right (11 dark bands at uniform pitch in the render; 11 at uniform 8.15 px in IMG_2073). The block's vertical **centre** is right to 0.05 mm. The block outline is rectangular, not staggered. The belt line on the cab door is right to 1.1 mm. The cream/red split along the serving bay is the *counter fascia*, not the body belt, and it is correctly level. The louvres self-shadow with the correct **sign** after rev 48. The wheels correctly do not rake.

**Frame hygiene:** ten distinct vehicle frames, not fifteen — five byte-identical duplicate pairs, plus `ref_source.jpeg` = `ref_playa_34.png` found this cycle, making it **nine**. Never count a duplicate as corroboration.

---

## 3. THE WORK LIST, RANKED

**Ranking formula, stated as required:** `score = (visible impact on photorealism, 1–5) × (confidence it is real, 0–1) ÷ (effort: small = 1, medium = 2, large = 4)`. Impact is judged on the frames the owner actually looks at (`r49s_hero34f`, `r49shut2_hero34r`, `r49s_counter`), not on a 20× crop. Where a finding was contested, confidence is the **post-adversarial** value, not the surveyor's.

Currently all three are candidates and only (a) and (b) are absent from the build — the bezel *is* 13.4 mm proud | A head-on frame does **not** do it: head-on, a 15° panel splay compresses the bezel by 3.4 %, which is 1 px on any nose we hold. ### C. BLOCKED ON AN OWNER RULING — as multiple choice, because he asked to be asked that way

**C1 — THE DELIVERY GENRE. This is the highest-value question on the page.** Every reference frame is a 3–6 m phone snapshot; every delivered frame is a 78 mm lens at 15 m on a white sweep. Far/near depth ratio: `hero34f` **1.279**; the only camera this project ever recovered from a photograph of this bus, `playa_ref`, **2.323**. Forty-nine revisions of colour and level arguments have been fought across that unacknowledged gap.
- **(a)** Keep the white-studio product look as the deliverable; the street photographs are dimensional references only, and colour arguments across the gap are formally closed.
- **(b)** Make `playa_ref` the deliverable — 36 mm at 4.9 m, the environment already built in `playa_env.py` (1695 lines, every mass placed by inverting that camera, dormant since rev 10, referenced by **no** verifier and **no** dimension).
- **(c)** Deliver both; judge fidelity only on `playa_ref` and presentation only on the studio.
- **(d)** Keep the studio but drop the heroes to ~7 m / 50 mm so the perspective divergence sits between the two.

*Whichever he picks, **render `playa_ref` once, this revision**. It is the only frame this project can compare pixel-registered against a photograph of its own subject; it is exactly the diffuse-dome rig rev 49 concluded is the **only** surviving W6 lever (its rig is characterised by measurement as "one large low lateral area source, dark absorbing surround; no sun, no sky"); and its ground is a real lit surface, so it closes `optics-6` and item A4 for free, with no `T1_SHADOW` gain, no floor, no `T1_FX` gate. One command: `hero.py playa_ref --scene playa`.*

**C2 — THE WIPERS.** `build.py:405` puts two 300 mm blades and two arms across the screen, 24 mm proud, in light chrome — among the most conspicuous objects on the face in three shipped frames. Their only warrant is SPEC §4's inventory line under the heading *"Stock 1963 T1"*, i.e. inferred, not measured. Three in-service frames of **this** bus show a bare spindle. Same evidence class as the over-rider bar he withdrew at rev 37 and the rear bumper SPEC 2.4 removed.
- **(a)** Remove arms and blades, keep the spindles (what the photographs show).
- **(b)** Keep as built.
- **(c)** Remove entirely, spindles included.

**C3 — THE ROOF APERTURE'S SIDE STRIPS.** His settlement was *"roughly 0.3 m each side."* The build gives **0.162 / 0.182 m in plan** and **0.286 / 0.306 m as arc** from aperture edge to drip rail. It passes only on the second reading, and the re-expression happened *after* the first failed.
- **(a)** He meant arc along the roof skin — build is correct, close it.
- **(b)** He meant plan width looking down — the aperture is ~120 mm too wide each side.
- **(c)** He meant "about a foot, don't measure it" — retire the number from the verifier.

**C4 — WHAT THE GLASS AND CHROME SEE.** Ten panes, both mirrors, every bezel and the bumper reflect a compositor fill with **zero scene presence** at world 0.05. In `r49s_front.png` the right windscreen pane carries a pure-white rectangle with knife-sharp edges and no falloff — the specular image of a rectangular area light in a mathematically planar pane. But he ruled the 90 m flat cyclorama correct and said do not reintroduce a wall.
- **(a)** Add a reflection-only environment (invisible to camera, visible to specular rays) — no wall in frame, glass gets content.
- **(b)** Leave it; the white studio genuinely has nothing to reflect.
- **(c)** Solve it by choosing **C1(b)** instead — `playa` has a real environment and the question dissolves.

**C5 — ARE THE SERVING BAYS GLAZED?** Already ruled *no*. But `ref_rear34.jpg` is a second target frame that SPEC 10.29's original argument never used, and it bears on the question. Asked once, then closed for good.
- **(a)** Ruling stands, unglazed. **(b)** Re-open on the new frame. **(c)** Glazed on some bays only.

### D. DECLARED AND HONEST — pose choices and presentation devices, correctly labelled, NOT defects

- **The engine lid shut with only the rear window open** (rev 49 ruling). Do not propose reopening the lower bay.
- **`T1_SHADOW = 9.0` / `T1_SHADOW_FLOOR = 0.030`** — a presentation device, declared and ablatable (`T1_SHADOW=1.0` restores the floating arm exactly), per SPEC 10.105's own template. The *rim discontinuity* (A22) is the defect; the device is not.
- **The white cyclorama at 90 m flat**, and the world cut to 0.05 to fight W6 — both declared, both with their reasoning in the file. The world value is a *cause* of A4, not a mistake.
- **`T1_SOFTEN=3.5` (`r49soft35_side.png`) and the darker cyclorama (`r49cyc30_side.png`)** — experiments, explicitly not shipped.
- **`bulge = 0.019`, `V_POW_Z = 0.60`, `FLOOR_W = 1.200`, `LOUV_BAY_D`** — all four carry the word AUTHORED or a lock. `FLOOR_W` and the bay depth are honest un-measured numbers, not laundered ones.
- **The un-measured rows in `verify.py`** — e.g. `verify.py:766` recording plainly that row 1's length "cannot carry the measurement because `X_NOSE` has never been measured." Honest. The defect is that it still counts toward "113 PASS" (see Rule 29).
- **`LID_OPEN_DEG` is NOT in this category.** A pose choice is a defect the moment the source comment and SPEC both describe the opposite of what is built.

## 4. WHAT WAS REFUTED — do not chase these

| Claim | Verdict | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **"THE ROCKER IS NOT MODELLED AT ALL"** (survey finding #1, BLOCKING) | **REFUTED** † | † I confirmed the adversary from source: `t1_core.section()` run B is `# B bottom roll`, `qbez((Yb,Zb),(Ybs,Zb),(Ybs,zb0))`, radius `RB_ALL(x)` = **0.122 m** across the main run, over run A's crowned bottom pan. `grep rocker` missed it because the project names it in `folk_gen.py:1337`, `audit.py:108/175/408`, not in the four files searched. Three further breaks: a **side ortho cannot show a turn-under** (its projection onto XZ *is* a straight silhouette at min z — "straight silhouette therefore flat cut" is a non-sequitur; the low 3/4 shows the roll); the "bright neutral trim strip" is an achromatic clearcoat specular on the rolled lip (G and B offsets over local diffuse **+89 / +97**, equal within noise — the additive signature of a highlight, and this project's own W6 headline is that the veil *is* achromatic); and the surveyor's own green-bus crop shows **no strip at all**, only the rocker the model has. `RAKE_DZDX` is not measured off a missing feature — `t1_core.py:143-145` re-anchors it against the model's own rocker |
| **"W4 HAS A PHOTOGRAPHED HANDLE AT LAST"** (finding #2, BLOCKING) | **PARTIALLY STANDS** — observable kept, framing struck | The **observable stands and is now stronger**: a homography search over all poses and all focal lengths gives best flat-plane rms **13.24 px** on the photograph against **0.22 px** on the render, so no camera can do it. But: it is **not new** (REF_MEASUREMENTS.md:490 already records this aperture as "→ circular" and :466 already concluded "a fitted projection model did not close… evidence that the panel is not planar"); "three independent photographs" is **one** (the two Nolita frames die to the proud-bezel argument, as the finding itself concedes); the quoted "74×70 = 1.06" is a **bounding box of a 30°-tilted ellipse** — the correct pair is 0.657 vs 0.93 = 1.42×, not 1.61×; the "3.5–7× outside the window" margin is overstated (two independent lines put f at 560–660 px, giving ~2×); "all three causes absent from the build" is false — the bezel stands **13.4 mm proud**; and "blocked on: NOTHING" contradicts the finding's own refusal to invert it. **Severity is inherited from W4, not established here** |
| Inverting the workshop aperture pair into a value for `bulge` | **REFUTED, by its own author** | Gives a 34–39° normal swing, ~1.1 m plan radius, 160 mm sagitta — a Beetle nose, not a Bus nose. The inversion is contaminated. Report the observable; never write it into `bulge` |
| "The render shows 11 louvre bands where the photograph shows 10" | **REFUTED** | IMG_2073 (GREEN, geometry) gives **eleven** dark minima at uniform 8.15 px pitch at two column windows. Count is right |
| "The louvre block's forward edge is raked / staggered" | **REFUTED** | Read twice off crops; it was the flower mural. Numerically the slot rows start at cols 1140–1149 with no systematic taper |
| "The grey speckle inside the louvre slots is a louvre defect" | **REFUTED** | G/R > 0.50 fraction 0.351 in the block against 0.283 / 0.252 / 0.314 on three plain flank panels. It is the flank's general wear field — *which is itself item A6*. "It is everywhere" is the evidence for A6, not a dismissal of it |
| "The belt line sits ~157 mm too high above the louvre block" | **REFUTED** | Read off IMG_2073's white/green break, which is **artwork** on the wrong vehicle under rule 26, and whose identity (belt vs counter fascia) cannot be settled in that frame |
| "The counter fascia is level in the photograph and raked in the render — 34 mm at the tail" | **REFUTED by its own control** | The same instrument makes `ref_side.jpg`'s drip rail **fall** 42 mm going aft where a body line must **rise** 47 mm — an 89 mm differential error, larger than the claimed effect |
| "`IND_DZ` is 3.8 σ low (0.167 m)" | **RETRACTED** | Scaled by a calibration that read `build.py:572`'s "0.149 m below the **belt**" as "above the lamp". Re-anchored: 0.197 m, 0.9 σ |
| "The V swage is too shallow" | **REFUTED by its third arm** | Street-lit `ref_nolita_front34` gives +104 % at d=1–2 px; the render 4.0 %; but `ref_workshop.jpg`'s flat overhead fluorescent — the only comparable lighting regime — gives **2.5 %**. Render 4.0 vs workshop 2.5 is agreement. The +104 % is rule 7(d) |

Two routes, same answer, opposite to the published conclusion. It rolls into A3 and A6 |

Calibrating to the member of a set that demands the smallest change is the laundering the comment argues against. The magnitude question is **open**, and A4 is where it lives |

---

## 5. THE SYSTEMIC FINDING — proposed **RULE 29**

The critic identified one class that generalises across four agents and most of the individual defects: **the acceptance tests are anchored to the project's own constants, and "0 fail, 0 warn / 113 PASS" is being read as evidence about the bus.** Instances: the contact shadow verified against SPEC 10.116.5's own 0.8729 (which was itself set to the weakest of four photographed readings); eight `verify_clone.sh` rows reading their acceptance figures out of `STATE.md`, which `audit.py` writes from the mesh; four rows that grep a constant's own following comment for a marker string; `TRACK_F`/`TRACK_R` checked against copies of themselves; the tyre's z = 0 row checked against the computed z = 0 row; the newest object on the vehicle carrying zero rows in either verifier.

> **RULE 29 — A TEST THAT CANNOT NAME A FRAME AND A PIXEL WINDOW IS NOT A FIDELITY TEST, AND MUST NOT BE COUNTED AS ONE.**
>
> Every row in `verify.py` and `verify_clone.sh` names, inline, the reference frame and the pixel window its acceptance figure came off — or it is tagged `SELF-CONSISTENCY, NOT FIDELITY` and reported in a separate count. Self-consistency rows are honest and worth keeping; they are not evidence about the vehicle, and a headline that adds them to fidelity rows is a false headline.
>
> Three corollaries, each of which would have caught a defect on this page:
>
> **29.1 — A TARGET SET FROM A SPREAD MUST STATE THE SPREAD, AND A TEST AGAINST THAT TARGET IS A TEST AGAINST THE SPREAD.** Where a constant was calibrated to one member of a set of photographed readings, the verifier compares to the **range**, never to the chosen member. *(Would have caught: the contact-shadow already-right, verified against a target chosen because it demanded the least change.)*
>
> **29.2 — WHEN A METRIC FAILS, THE METRIC IS THE FINDING. NEITHER THE WINDOW NOR THE STATISTIC MAY MOVE AFTERWARD.** A re-expression that first appears after the original expression failed is recorded as a re-expression, with both values, and goes to the owner rather than into the file. *(Would have caught: the roof strips re-expressed from plan to arc; W6's P1 measured on the flank at 3.49 σ with the same-frame tail control at 1.46 σ left unused; the burst spike pitch accepted on a two-sided bracket 46 % wide.)*
>
> **29.3 — NO FINDING IS ATTRIBUTED TO A CAUSE UNTIL A CONTROL SEPARATES IT FROM THE OTHER CANDIDATES.** A symptom consistent with three mechanisms is reported as a symptom with its three candidates named, not as the last mechanism the author happened to be reading. *(Would have caught: the lamp ellipse attributed to nose flatness when unrotated lamp axes and a proud flange produce the same image; "the tyre speckle is mostly Monte-Carlo" when the author's own two numbers separate noise from texture at 24/76 and were read backwards; "the speckle is everywhere therefore it is not a defect" when everywhere-ness was the evidence for the very defect being dismissed in the next dimension.)*

And a companion the critic earned separately: **an estimator is not admissible until it has recovered a known answer on a render.** Three louvre instruments were built this cycle; the one that looked most authoritative returned a plausible 9.44 px pitch on the block and a comparable 6.80 px on blank paint, and the end-finder under-read the render's known aft end by 122 mm. Reporting "I cannot measure it" is the correct output; reporting a number that was never controlled is what rev 47's LINE_GAP did.

---

## 6. HONEST CEILING — what this survey could NOT determine

1. **No number here was taken off the mesh.** Blender was not run (correctly — a render queue holds all four cores). Every model-side figure is read from source constants or from the shipped PNGs. Two constants that several findings lean on — the nose skin's x at the indicator station, and the exact panel normal at the lamp — are inferred from `probe_rev45_nose`'s *published* raycast, not measured afresh. A6, A11 and A21 would each be sharper with one `bpy` probe.

2. **W4 cannot be apportioned, and this document deliberately does not try.** The observable is now proven camera-independent (13.24 px vs a 0.22 px control), but panel plan-crown, lamp-axis splay and a proud rolled aperture flange all produce it, and only B3 separates them. The one thing that *is* settled is the **cap**: a three-point collinearity test on `ref_workshop.jpg` puts the crown at |crown| < ~80 mm at 2 σ over the 0.545 m station span, against 8.8–11.3 mm built — so the natural over-reaction ("multiply `bulge` by 5–10") is excluded by the photograph. That bound's *point estimate* is worthless (its sign says the nose is concave, which is impossible — the far bore is clipped by the vehicle's own silhouette); only the bound survives.

3. **Every metric number in the nose dimension scales with `ROUNDEL_D = 0.280 m`, which was itself derived from `ref_workshop.jpg`.** If the roundel is not 280 mm they all move together. The *aspect ratios* and the *source facts* do not.

4. **`k_t` = 215.5 px/m, carried off the rear hub through the SPEC 10.35 flank map, is known to be wrong somewhere.** `flank_compare.py:218-232` records the two calibrated instruments disagreeing by 2.3 % **with the sign wrong**, and the error is not a pure scale: applied to the drip-rail fit it makes a body line fall 42 mm where it must rise 47 mm. **Single-station** quantities are largely immune; anything quoted as a **slope or an absolute height along the flank** is not. That control already killed one written-up finding.

5. **The louvre block's length and station are not measurable from the frames we hold**, and this is a positive result, not a gap in effort: three estimators, all failing their own controls, one on the render where the answer is exact. Any future "re-measurement" of block geometry off `ref_side.jpg` must first be shown to recover the render's known ends.

6. **The under-body / cavity finding is qualitatively certain and quantitatively window-dependent.** † My own generic detector returned 255.0 on the fidelity bar — the opposite of the critic's number — purely from window placement. A4 is therefore anchored on the crop pair and a stated-window number, and it needs one agreed statistic before anyone tunes against it.

7. **The gloss half of A5 has no number.** † My `(p99−p50)/p50` windows contained glazing and background and were discarded rather than published. The source facts (Coat Weight 0.02; Coat Roughness 0.300, ten times out of family; `t1_mats.py:178` declaring the body diffuse-dominated) and the crop pair are what carry it. A proper measure needs a cream window verified by eye in both a render and a *daylight* frame of the target — `ref_side.jpg`, not the night-flash `ref_rear34.jpg`.

8. **W6 was not advanced this pass and cannot be from the studio rig.** Two of its supporting statistics are themselves contested (the headline G/R compares two display-referred ratios at different display levels through AgX-Punchy, where 15–34 % of the published 3.5 σ is a units artefact; P1's same-frame tail control reads 1.46 σ against the flank's 3.49 σ and is unused). Rev 49 already measured that the only live lever is a diffuse dome. **C1 is the resolution, not another measurement.**

9. **Fourteen items on this list — including the four highest-ranked — were never owned by any of the twelve dimensions.** Ground/undercarriage, per-instance variation, what specular surfaces see, the delivery genre, and the whole of `playa_env.py` (1695 lines, one commit, rev 10, in no verifier and no dimension) fell between the survey's own partitions. A twelve-way subject split will keep producing that gap; the next survey should carry a thirteenth pass whose only brief is *what does no dimension own*.
